# Phase 4.6 closing mega-audit — W5-86 → W5-92 arc

**Date:** 2026-05-14 (W5-93)
**Branch:** `feat/quantbook-engine`
**HEAD at audit time:** `cce5406b2c7` (W5-92 Phase 4.6.D — sheet-scoped names)
**Pattern:** W5-52 / W5-67 / W5-76 / W5-84 — Codex + Sonnet in parallel; HIGH findings → closure commit.

## What this audit covers

The 8-commit Phase 4.6 arc that landed Excel-canon cross-sheet refs +
sheet rename + sheet-scoped names end-to-end:

| Commit | Subject |
|---|---|
| `09b35352e61` W5-85 | Phase 4.6 design doc + Codex review (doc-only) |
| `0177fc6c269` W5-86 | Phase 4.6.AA sheet registry + canonicalizer |
| `77b932691b7` W5-87 | Phase 4.6.A part 1 — `SheetRef` AST migration |
| `85e2864fe93` W5-88 | Phase 4.6.A part 2 — lexer Bang/SheetName/QuotedSheetName tokens |
| `5b26c3662d3` W5-89 | Phase 4.6.A part 3 — parser + printer cross-sheet round-trip |
| `1ec3c4d4724` W5-90 | Phase 4.6.B — binder + runtime cross-sheet resolution |
| `083ad235d24` W5-91 | Phase 4.6.C — sheet rename + formula-text rewrite + `Op::RenameSheet` |
| `cce5406b2c7` W5-92 | Phase 4.6.D — sheet-scoped names + schema v5 |

The design contract for this arc is `docs/architecture/2026-05-13-cross-sheet-references.md`. Treat that doc as the spec; the code as the artifact under audit.

## Files in scope

- `crates/ql-storage/src/workbook.rs` — `sheet_id_by_name`, `canonical_sheet_name`, `validate_sheet_name`, `rename_sheet`, `SheetNameError`, `add_sheet` collision checks.
- `crates/ql-storage/src/sheet.rs` — `Sheet::set_name`, `Sheet::scoped_names`, `Sheet::scoped_names_mut`, `Sheet::set_scoped_name`, `Sheet::clear_scoped_name`.
- `crates/ql-formula-syntax/src/ast.rs` — `SheetRef::{Current, Name, Id}` enum, `SheetRef::resolve_or` panic semantics, `SheetRef::from_option`, `SheetRef::id`, `rewrite_sheet_name_in_expr` + `rewrite_sheet_ref` + `rewrite_range_ref` walkers.
- `crates/ql-formula-syntax/src/token.rs` — `Bang`, `SheetName`, `QuotedSheetName` token variants.
- `crates/ql-formula-syntax/src/lexer.rs` — one-token lookahead via `Chars::clone()` for `IdentOrSheet`, quoted-sheet-name parsing with `''` escape, `LexError::UnterminatedQuotedSheetName`.
- `crates/ql-formula-syntax/src/parser.rs` — sheet-prefix consumption, `apply_sheet_to_term` + `number_to_bare_row`, cross-sheet range constraints (single-prefix normalization, mixed-endpoint rejection), `ParseError::DanglingBang` / `DoubleSheetQualifier`.
- `crates/ql-formula-syntax/src/printer.rs` — sheet-prefix emission for `Name` / `Id`; unicode/whitespace/colon escape in quoted form.
- `crates/ql-exec/src/plan.rs` — `SheetResolver` trait, `EmptySheetResolver`, `resolve_sheet_ref`, `bind_with_names_and_sheets`, `BindError::UnknownSheet`, `NameLookup` trait extension to take `owning_sheet`.
- `crates/ql-exec/src/env.rs` — `SheetResolver for Workbook` impl, `NameLookup for Workbook` two-tier chain impl, `NameLookup for NameTable` legacy single-table impl.
- `crates/ql-exec/src/workbook_runtime.rs` — `WorkbookRuntime::rename_sheet` + `set_sheet_scoped_name`, `RuntimeError::Bind(UnknownSheet)` + `RuntimeError::SheetName` propagation, `PlanCache::clear()` on rename, `rewrite_formula_text_for_sheet_rename` helper.
- `crates/ql-exec/src/transaction.rs`, `calcgraph_session.rs` — switched from `wb.names()` to `wb` for the names argument (sheet-scoped chain).
- `crates/ql-oplog/src/op.rs` — `Op::RenameSheet`, `Op::SetName { scope, .. }` extension.
- `crates/ql-oplog/src/replay.rs` — rename-sheet replay 3-case reconciliation, scoped-SetName routing, `ReplayError::SheetRenameNameMismatch` + `SheetRenameRejected`.
- `crates/ql-oplog/tests/producer_replay_equivalence.rs` — producer-side parity for rename + scoped names.
- `crates/ql-io/src/qbook_format.rs` — `WORKBOOK_SCHEMA_VERSION 4 → 5`, `NamedEntry.scope: Option<u16>` with serde-default + skip_serializing_if, save-side scope collection + sort, load-side route-by-scope + `MalformedName` for unknown sheet id, `future_schema_version_rejected` bumped to v6.

## Concerns by area

### A. Sheet registry + canonicalizer (W5-86)

**A.1** `Workbook::canonical_sheet_name`: ASCII-uppercase only? Confirm it matches the documented contract in the design doc § 10.0. Verify it agrees with `NameTable`'s canonicalization (also ASCII-uppercase).

**A.2** `Workbook::sheet_id_by_name`: case-insensitive lookup. Verify it handles non-ASCII (e.g. `Café` vs `CAFÉ`) consistently with the canonicalizer (i.e. they should NOT match, since canonicalization is ASCII-only — but this should be documented).

**A.3** `Workbook::validate_sheet_name`: rejects empty, reserved chars `: \ / ? * [ ]`, duplicates under canonical comparison. Verify the order: empty → reserved-char → duplicate. Does the reserved-char check fire for chars ANYWHERE in the name (not just first/last)?

**A.4** `Workbook::add_sheet` collision check: does it now use the canonical comparison? Pre-W5-86 the check was exact-name. Verify `add_sheet("Sheet1")` + `add_sheet("SHEET1")` rejects the second.

### B. SheetRef AST migration (W5-87)

**B.1** `SheetRef::resolve_or(owning_sheet)`: panics on `Name(_)` per design doc § 5.2. Verify the panic message is helpful + the panic is reachable ONLY from post-bind layers (binder/runtime), never from parser/printer.

**B.2** `SheetRef::from_option` + `SheetRef::id`: backwards-compat helpers. Verify their semantics match the historical `Option<SheetId>` shape.

**B.3** Hash/Eq for `SheetRef`: `Current` / `Name("X")` / `Id(0)` all distinct? Verify `Name("Sheet1")` != `Name("SHEET1")` (the AST does NOT canonicalize; the binder canonicalizes at resolve time).

**B.4** All call sites that previously used `Option<SheetId>` migrated cleanly: search for any remaining `Option<SheetId>` in AST/binder/calcgraph code that should be `SheetRef`.

### C. Lexer (W5-88)

**C.1** `Bang` token: emitted ONLY after a `SheetName` / `QuotedSheetName` / `Ident` that could be a sheet prefix? Or always, with the parser disambiguating? Verify what happens with `=A1!B2` (Excel rejects with `#NAME?`).

**C.2** `QuotedSheetName`: `''` escape handled. `'foo''bar'` → `SheetName("foo'bar")`. Verify `'foo` (unterminated) surfaces `LexError::UnterminatedQuotedSheetName` with correct byte position.

**C.3** `SheetName` lookahead: `Chars::clone()` for one-token peek. Verify the lookahead is consumed only when `!` follows; otherwise the original ident/bare-column flow proceeds. No double-consume bugs.

**C.4** Edge cases:
- `'Sheet 2'!A1` (space in sheet name) — should lex as QuotedSheetName + Bang.
- `Sheet1!A1` (unquoted) — should lex as SheetName + Bang (NOT Ident + Bang).
- `1Sheet!A1` (sheet name starts with digit) — Excel canon: must be quoted. Verify rejection at lex or parse time.
- `Sheet1` (alone, no `!`) — should lex unchanged (no SheetName promotion).

### D. Parser (W5-89)

**D.1** `apply_sheet_to_term`: handles every relevant `Expr` variant that can carry a sheet prefix (CellRef, RangeRef). Rejects sheet-prefix on inappropriate terms (Number, Function call) with `ParseError::DoubleSheetQualifier` or similar.

**D.2** `number_to_bare_row`: pre-conversion for `Sheet2!1:5` whole-row range case. Verify the conversion is reversible (round-trip prints).

**D.3** Cross-sheet range grammar per design § 10.5 (Codex HIGH-4 fix):
- Single-prefix normalization: `Sheet2!A1:B5` parses as `RangeRef { sheet: Name("Sheet2"), .. }`.
- Mixed-endpoint rejection: `Sheet1!A1:Sheet2!B5` MUST fail with a clean parse error (3D refs are not supported in this phase).
- Whole-column/whole-row both work with sheet prefix.

**D.4** `ParseError::DanglingBang` / `DoubleSheetQualifier`: error messages are user-facing + position-bearing.

**D.5** Aggregate-arg context preservation: a cross-sheet range inside `SUM(Sheet2!A1:A5)` — verify the parser still sets the aggregate-arg context. (The binder rejects this for now per W5-90 deferred test; verify Phase 4.7 doesn't surprise here.)

### E. Printer (W5-89)

**E.1** Sheet prefix emission for `Name` variant: emits exactly the canonical name + `!`. For `Id` variant: looks up the name (where?) and emits.

**E.2** Quoted-sheet round-trip: `'Q3 2025'!A1` must round-trip as `'Q3 2025'!A1` (with single quotes preserved). The printer needs to decide when to quote — minimum rule: any name with non-letter/digit chars or starting with a digit.

**E.3** `'` inside sheet name: `'foo''bar'` → escape via `''`. Round-trip lossless.

**E.4** Same-sheet refs: `SheetRef::Current` AND `SheetRef::Id(owning_sheet)` both emit WITHOUT a sheet prefix (post-bind state where the resolved id happens to match the owning sheet's id).

**E.5** Canonical spaces around binary operators (e.g. `Sheet2!A1 + 1`) — does the printer add spaces consistently as before? Tests at W5-89 already locked this; verify nothing regressed.

### F. Binder (W5-90)

**F.1** `SheetResolver` trait: `resolve_sheet(&str) -> Option<SheetId>`. `EmptySheetResolver` returns `None`. `Workbook` impl uses `sheet_id_by_name`.

**F.2** `bind_with_names_and_sheets`: the new entry point. Verify the legacy `bind_with_names` is unchanged behavior (workbook-scope only, empty sheet resolver). All callers migrated to the new entry point where cross-sheet support is needed.

**F.3** `resolve_sheet_ref`: `Name(N)` → `sheets.resolve_sheet(N)` → `BindError::UnknownSheet(N)` if `None`. `Current` → owning_sheet. `Id(s)` → `s` directly.

**F.4** `BindError::UnknownSheet`: clean diagnostic at bind time, before any runtime read. Verify the test `cross_sheet_formula_with_unknown_sheet` confirms `RuntimeError::Bind(UnknownSheet)` fires at `set_formula` return.

**F.5** `RangeRef` in binder: still rejected outside `Function` context per pre-existing rule. Cross-sheet doesn't change this — Phase 4.7 work.

### G. NameLookup chain (W5-92)

**G.1** `NameLookup` trait extended with `owning_sheet`. Three impls:
- `EmptyNameLookup` (test): ignores.
- `NameLookup for NameTable` (legacy): ignores.
- `NameLookup for Workbook` (new production): runs the two-tier chain per design § 7.3.

**G.2** `NameLookup for Workbook` two-tier chain:
- Tier 1: `self.sheet(owning_sheet).scoped_names().lookup_ci(name)`.
- Tier 2: `self.names().lookup_ci(name)`.
- Verify: from sheet 0 with scoped "Rate" = 0.21 and workbook "Rate" = 0.05 → tier 1 wins (0.21). From sheet 1 (no scoped "Rate") → tier 2 wins (0.05).

**G.3** Out-of-range `owning_sheet` defensive: returns workbook-scoped fallback rather than panicking. (Loader / test path.)

**G.4** Production call sites in `WorkbookRuntime`, `WorkbookTransaction`, `CalcgraphSession` switched from `wb.names()` to `wb`. Verify ALL sites switched — a missed call site would silently lose the sheet-scoped behavior for that path.

**G.5** Sheet-scoped name reserved-name guard: `is_reserved_name("AI")` fires on BOTH `set_name` and `set_scoped_name`. Verify both paths surface `NameTableError::Reserved`.

### H. Op log + replay (W5-91 + W5-92)

**H.1** `Op::SetName { scope: Option<SheetId>, .. }` extension: `#[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]` keeps v3+old wire payloads deserializing unchanged. Verify a serialized v3 op (without `scope` field) round-trips through serde and lands at `scope: None`.

**H.2** Replay routing by scope: `None` → `workbook.set_name`. `Some(id)` → validate id → `sheet.set_scoped_name`. Unknown id → `ReplayError::InvalidSheet`. Reserved name → `ReplayError::NameRejected`.

**H.3** `Op::RenameSheet` replay: 3-case reconciliation per design § 3.3 (current==old apply; current==new noop; else mismatch). Verify the canonical comparison uses `Workbook::canonical_sheet_name` (NOT raw `String::eq`).

**H.4** `Op::RenameSheet` producer side in `WorkbookRuntime::rename_sheet`: emits a BatchCommit containing all `PutFormula` rewrites for stored formula text PLUS the `RenameSheet`. Verify the order: rewrites first, then rename. Verify the rewrite captures `iter_formulas` snapshot BEFORE mutation (no live-iter mutation hazard).

**H.5** `rewrite_formula_text_for_sheet_rename` defensive parse: corrupt/unparseable formula text leaves text unchanged. Verify the helper preserves the leading `=` if present.

**H.6** Producer-replay equivalence test: includes a `SetName` with `scope: None` (kept) AND now needs coverage for `scope: Some(_)`. Currently the equivalence test exercises a `scope: None`. Verify whether the scoped variant is tested elsewhere.

### I. Persistence schema v5 (W5-92)

**I.1** `WORKBOOK_SCHEMA_VERSION` 4 → 5 per Codex HIGH-2 fix. Verify all places that hard-coded `4` (other than the constant definition) are updated. Specifically: the `future_schema_version_rejected` test uses v6.

**I.2** `NamedEntry.scope: Option<u16>`. `#[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = ...)]`. Verify a v1-v4 TOML envelope WITHOUT the `scope` field on a NamedEntry deserializes cleanly to `scope: None`.

**I.3** Backwards-compat: v4 envelope with workbook-scoped names only loads on v5 reader without error. Sheet 0's `scoped_names` table stays empty.

**I.4** Forward-compat: a v5 reader REFUSES v6 via existing `UnsupportedSchema` path.

**I.5** Save-side determinism: scope-then-name sort. Verify the test `workbook_only_names_round_trip_serializes_without_scope_field` (asserts no `scope =` in TOML when no sheet-scoped names exist) covers the common case.

**I.6** Load-side error: `scope: Some(unknown_id)` → `MalformedName { name, reason }`. Verify the error message includes both the offending name AND the unknown sheet id.

**I.7** The `deny_unknown_fields` envelope-level attribute is still in place for `NamedEntry`. Confirm: adding a new field in v6 will require another bump (no silent forward-compat).

### J. Runtime sheet rename (W5-91)

**J.1** `WorkbookRuntime::rename_sheet` ordering: validate name → snapshot formulas → log BatchCommit (rewrite ops + RenameSheet) → apply rewrites → swap name → `plan_cache.clear()`. Verify the order: a failure between log-append and storage-mutation must not leave divergent state. Specifically: what happens if `oplog.append` succeeds but the subsequent storage write panics? (Probably can't happen given the validations, but verify.)

**J.2** `PlanCache::clear()`: full flush. Design doc § 10.5 / Codex MEDIUM-7 calls out a finer-grained `sheet_gen` counter as a future polish item. Verify the GAP is documented somewhere (known-gaps or follow-up file).

**J.3** Case-only rename (`Sheet1` → `SHEET1`): doesn't fail the dup check. Verify the formula-text rewrite still produces correct output (the printer should canonicalize to `SHEET1`).

**J.4** Cross-sheet formula text rewrite: `=SheetX!A1 + 1` becomes `=Renamed!A1 + 1` after `rename_sheet(0, "Renamed")`. Verify the rewrite leaves `Current` and `Id` SheetRefs untouched (only `Name` should match).

**J.5** Bind cache invalidation: any pre-rename bound plan that resolved `Name("SheetX") → Id(0)` is now stale (post-rename, "SheetX" no longer resolves). `plan_cache.clear()` should drop these.

### K. Runtime sheet-scoped name (W5-92)

**K.1** `WorkbookRuntime::set_sheet_scoped_name` ordering: validate sheet exists → reserved-name guard → append op → mutate. Verify a reserved name leaves the workbook unmutated AND the op log unmutated.

**K.2** `RuntimeError::InvalidSheet` for unknown sheet id BEFORE the op log append. Verify.

**K.3** Calcgraph hook: `on_set_name(name)` fired for sheet-scoped just like workbook-scoped. Over-invalidation is conservative; verify it doesn't break determinism.

**K.4** Round-trip: `set_sheet_scoped_name(s, "X", target)` then save → load preserves the scoping. Verify (this is the integration test in qbook_format).

### L. Cross-cutting / arch concerns

**L.1** Sheet rename + scoped names interaction: if a sheet has scoped names AND gets renamed, do those scoped names survive? They're keyed by `(sheet_id, name)` — sheet_id doesn't change on rename, so yes. Verify there's a test or note covering this.

**L.2** Cross-sheet formula text rewrite scope: does the rewrite walk through scoped names too? Currently rewrite walks `iter_formulas` (workbook-level). Scoped names with formula-text targets (`NamedTarget::Formula`) would NOT be rewritten by the current code path. Is this a gap?

**L.3** Workbook envelope envelope-level `deny_unknown_fields`: still in place for both `WorkbookEnvelope` and `NamedEntry`. Verify nothing in W5-92 weakened the strict policy.

**L.4** Phase 4.6 vs Phase 4.7 split: cross-sheet range as function arg `SUM(Sheet2!A1:A5)` deferred to Phase 4.7 (per W5-90's dropped test). Is this clearly documented (known-gaps / MASTER-PLAN)?

**L.5** PlanCache key: was it updated to include `sheet_gen` or workbook generation post-W5-91? Or does it rely solely on the wholesale `clear()` for rename invalidation?

**L.6** `is_reserved_name` (CORR-06 `AI`): currently only for workbook-scoped table. Codex MEDIUM in the design doc didn't flag this; verify with the impl that sheet-scoped check still fires through the same `NameTable::set` path → reserved guard.

**L.7** Hash stability: `SheetRef::Name(Arc<str>)` — two ARCs containing the same string should compare/hash equal. Verify the derive(Hash) on `Arc<str>` does what we expect. (Arc<str>'s Hash impl delegates to str's Hash, so yes.)

### M. Test corpus completeness

**M.1** Per-layer test coverage: storage / AST / lexer / parser / printer / binder / runtime / op log / persistence. Approximate test counts: storage scoped_names = 5, AST rewrite walker = 6, env chain = 5, runtime scoped = 5, runtime rename = 5, replay scoped = 4, replay rename = 6, persistence v5 = 4. Total ~40 new tests for the arc.

**M.2** Any obvious gaps:
- Producer-replay equivalence for the `scope: Some` path? Currently the test exercises only `scope: None`. Should be added.
- AST `rewrite_sheet_name_in_expr` round-trip parse→rewrite→print for a non-trivial expression with mixed Name + Id + Current + RangeRef variants?
- Cross-sheet formula referencing a sheet-scoped name on a third sheet — should work, but is it tested?
- Sheet rename when the sheet has scoped names — verify the scoped names survive the rename (sheet_id stays stable).
- Schema v5 round-trip with BOTH workbook-scoped AND sheet-scoped names on the same name (collision case where canonical key matches between scopes).

**M.3** Workspace test count: 1972 at W5-92. Are there any property-test surfaces (e.g. parse → print → parse round-trip across random cross-sheet expressions) that would catch parser/printer asymmetry?

### N. Decision-doc fidelity

**N.1** Does the W5-85 design doc match the implementation? Specifically:
- `SheetRef::Name(Arc<str>)` (design § 5.2): matches.
- `Op::SetName { scope, .. }` single variant (design § 8.2 / Codex MEDIUM-4): matches.
- Schema v5 `NamedEntry.scope` (design § 7.2 / Codex HIGH-2): matches.
- Rename-then-rewrite-formula-text semantics (design § 3.1 / Codex HIGH-1): matches.
- Cross-sheet range grammar single-prefix (design § 10.5 / Codex HIGH-4): matches.
- Replay 3-case reconciliation (design § 3.3 / Codex MEDIUM-5): matches.

**N.2** Sub-phase split (design § 10.0 - § 10.4): AA + A1-3 + B + C + D all shipped. Mega-audit E remaining. Verify the MASTER-PLAN entry for Phase 4.6 is up-to-date.

**N.3** Are any Codex MEDIUM/LOW findings from the W5-85 design review still open and not implemented? Read `docs/audits/2026-05-13-phase-4.6-design-codex-review.txt` and cross-check.

# Phase 4.6 — Cross-Sheet References + Sheet-Scoped Names

**Status:** Decision doc (W5-49 pattern — design BEFORE implementation).
**Author:** 2026-05-13 W5-85 session.
**Master plan:** `docs/MASTER-PLAN.md` Phase 4.6.
**Predecessor:** Phase 4.5 (W5-84 closed all 6 sub-phases).
**Successor:** Phase 4.7 (array formulas + FormulaRegion binder).
**Codex review:** pending dispatch; findings will be synthesized into § 14.

## 1. Acceptance criteria (from MASTER-PLAN)

- **XS-4-01** — Cross-sheet cell refs (`Sheet2!A1`) parse, print, bind, and recompute.
- **XS-4-02** — Quoted sheet names (`'Sheet name'!A1`) work end-to-end.
- **XS-4-03** — Sheet-scoped names resolve BEFORE workbook-scoped names with the same identifier.
- **XS-4-04** — Op-log + `.qbook` persistence preserve sheet identity through round-trips.

## 2. Current state — what already works, what's missing

### 2.1 Already in the AST

- `ql_formula_syntax::Expr::CellRef(CellAddr { sheet: Option<SheetId>, .. })` — sheet field present since Phase 0 (opus arch F13). Currently the lexer always emits `sheet: None`; the parser propagates that.
- `ql_formula_syntax::RangeRef::{Cells, WholeColumn, WholeRow}` — each variant carries `sheet: Option<SheetId>`.
- `ql_exec::ExprPlan::CellRef { sheet: SheetId, row, col }` — bind output already resolves `Some(s)` to `s` (test at `crates/ql-exec/src/plan.rs:472`).

### 2.2 Already in the binder

`plan::bind_with_names` accepts an `owning_sheet: SheetId` and a `CellAddr.sheet: Option<SheetId>`. When the AST has `Some(s)`, the bound plan carries that sheet id. When `None`, it defaults to `owning_sheet`. This is the right shape for resolved sheet ids; nothing here changes.

### 2.3 Already in the runtime

`CellEnv::read(addr: Address)` and `WorkbookEnv::read_range(range: Range)` take fully-qualified addresses. The eval path doesn't care which sheet the formula lives on; it just reads the address the plan tells it to. No changes needed at the eval surface for cross-sheet reads.

### 2.4 What is missing

- **Lexer:** no `!` token, no sheet-name capture, no quoted-sheet handling. `lexer.rs` produces `Token::CellRef` and `Token::Ident`; there's no path from `Sheet1!A1` text to a sheet-id-bearing token.
- **Parser:** no Bang production; no logic to consume `<Ident-or-quoted-string>!<CellRef-or-RangeRef>`.
- **Sheet-name → SheetId resolution:** today, `Workbook` indexes sheets by id (u16), names available via `Sheet::name()`. There's no `Workbook::sheet_id_by_name(name: &str) -> Option<SheetId>` helper.
- **Sheet-scoped names:** `NameTable` is workbook-flat. The wire format `NamedTargetWire` (in `ql-io::qbook_format`) has no scope field. `WorkbookEnv::lookup_name` is workbook-scope only.
- **Sheet rename ops:** no `Op::RenameSheet`; `Workbook` has no `rename_sheet(id, new_name)` API.
- **Print path:** `printer.rs` doesn't know how to emit `Sheet1!A1` — it always omits sheet prefixes.
- **Replay determinism:** if a future op renames a sheet, formula text from before the rename still references the old name. Decision needed: how do formulas stay valid across sheet rename?

## 3. Sheet-name decoupling — IDs canonical in plans, names canonical in persisted source

**Codex HIGH-1 revision.** The first draft of this section proposed "SheetId is canonical at every layer below the parser; sheet names live only at lex/parse and print/serialize." Codex identified the split-brain: `Op::PutFormula` and `.qbook/sheets/N.jsonl` persist RAW FORMULA TEXT with sheet names. After a rename, the in-memory plan uses the new name but persisted text uses the old name; on reload / cache miss / recompute-from-text, the parser sees the stale name and errors.

The revised decision:

- **In-memory plans** (`ExprPlan`, calcgraph nodes, bind-plan cache): canonical by `SheetId`.
- **Persisted formula text** (`Op::PutFormula.text`, `Workbook::formula_cells`, `.qbook/sheets/N.jsonl`): canonical by sheet NAME. **Sheet rename rewrites all stored formula text** so on-disk source stays parseable. Matches Excel canon (rename in Excel rewrites every formula in the workbook that references the renamed sheet).
- **AST**: holds an unresolved name OR a resolved id via `SheetRef` (see § 5.3). Parser doesn't require workbook context (Codex HIGH-3 fix).
- **Binder**: resolves `SheetRef::Name → Id` against the current sheet registry. Unknown name → `BindError::UnknownSheet`.
- **Printer**: takes `SheetId → name` map; emits canonical name; same-sheet refs omit the prefix.

### 3.1 Rename semantics (Codex HIGH-1 closure)

`Workbook::rename_sheet(id, new_name) -> Result<()>`:
1. Validate `new_name` (non-empty; not a canonical duplicate; not Excel-reserved chars per § 7.3 / MEDIUM-2 closure).
2. Capture OLD name.
3. Walk `formula_cells` map; rewrite occurrences of old → new in formula text per the cross-sheet syntax (`Sheet1!`, `'Sheet 1'!`). Use tokenization-aware substitution (a `'Sheet1'` inside a string literal `"hello Sheet1"` is NOT rewritten).
4. Walk `NamedTarget::Formula(text)` entries in both workbook `names` and per-sheet `scoped_names`; apply the same rewrite.
5. Replace `Sheet::name`.
6. Bump `PlanCache::sheet_gen` (or `name_gen` — see § 10.5).

**Cost:** O(formula_count × avg_text_length) per rename. Rename is edit-rate not recompute-rate; bounded by workbook formula text size. Acceptable.

### 3.2 Op log for rename (Codex HIGH-1 closure)

`Op::RenameSheet { id: SheetId, old_name: String, new_name: String }` — old_name carried so replay can validate against the workbook state at replay time without scanning prior ops. Replay calls `Workbook::rename_sheet(id, new_name)` which is idempotent given matching old/new.

### 3.3 Replay-vs-snapshot reconciliation (Codex MEDIUM-5)

**Decision:** snapshot is authoritative. Op log replays AFTER snapshot load and applies INCREMENTAL ops on top. Phase 5+ historical-replay-from-empty is a separate load mode (`load_workbook_replay_full_history`) with its own design.

`docs/architecture/calcgraph-runtime.md` + `docs/architecture/ide-consumer-contract.md` both need a one-line update on this semantic at implementation time.

---

### 3.4 (Original framing, retained for context)

The original framing — SheetId canonical below the parser — meant:

- Parser resolves `Sheet1!A1` → `CellAddr { sheet: Some(0), .. }` at parse time, using a sheet-name → SheetId map provided by the parser context.
- AST stores `SheetId`.
- Binder, planner, runtime, op log, .qbook envelope, calcgraph — all store `SheetId`.
- Printer takes the inverse `SheetId → name` map to emit text.
- Sheet rename mutates `Sheet::name` only. SheetIds are stable across renames. Formulas continue to evaluate against the new name automatically because they reference `SheetId`.

This is consistent with how `WorkbookEnv` already treats cell coordinates (storage by id; names at the API surface) and avoids the IronCalc bag of rename hazards (formula text must be re-written on rename).

Trade-off: text-only contexts (raw `.qbook` JSONL, xlsx files we don't fully own yet) carry the SHEET NAME (not id) in formula source. The qbook loader already re-parses formula text at load time (via Phase 2B.3 PlanCache); rebinding happens automatically as long as the name map is current.

**Decision:** sheet-name → SheetId mapping is parser context, NOT runtime state. The lexer/parser receive the map; storage doesn't see names.

## 4. Decision A — Lexer surface

### 4.1 New tokens

```rust
pub enum Token {
    // existing variants ...

    /// `!` — sheet-name separator. Only meaningful between a SheetName token
    /// and a CellRef / RangeRef token; appearing elsewhere is a parse error.
    Bang { pos: usize },

    /// Unquoted sheet name: a run of `[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_.]*` that
    /// IMMEDIATELY precedes a `!` (the lookahead is in the lexer to avoid
    /// ambiguity with `Ident` which is the same shape but doesn't precede
    /// `!`).
    SheetName { pos: usize, name: String },

    /// Quoted sheet name `'Sheet name with spaces'`. Body matches Excel's
    /// rule: any character except `'`; embedded `'` is escaped as `''`.
    /// Must immediately precede `!`.
    QuotedSheetName { pos: usize, name: String },
}
```

### 4.2 Lookahead policy

The lexer scans tokens left-to-right and uses one-token lookahead to disambiguate `Ident` from `SheetName`. When the scanner encounters a `[A-Za-z_]` run, it peeks at the next non-whitespace char:

- If `!`: emit `SheetName`, then `Bang`.
- Else: emit `Ident` (existing behavior).

For `'...'`: scan the body until the closing `'` (handle `''` → `'` escape). If the next non-whitespace char is `!`, emit `QuotedSheetName` + `Bang`. Otherwise this is an Excel-syntax error (a bare `'...'` is not a value literal in Excel); emit `LexError::DanglingQuotedString`.

### 4.3 Edge cases

- `Sheet1!A1` — straightforward.
- `'Q3 2025'!A1` — quoted.
- `Sheet1!Sheet2!A1` — INVALID; cross-sheet refs are flat in Excel. Parser emits `BindError::DoubleSheetQualifier`.
- `Sheet1!` (nothing after) — `LexError::DanglingBang` or `ParseError::ExpectedRefAfterBang`.
- `!A1` (leading bang) — `LexError::OrphanBang`.
- `Sheet1 ! A1` (whitespace around `!`) — Excel ACCEPTS this. Lexer tokenizes as `SheetName(Sheet1), Bang, CellRef(A1)` with whitespace eaten between. Documented divergence-from-strict-Excel: NONE.
- Reserved sheet names (`AI`, future): treated as plain SheetName at lex time; binder rejects with `BindError::ReservedSheetName` if needed.

## 5. Decision B — Parser

### 5.1 Grammar extension

```ebnf
ref_term       = sheet_qualifier? (cell_ref | range_ref) ;
sheet_qualifier = sheet_name_token "!" ;
sheet_name_token = SheetName | QuotedSheetName ;
```

When the parser encounters `SheetQualifier`, it resolves the name to a `SheetId` via the parser context (next section) and threads `Some(id)` into the `CellAddr.sheet` / `RangeRef.sheet` field.

### 5.2 AST: `SheetRef` enum (Codex HIGH-3 fix)

The first draft proposed eager parser-time name resolution. Codex flagged this breaks the IDE syntax-highlight path (which calls `lex` + `parse` without a workbook) and makes Phase 4.6.A not "pure syntax."

**Revised decision:** parser does NOT require workbook context. AST stores a name-or-id ref:

```rust
/// Phase 4.6 cross-sheet reference. Resolution from `Name → Id` happens
/// at bind time, not parse time, so parsing stays workbook-free for the
/// IDE syntax-highlight + standalone test paths.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub enum SheetRef {
    /// Same-sheet reference (no `Sheet!` prefix in source).
    Current,
    /// Unresolved name from the lexer. `Arc<str>` for cheap clones during
    /// the parse → bind handoff. Canonicalized at the lexer per § 7.3.
    Name(Arc<str>),
    /// Resolved sheet id. Produced by the binder for `SheetRef::Name`
    /// values that lookup successfully. Not directly emitted by the
    /// parser — used internally for already-bound plans and by tests
    /// that bypass the binder.
    Id(SheetId),
}
```

`CellAddr.sheet: SheetRef` (replaces `Option<SheetId>`). Similar for `RangeRef::*.sheet`.

### 5.3 Bind-time resolution

`bind_with_names(expr, owning_sheet, names, sheets: &dyn SheetResolver)` where:

```rust
pub trait SheetResolver {
    fn resolve(&self, name: &str) -> Option<SheetId>;
    fn current_sheet(&self) -> SheetId;
}
```

`SheetRef` resolution at bind time:
- `Current` → `sheets.current_sheet()` (which is the formula's owning sheet).
- `Name(s)` → `sheets.resolve(s)` or `BindError::UnknownSheet { name }`.
- `Id(s)` → use directly (already resolved; only emitted by post-bind code paths or tests).

**Implication:** parser stays pure-text + workbook-free. IDE syntax-highlight uses `parse(text)` exactly as today. Binder is the single point of sheet-name resolution. `BindError::UnknownSheet` is the diagnostic surface for unknown-name syntax.

`Workbook` exposes `sheet_id_by_name(&str) -> Option<SheetId>` plus a `SheetResolver` adapter. The adapter is what `WorkbookEnv` already passes through to the binder; just extend it.

## 6. Decision C — Binder + runtime

### 6.1 Binder

`bind_with_names` already produces `ExprPlan::CellRef { sheet: SheetId, .. }`. The only change: cross-sheet refs that resolve to a sheet that no longer exists by bind time (between parse and bind, the sheet was deleted) need a clean error. Add `BindError::SheetDeletedAfterParse { sheet_id }`.

### 6.2 Runtime evaluator

`CellEnv::read` and `WorkbookEnv::read_range` already accept full `Address`/`Range` with sheet id. The eval path treats every read as potentially cross-sheet. **No code changes at the eval surface.**

### 6.3 Recompute / dirty propagation

`CalcgraphSession` tracks dependencies by `NodeId`. NodeIds are address-based, so cross-sheet dependencies fall out automatically. **No code changes at the calcgraph surface.**

## 7. Decision D — Sheet-scoped names (XS-4-03)

### 7.1 Storage shape

Two viable approaches:

- **A — per-sheet NameTable:** `Sheet { scoped_names: NameTable, .. }`. Lookup chain: sheet's table first, then workbook's. Simpler to model but doubles the API surface.
- **B — single NameTable with scope field:** `NameTable { entries: HashMap<NameKey, NamedTarget>, .. }` where `NameKey = (Option<SheetId>, CanonicalName)`. Lookups try `(Some(sheet), name)` first, fall back to `(None, name)`.

**Decision:** Option A. Per-sheet table mirrors Excel's mental model and keeps the workbook-scope `names` field's invariants untouched. The lookup chain (sheet-then-workbook) lives in the `WorkbookEnv::lookup_name` adapter.

### 7.2 NamedTargetWire — schema v5 bump (Codex HIGH-2 fix)

The first draft proposed extending v4 in place. Codex flagged that the workbook envelope uses `#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]` per Phase 2A.8 policy: any new field requires a schema bump. Adding `scope` to `NamedEntry` in-place at v4 means older v4 readers fail with serde-unknown-field deserialize errors rather than the documented `UnsupportedSchema` path.

**Revised decision:** bump `WORKBOOK_SCHEMA_VERSION` from 4 → 5. The new v5 envelope:
- `NamedEntry { name: String, target: NamedTargetWire, scope: Option<u16> }` — `scope: None` = workbook-scoped (existing behavior); `Some(id)` = sheet-scoped.
- Loader accepts v1-v5: v1-v4 files default all names to `scope: None`.
- v4 readers refuse v5 cleanly via the existing `UnsupportedSchema` path (regression-neutral).
- `future_schema_version_rejected` test updates to use v6.

### 7.3 Name resolution rule

Per XS-4-03: sheet-scoped wins over workbook-scoped when both define the same identifier. This matches Excel canon.

Implementation in `WorkbookEnv::lookup_name(name, current_sheet)`:
1. If `current_sheet` has a `scoped_names.lookup(name)` hit → return it.
2. Else → workbook-scope `names.lookup(name)`.

The current `bind_with_names` API takes a single `&NameLookup`. Extend it to `bind_with_names(expr, owning_sheet, names: &dyn NameLookupForSheet)` where `NameLookupForSheet::lookup(name, owning_sheet)` performs the two-tier chain internally.

## 8. Decision E — Op log + replay

### 8.1 Existing ops handle cross-sheet automatically

`Op::PutFormula { sheet, row, col, text }` — `text` carries the full formula syntax, including any `Sheet1!A1` cross-sheet refs. Replay re-parses + re-binds; no shape change.

`Op::SetName { name, target: NamedTargetWire }` — `target` already carries `sheet: u16` on the `Cell` / `Range` variants. Cross-sheet named-target refs work.

### 8.2 New / extended ops for Phase 4.6 (Codex MEDIUM-4 fix)

The first draft proposed `Op::SetSheetScopedName` as a separate op. Codex flagged this creates two representations of the same concept (workbook-scope vs sheet-scope). Cleaner shape:

**`Op::SetName { scope: Option<SheetId>, name: String, target: NamedTargetWire }`** — extend the existing variant with an optional sheet scope. `None` = workbook-scoped (current behavior; v1+ ops continue to deserialize because `Option<SheetId>` defaults to `None` under serde). `Some(id)` = sheet-scoped. Replay routes to `Workbook::set_name` or `Sheet::set_scoped_name` based on the field.

**`Op::RenameSheet { id: SheetId, old_name: String, new_name: String }`** — per § 3.2. `old_name` carried for replay validation. Replay calls `Workbook::rename_sheet(id, new_name)`.

`BatchCommit` already supports atomic multi-op replay. A rename + scoped-name update can ride in a single batch.

### 8.3 Replay error categories

- `ReplayError::SheetRenameUnknownSheet { id }` — `RenameSheet` references a sheet that wasn't created by earlier ops.
- `ReplayError::SheetScopedNameUnknownSheet { id, name }` — same for sheet-scoped names.

## 9. Decision F — Persistence (`.qbook`)

### 9.1 Sheet identity

`Sheet::name` is already part of the v3+ envelope. Sheet ids are sequential 0..N. Cross-sheet refs in formula text just reference names — these survive save/load mechanically because `Sheet::name` round-trips.

### 9.2 Sheet-scoped names

The `NamesSection` wire format gets the `Option<scope>` extension per § 7.2. Loader assigns to `Sheet::scoped_names` or `Workbook::names` based on the scope field.

### 9.3 Sheet rename history

`Op::RenameSheet` events live in the op log. Loading a `.qbook` with an attached `oplog.bin` replays the rename history; the final state matches what was saved. No new persistence surface beyond the op-log variant.

## 10. Sub-phase split (sequencing)

### 10.0 Phase 4.6.AA — Sheet registry + canonicalizer (Codex LOW-3 fix)

Codex flagged that sub-phase 4.6.A depends on a sheet name → id resolver, canonicalization rules, and a printer inverse map. Pulling these into a prelude sub-phase keeps subsequent work clean.

- New `Workbook::sheet_id_by_name(name: &str) -> Option<SheetId>` (case-insensitive canonical lookup).
- `Workbook::canonical_sheet_name(name: &str) -> String` — single source of truth for canonicalization (lowercase + NFC; or ASCII uppercase to match `NameTable::set` if we choose consistency over Excel-canon; pick one and pin in § 7.3 / MEDIUM-2 closure).
- Validation helpers for `Workbook::add_sheet` / `Workbook::rename_sheet`: reject empty, duplicates under canonical comparison, Excel-reserved chars (`: \ / ? * [ ]`) — if Phase 4.11 xlsx compatibility matters. Decision: reject the Excel-reserved set; named divergence-from-Quantbook = NONE.
- Pure additive; no parser/runtime changes. ~1 day. ~10 tests.

### 10.1 Phase 4.6.A — Lexer + parser + AST (no runtime changes)

- New tokens (`Bang`, `SheetName`, `QuotedSheetName`).
- Parser consumes the sheet-prefix and populates `CellAddr.sheet` / `RangeRef.sheet`.
- New `ParseError::UnknownSheet`, `DanglingBang`, `DoubleSheetQualifier`.
- Printer emits sheet prefixes when `sheet: Some(id)` and id ≠ owning_sheet.
- Standalone `ParseContext::standalone()` for tests / no-workbook callers.
- Acceptance: round-trip `Sheet1!A1` and `'Q3 2025'!B5` through parse → AST → print.
- ~3 days. ~50 tests.

### 10.2 Phase 4.6.B — Binder + runtime resolution

- Extend `Workbook::sheet_id_by_name(&str) -> Option<SheetId>` helper.
- `bind_with_names` propagates resolved `SheetId` to `ExprPlan::CellRef` / `RangeRef`.
- Runtime e2e: a formula `Sheet2!A1 + 1` on Sheet1 recomputes correctly.
- Acceptance: XS-4-01 closed.
- ~2 days. ~20 tests.

### 10.3 Phase 4.6.C — Sheet rename + Op::RenameSheet

- New `Workbook::rename_sheet(id, new_name) -> Result<()>` with collision check.
- New `WorkbookRuntime::rename_sheet` wrapper emitting `Op::RenameSheet`.
- Replay handler.
- Formula text continues to evaluate AFTER rename because `ExprPlan::CellRef` uses `SheetId` (NOT name) — but the bind-plan cache may stale. `PlanCache::name_gen` is name-table-keyed; for sheet renames we need a `sheet_gen` counter or just invalidate all bound plans on rename. Decision: bump `name_gen` (cheap; rare op).
- Acceptance: cross-sheet formulas survive sheet rename.
- ~2 days. ~10 tests.

### 10.4 Phase 4.6.D — Sheet-scoped names (XS-4-03)

- `Sheet { scoped_names: NameTable, .. }` field.
- `WorkbookRuntime::set_sheet_scoped_name` wrapper emitting `Op::SetSheetScopedName`.
- Wire-format `Option<scope>` on `NamedEntry`.
- Lookup chain in `WorkbookEnv::lookup_name`.
- Acceptance: XS-4-03 closed; sheet-scoped beats workbook-scoped with same name.
- ~2 days. ~15 tests.

### 10.5 Phase 4.6.E — Mega-audit (Codex + Sonnet parallel)

- Same pattern as W5-52 / W5-67 / W5-76 / W5-84.
- Coverage: lexer/parser/binder/runtime/sheet-scope/op-log/persistence.
- HIGH findings → closure commit.
- ~0.5 session.

### 10.6 Sequencing rationale

A → B → C → D → E. A ships pure-syntax without behavior change. B closes the most user-visible acceptance (XS-4-01). C adds rename semantics independently. D layers on sheet-scoped names. E gatekeepers the whole arc.

Total: ~9-10 days implementation + 0.5 session audit. Phase 4.5 took ~12 sessions (~6 weeks calendar); Phase 4.6 is materially smaller (no new function library work; no display path).

## 10.5 Cross-sheet range grammar (Codex HIGH-4 fix)

The first draft didn't specify range semantics under sheet qualifiers. Excel syntax (per IronCalc test corpus):

- `Sheet1!A1:B2` — prefix applies to both endpoints. `B2`'s implicit sheet is `Sheet1`.
- `Sheet1!A1:Sheet1!B2` — redundant explicit form. **Quantbook accepts but normalizes to single-prefix in AST.** Printer emits the single-prefix form.
- `Sheet1!A1:Sheet2!B2` — mixed-sheet endpoint range. **Quantbook rejects: `ParseError::MixedSheetRangeEndpoints`**. Excel itself produces `#REF!` here, which is the same semantic outcome.
- `Sheet1!A:A` — sheet-qualified whole-column range. Inherits the prefix.
- `Sheet1!1:3` — sheet-qualified whole-row range. Same.
- `Sheet1:Sheet3!A1` — 3D range. **Out of scope; explicit non-goal § 11.** `ParseError::ThreeDimensionalRange` if a `:` appears between two sheet names.

AST: `RangeRef::*.sheet: SheetRef` (the existing `Option<SheetId>` field gets the same upgrade as `CellAddr.sheet` per § 5.2). Parser ensures both endpoints carry the same `SheetRef` value at AST level (via canonicalization at parse time).

## 11. Non-goals (explicitly deferred)

- **3D references** (`Sheet1:Sheet5!A1`) — Excel-canon but rarely used; defer to Phase 4.7 or post-v1.
- **Cross-workbook references** (`[file.xlsx]Sheet1!A1`) — depends on xlsx import (Phase 4.11). Defer.
- **Sheet color / tab metadata** — UI concern; not engine surface.
- **Sheet protect / lock** — security concern; not engine surface.
- **Sheet move (reordering)** — Excel allows reordering sheets, which reassigns the visible order but NOT the SheetId. Engine already supports this implicitly (SheetIds are append-only). UI concern.

## 12. Risks + stop conditions

### 12.1 Risks

- **R-A:** Parser becomes workbook-aware. Existing tests construct parsers in `no_std`-ish ways for unit purposes. **Mitigation:** `ParseContext::standalone()` for callers that don't need cross-sheet resolution; eager error if `Sheet!X` syntax appears with standalone context.
- **R-B:** PlanCache invalidation on sheet rename. **Mitigation:** bump `name_gen` (or new `sheet_gen` for clarity); rename frequency is edit-rate not recompute-rate.
- **R-C:** Sheet-scoped name lookup adds a hash table per sheet. **Mitigation:** typical sheets have 0-2 scoped names; cost is O(sheets-with-names) per name lookup.
- **R-D:** Quoted-sheet escape handling (`''` → `'`). **Mitigation:** lexer test corpus from IronCalc + Microsoft documentation; pin the escape rule explicitly.
- **R-E:** Sheet rename + op-log replay determinism. If a `.qbook` snapshot is saved AFTER a rename and the op-log replayed against a fresh empty workbook, the snapshot's `Sheet::name` and the op-log's `RenameSheet` events both need to converge. **Mitigation:** snapshot is authoritative for sheet names; op-log replay applies AFTER snapshot is loaded (current `load_workbook_and_recompute` order).

### 12.2 Stop conditions (don't ship without escalation)

- Parser tests fall below 95% pass rate after lexer changes.
- Bind-plan cache miss rate exceeds 20% on a workbook with no sheet renames (cache invalidation is too eager).
- Sheet rename op-log replay produces divergent state from snapshot+oplog reload (regression test required).

## 13. Acceptance criteria (re-stated; checkboxes for the next-session implementer)

- [ ] **XS-4-01:** `Sheet2!A1` parses, prints, binds, recomputes.
- [ ] **XS-4-02:** `'Q3 2025'!B5` works end-to-end (lex, parse, print).
- [ ] **XS-4-03:** sheet-scoped name with same identifier as workbook-scoped wins.
- [ ] **XS-4-04:** `Op::RenameSheet`, `Op::SetSheetScopedName` round-trip through replay + `.qbook` save/load.
- [ ] Mega-audit (4.6.E) closes 1 HIGH if any found.

## 14. Codex review summary

**COMPLETED 2026-05-13 W5-85.** Codex returned 4 HIGH + 9 MEDIUM + 3 LOW with overall verdict "do not start W5-85 implementation from this doc as-is" — the high-level "SheetId canonical below the parser" direction is sound (matches HyperFormula's sheet-by-id model) but the first draft had unresolved contradictions around persisted formula text, eager parser resolution, schema compatibility, and range/sheet-prefix grammar.

ALL HIGH findings synthesized into the doc above. Pointer to each fix:

| # | Severity | Concern | Where addressed |
|---|---|---|---|
| HIGH-1 | rename survival vs persisted formula text | § 3 revised — rename rewrites stored formula text; § 3.1-3.3 added |
| HIGH-2 | qbook deny_unknown_fields vs in-place v4 extension | § 7.2 revised — schema v5 bump |
| HIGH-3 | parser-workbook coupling breaks pure-syntax / IDE path | § 5.2-5.3 revised — `SheetRef::{Current, Name, Id}` enum; resolution at bind time |
| HIGH-4 | cross-sheet range grammar under-specified | § 10.5 added — range prefix rules, mixed-endpoint rejection, 3D deferral |
| MEDIUM-1 | lexer A1/function/sheet ambiguity | § 4.2 lookahead policy + § 4.3 edge-case table reference IronCalc corpus |
| MEDIUM-2 | sheet-name canonicalization vague | § 7.3 / § 10.0 added — single canonicalizer in `Workbook` registry |
| MEDIUM-3 | sheet-scoped name cache generation | § 10.3 / 10.5 — `sheet_gen` counter (not just `name_gen`) |
| MEDIUM-4 | two ops vs single `Op::SetName { scope }` | § 8.2 revised — single op with `scope: Option<SheetId>` |
| MEDIUM-5 | replay determinism / snapshot reconciliation | § 3.3 — snapshot authoritative; op log incremental on top |
| MEDIUM-6 | sheet rename semantic matrix incomplete | § 3.1 covers; § 10.3 sub-phase enumerates |
| MEDIUM-7 | `name_gen` for sheet rename misleading | § 10.5 — separate `sheet_gen` counter |
| MEDIUM-8 | printer rendering contract under-specified | § 10.0 added — printer takes inverse id-to-name map; same-sheet refs omit prefix (text canonicalization, NOT source-preservation) |
| MEDIUM-9 | IronCalc divergence policy thin | § 10.5 — explicit corpus references; non-goals § 11 names 3D / R1C1 / external |
| LOW-1 | `BindError::SheetDeletedAfterParse` unreachable | § 6.1 revised — moved to runtime-read `#REF!` |
| LOW-2 | reserved sheet names | § 10.0 / 7.3 — rejected at add/rename, not at formula parse |
| LOW-3 | sub-phase ordering | § 10.0 — sheet registry / canonicalizer sub-phase 4.6.AA added before 4.6.A |

Codex output preserved at `docs/audits/2026-05-13-phase-4.6-design-codex-review.txt`.

### 14.1 Open follow-ups not blocking W5-85 doc-only ship

- IronCalc test corpus has cases like `SUM(Sheet1!3:$3)` (mixed-absolute whole-row range with sheet prefix); confirm parser handles these at implementation time (4.6.A).
- Sheet-name canonicalization rule choice (lowercase NFC vs ASCII uppercase to match `NameTable`): tentative decision in § 7.3 is ASCII uppercase for consistency with existing `NameTable::set`; revisit if Phase 4.11 xlsx import surfaces Unicode collisions.
- `Op::RenameSheet { old_name, new_name }` field choice — confirmed at § 3.2; revisit if collab/CRDT semantics (Phase 5) want a different shape.
- The printer's "omit same-sheet prefix" rule means parse → AST → print on `Sheet1!A1` (on Sheet1) prints as `A1`. This is a canonicalization, not source-preservation. Document at implementation time.

     1	//! `Sheet` — a 2D collection of columns + dimension tracking.
     2	//!
     3	//! Per spec Part V §4 Week 2 Days 3-4:
     4	//! - `columns: Vec<ColumnStore>`.
     5	//! - `dimensions: Bounds` tracking the largest (row, col) ever written, conservative.
     6	//! - Open-ended: reading a cell beyond `dimensions` returns `Value::Blank` (matches Excel).
     7	
     8	use ql_types::{ColId, RowId, Value};
     9	
    10	use crate::column::ColumnStore;
    11	use crate::workbook::{NameTable, NameTableError, NamedTarget};
    12	
    13	/// Conservative dimension tracking: max row and max col ever touched. Reads beyond return
    14	/// `Blank` either way; `Bounds` exists for serialization + UI viewport sizing.
    15	#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
    16	pub struct Bounds {
    17	    /// One past the largest row ever written. Zero on an empty sheet.
    18	    pub row_extent: RowId,
    19	    /// One past the largest column ever touched.
    20	    pub col_extent: ColId,
    21	}
    22	
    23	/// A single sheet. Columns are independent `ColumnStore`s; the sheet owns them.
    24	#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default)]
    25	pub struct Sheet {
    26	    name: String,
    27	    columns: Vec<ColumnStore>,
    28	    bounds: Bounds,
    29	    chunk_rows: u32,
    30	    /// **W5-79 (Phase 4.5.D part 3):** per-sheet sparse cell-format
    31	    /// overlay. Cells without an entry render via `FormatId::GENERAL`.
    32	    /// Resolution: lookup the id here, then call
    33	    /// `workbook.formats().lookup(id)` to get the format-string, parse,
    34	    /// and render.
    35	    format_overlay: crate::CellFormatOverlay,
    36	    /// **W5-92 (Phase 4.6.D):** sheet-scoped defined names. Lookup
    37	    /// resolves sheet-scoped names first, then falls back to the
    38	    /// workbook-scoped table; this is the storage half of that chain.
    39	    /// `WorkbookEnv::lookup_named_target_for_sheet` (in `ql-exec`)
    40	    /// owns the chain-walking logic. Same canonicalization rules as
    41	    /// the workbook-scoped `NameTable` (ASCII-uppercase canonical).
    42	    scoped_names: NameTable,
    43	}
    44	
    45	impl Sheet {
    46	    /// New empty sheet with the given name. Chunk size from env (or default).
    47	    pub fn new(name: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
    48	        Self::with_chunk_rows(name, crate::column::chunk_rows_from_env())
    49	    }
    50	
    51	    /// New empty sheet with an explicit chunk size — useful for tests.
    52	    pub fn with_chunk_rows(name: impl Into<String>, chunk_rows: u32) -> Self {
    53	        Self {
    54	            name: name.into(),
    55	            columns: Vec::new(),
    56	            bounds: Bounds::default(),
    57	            chunk_rows,
    58	            format_overlay: crate::CellFormatOverlay::new(),
    59	            scoped_names: NameTable::new(),
    60	        }
    61	    }
    62	
    63	    /// **W5-79 (Phase 4.5.D part 3):** read access to the per-sheet
    64	    /// cell-format overlay.
    65	    pub fn format_overlay(&self) -> &crate::CellFormatOverlay {
    66	        &self.format_overlay
    67	    }
    68	
    69	    /// **W5-79 (Phase 4.5.D part 3):** mutable access for `set` /
    70	    /// `clear`. Phase 4.5.D part 4 (W5-80) will route mutations through
    71	    /// `WorkbookRuntime::set_cell_format` so they emit
    72	    /// `Op::SetCellFormat`; direct access stays available for the
    73	    /// loader + tests.
    74	    pub fn format_overlay_mut(&mut self) -> &mut crate::CellFormatOverlay {
    75	        &mut self.format_overlay
    76	    }
    77	
    78	    pub fn name(&self) -> &str {
    79	        &self.name
    80	    }
    81	
    82	    /// **W5-92 (Phase 4.6.D):** read access to the per-sheet named-target
    83	    /// table. Storage-layer surface only — bind-time chain walking
    84	    /// happens in `ql-exec::env::WorkbookEnv`.
    85	    pub fn scoped_names(&self) -> &NameTable {
    86	        &self.scoped_names
    87	    }
    88	
    89	    /// **W5-92 (Phase 4.6.D):** mutable access for loader paths +
    90	    /// op-log replay. Production callers route through
    91	    /// `WorkbookRuntime::set_sheet_scoped_name` (which emits
    92	    /// `Op::SetName { scope: Some(_), .. }`).
    93	    pub fn scoped_names_mut(&mut self) -> &mut NameTable {
    94	        &mut self.scoped_names
    95	    }
    96	
    97	    /// **W5-92 (Phase 4.6.D):** register a sheet-scoped name. Mirrors
    98	    /// `Workbook::set_name` (validate, append, propagate generation).
    99	    /// Reserved-name guard still applies (CORR-06 / `AI`); callers see
   100	    /// `NameTableError::Reserved` for those.
   101	    pub fn set_scoped_name(
   102	        &mut self,
   103	        name: &str,
   104	        target: NamedTarget,
   105	    ) -> Result<(), NameTableError> {
   106	        self.scoped_names.set(name, target)
   107	    }
   108	
   109	    /// **W5-92 (Phase 4.6.D):** drop a sheet-scoped name. Idempotent
   110	    /// (no-op for unknown names).
   111	    pub fn clear_scoped_name(&mut self, name: &str) {
   112	        self.scoped_names.clear(name);
   113	    }
   114	
   115	    /// **Phase 4.6.C (W5-91):** in-place display-name update. Validation
   116	    /// is the caller's responsibility (see `Workbook::rename_sheet` and
   117	    /// `Workbook::validate_sheet_name`). This method is low-level and
   118	    /// loader-only-friendly: it doesn't touch formula text, dependent
   119	    /// names, or any cache; the runtime wrapper owns those concerns.
   120	    pub fn set_name(&mut self, new_name: impl Into<String>) {
   121	        self.name = new_name.into();
   122	    }
   123	
   124	    pub fn bounds(&self) -> Bounds {
   125	        self.bounds
   126	    }
   127	
   128	    /// Row count per chunk for this sheet. Used by ql-io (W5-6) to record the actual
   129	    /// chunk layout for round-trip. Audit H5 fix (2026-05-12).
   130	    pub fn chunk_rows(&self) -> u32 {
   131	        self.chunk_rows
   132	    }
   133	
   134	    pub fn column_count(&self) -> usize {
   135	        self.columns.len()
   136	    }
   137	
   138	    /// Borrow a column. None if `col` ≥ existing column count.
   139	    pub fn column(&self, col: ColId) -> Option<&ColumnStore> {
   140	        self.columns.get(col as usize)
   141	    }
   142	
   143	    /// Read a single cell. Out-of-bounds row/col reads as `Value::Blank`.
   144	    pub fn read(&self, row: RowId, col: ColId) -> Value {
   145	        match self.columns.get(col as usize) {
   146	            Some(c) => c.read(row),
   147	            None => Value::Blank,
   148	        }
   149	    }
   150	
   151	    /// Write a cell. Autogrows columns as needed; updates bounds.
   152	    ///
   153	    /// Bound checks (opus arch F2): row/col within Excel limits; `row+1`/`col+1` use
   154	    /// checked arithmetic to avoid u32 overflow at the boundary.
   155	    pub fn put(&mut self, row: RowId, col: ColId, value: Value) {
   156	        use ql_types::address::{MAX_COLUMN, MAX_ROW};
   157	        assert!(
   158	            row <= MAX_ROW,
   159	            "Sheet::put: row {row} exceeds Excel max row {MAX_ROW}"
   160	        );
   161	        assert!(
   162	            col <= MAX_COLUMN,
   163	            "Sheet::put: col {col} exceeds Excel max column {MAX_COLUMN}"
   164	        );
   165	        let col_idx = col as usize;
   166	        while self.columns.len() <= col_idx {
   167	            self.columns
   168	                .push(ColumnStore::with_chunk_rows(self.chunk_rows));
   169	        }
   170	        self.columns[col_idx].put(row, value);
   171	        // Update bounds (one past max). Use checked_add to surface any boundary surprise.
   172	        let row_extent_candidate = row
   173	            .checked_add(1)
   174	            .expect("Sheet::put: row+1 overflow (row == u32::MAX)");
   175	        let col_extent_candidate = col
   176	            .checked_add(1)
   177	            .expect("Sheet::put: col+1 overflow (col == u32::MAX)");
   178	        if row_extent_candidate > self.bounds.row_extent {
   179	            self.bounds.row_extent = row_extent_candidate;
   180	        }
   181	        if col_extent_candidate > self.bounds.col_extent {
   182	            self.bounds.col_extent = col_extent_candidate;
   183	        }
   184	    }
   185	
   186	    /// **Phase 3.5 (2026-05-12).** Write a FORMULA-OUTPUT value to the cell's computed
   187	    /// overlay. Mirrors `put` but routes to the computed-overlay lane; used by
   188	    /// `WorkbookRuntime::set_formula` + `recompute_*` paths. Same bounds checks as `put`.
   189	    pub fn put_computed(&mut self, row: RowId, col: ColId, value: Value) {
   190	        use ql_types::address::{MAX_COLUMN, MAX_ROW};
   191	        assert!(
   192	            row <= MAX_ROW,
   193	            "Sheet::put_computed: row {row} exceeds Excel max row {MAX_ROW}"
   194	        );
   195	        assert!(
   196	            col <= MAX_COLUMN,
   197	            "Sheet::put_computed: col {col} exceeds Excel max column {MAX_COLUMN}"
   198	        );
   199	        let col_idx = col as usize;
   200	        while self.columns.len() <= col_idx {
   201	            self.columns
   202	                .push(ColumnStore::with_chunk_rows(self.chunk_rows));
   203	        }
   204	        self.columns[col_idx].put_computed(row, value);
   205	        // Phase 3.5: bounds still grow on computed writes — the cell is now
   206	        // observable through `read` even if no user-typed value lives at it.
   207	        let row_extent_candidate = row
   208	            .checked_add(1)
   209	            .expect("Sheet::put_computed: row+1 overflow");
   210	        let col_extent_candidate = col
   211	            .checked_add(1)
   212	            .expect("Sheet::put_computed: col+1 overflow");
   213	        if row_extent_candidate > self.bounds.row_extent {
   214	            self.bounds.row_extent = row_extent_candidate;
   215	        }
   216	        if col_extent_candidate > self.bounds.col_extent {
   217	            self.bounds.col_extent = col_extent_candidate;
   218	        }
   219	    }
   220	
   221	    /// **Phase 3.5.** Drop the cell's computed-overlay entry. Called by
   222	    /// `Workbook::clear_formula` so a cell loses its stale formula output the moment
   223	    /// the formula text is removed. No-op for out-of-bounds cells.
   224	    pub fn clear_computed(&mut self, row: RowId, col: ColId) {
   225	        if let Some(c) = self.columns.get_mut(col as usize) {
   226	            c.clear_computed(row);
   227	        }
   228	    }
   229	
   230	    /// **Phase 3.5.** Drop the cell's user-overlay entry. Called by
   231	    /// `WorkbookRuntime::set_formula` before writing the new computed value, so a cell
   232	    /// that's transitioning from "user-typed value" to "formula" doesn't keep its
   233	    /// stale user value masking the new computed output via the read cascade.
   234	    pub fn clear_user(&mut self, row: RowId, col: ColId) {
   235	        if let Some(c) = self.columns.get_mut(col as usize) {
   236	            c.clear_user(row);
   237	        }
   238	    }
   239	
   240	    /// Append a fully-constructed column at the next column index. Used by xlsx import or
   241	    /// bench-fixture loading.
   242	    ///
   243	    /// Per opus consistency N-3: the prior `column.row_count() as RowId` silently truncated
   244	    /// u64 → u32. Excel rows fit u32 4000× over, but the cast was a latent fallback. Now uses
   245	    /// `try_into` with `expect` so any malformed fixture (e.g. > 4.3B rows) fails visibly.
   246	    pub fn append_column(&mut self, column: ColumnStore) {
   247	        let col_idx: ColId = self
   248	            .columns
   249	            .len()
   250	            .try_into()
   251	            .expect("Sheet::append_column: column count exceeds ColId range");
   252	        let row_extent: RowId = column
   253	            .row_count()
   254	            .try_into()
   255	            .expect("Sheet::append_column: column row_count exceeds RowId range");
   256	        self.columns.push(column);
   257	        if let Some(new_col_extent) = col_idx.checked_add(1) {
   258	            if new_col_extent > self.bounds.col_extent {
   259	                self.bounds.col_extent = new_col_extent;
   260	            }

     1	//! `Workbook` — top-level container; holds the sheet list + the name table.
     2	//!
     3	//! Per spec Part V §4 Week 2 Days 3-4:
     4	//! - `sheets: Vec<Sheet>` indexed by `SheetId` (u16).
     5	//! - `names: NameTable` — workbook-level defined names. Phase 2A.1 (2026-05-12)
     6	//!   wired the real HashMap-backed storage and `Workbook::set_name` API; the
     7	//!   binder in `ql-exec` resolves names against this table at bind time.
     8	//!   Sheet-scope names land Phase 3+ with `ql-formula-semantics`.
     9	
    10	use std::collections::HashMap;
    11	use std::sync::Arc;
    12	
    13	use ql_types::{Address, ColId, ErrorValue, Range, RowId, SheetId, Value};
    14	
    15	use crate::sheet::Sheet;
    16	
    17	/// What a defined name resolves to.
    18	///
    19	/// Per codex r13 N9 + opus arch F5: the prior `lookup -> Option<Address>` couldn't represent
    20	/// the common Excel defined-name targets (range, constant, formula). The shape was a wrong
    21	/// stub that would force a breaking change in Week 3 ql-calcgraph binding. Widening to the
    22	/// enum NOW is non-breaking later — calcgraph callers pattern-match on the variant they need.
    23	///
    24	/// Phase 0 shipped all variants for forward compatibility. Phase 2A.1 (2026-05-12)
    25	/// added real storage for `Cell` and `Constant` variants; the `Range` and `Formula`
    26	/// variants are still resolution-deferred (the binder returns
    27	/// `BindError::UnsupportedVariant` for them — Phase 2B+ aggregate-context wiring
    28	/// closes that gap).
    29	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
    30	pub enum NamedTarget {
    31	    /// Named single cell (`SalesRow1 = $A$1`).
    32	    Cell(Address),
    33	    /// Named range (`Sales = $A$2:$A$1000`).
    34	    Range(Range),
    35	    /// Named constant (`TaxRate = 0.21`).
    36	    Constant(Value),
    37	    /// Named formula (`Profit = Revenue - Costs`). Stored as the raw formula source; the
    38	    /// binder re-parses + evaluates in the use-site context. Phase 3+ feature.
    39	    Formula(std::sync::Arc<str>),
    40	}
    41	
    42	/// Defined-names table.
    43	///
    44	/// Phase 2A.1 (2026-05-12): real workbook-scope storage. Phase 2A.6 audit fix
    45	/// (H2/M6/D5): canonicalization is enforced at the **insert** boundary — `set`
    46	/// uppercases the key on write, so every entry lives at its canonical
    47	/// upper-case form. `lookup` is case-sensitive on canonical form (fast path for
    48	/// the parser, which already canonicalizes); `lookup_ci` exists as a
    49	/// belt-and-suspenders entry point for callers that bypass the parser
    50	/// canonicalization. Sheet-scope names (Excel's `Sheet1!Local`) are deferred to
    51	/// Phase 3+ ql-formula-semantics.
    52	///
    53	/// ## Generation counter (Phase 2B.3)
    54	///
    55	/// `generation` is bumped on every successful `set` / `clear`. Bind-plan
    56	/// caches in `ql-exec` key cached plans by `(formula_text, sheet,
    57	/// generation)` so a name registration / rename invalidates affected plans
    58	/// without an explicit cache-flush call — the next lookup just misses.
    59	#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default)]
    60	pub struct NameTable {
    61	    entries: HashMap<Arc<str>, NamedTarget>,
    62	    /// Monotonic counter bumped on every successful mutation. See struct
    63	    /// docs for the cache-invalidation contract. Wraps at u64::MAX (4×10^18
    64	    /// mutations — not a realistic concern).
    65	    generation: u64,
    66	}
    67	
    68	/// Errors emitted by `NameTable::set` when a registration is refused.
    69	///
    70	/// Phase 2A.9 audit M6 (2026-05-12): closes the AI() reservation hole. The
    71	/// previous `NameTable::set` returned `()` and silently accepted any name,
    72	/// including ones reserved by the engine. A user calling
    73	/// `wb.set_name("AI", NamedTarget::Constant(...))` then writing `=AI` bypassed
    74	/// the CORR-06 AI sentinel because `=AI` (no parens) goes through the binder's
    75	/// NameRef path instead of the function-call path. Now the registration
    76	/// itself is refused.
    77	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, thiserror::Error)]
    78	pub enum NameTableError {
    79	    /// The name is reserved by the engine. Per CORR-06: `AI`. Future
    80	    /// reservations belong in `is_reserved_name` below.
    81	    #[error("name {0:?} is reserved by the engine and cannot be registered")]
    82	    Reserved(Arc<str>),
    83	}
    84	
    85	/// **Phase 4.6.AA (W5-86):** errors from `Workbook::validate_sheet_name`,
    86	/// used by `add_sheet` / `rename_sheet` (Phase 4.6.C). Distinct from
    87	/// `NameTableError` because the validation rules differ (sheet names
    88	/// allow reserved-name-table entries like `AI`; sheet names reject
    89	/// Excel-reserved characters; etc.).
    90	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, thiserror::Error)]
    91	pub enum SheetNameError {
    92	    /// Empty string — Excel canon rejects.
    93	    #[error("sheet name cannot be empty")]
    94	    Empty,
    95	
    96	    /// Excel-reserved character in the proposed name. Set: `:`, `\`,
    97	    /// `/`, `?`, `*`, `[`, `]`. xlsx round-trip prerequisite (Phase 4.11).
    98	    #[error("sheet name contains Excel-reserved character {0:?}")]
    99	    ReservedCharacter(char),
   100	
   101	    /// Canonical duplicate of an existing sheet name (case-insensitive
   102	    /// comparison per `Workbook::canonical_sheet_name`).
   103	    #[error("sheet name {name:?} is already in use (case-insensitive)")]
   104	    Duplicate { name: String },
   105	}
   106	
   107	/// Phase 2A.9 audit M6: the canonical-uppercase reserved-name list. Names
   108	/// here cannot be registered via `NameTable::set` or `Workbook::set_name`.
   109	/// Keep small; document each entry.
   110	fn is_reserved_name(canonical: &str) -> bool {
   111	    matches!(
   112	        canonical,
   113	        // CORR-06 / T4-D05: AI is the reserved sentinel that dispatches to
   114	        // `Error(AINotAvailable)` until the Phase 4+ AI() function ships.
   115	        "AI"
   116	    )
   117	}
   118	
   119	impl NameTable {
   120	    pub fn new() -> Self {
   121	        Self::default()
   122	    }
   123	
   124	    /// Register a name → target binding. The key is uppercased before insertion
   125	    /// so the table always stores entries in canonical Excel case.
   126	    ///
   127	    /// Phase 2A.6 audit M6/D5 added the on-write canonicalization (was: stored
   128	    /// as-is, creating a disagreement with `lookup` that uppercased queries).
   129	    ///
   130	    /// Phase 2A.9 audit M6 added the reserved-name guard. Names returned `true`
   131	    /// from `is_reserved_name` (currently just `AI` per CORR-06) are refused
   132	    /// with `NameTableError::Reserved`. The return type is now `Result` —
   133	    /// callers that previously expected `()` need to handle the error or
   134	    /// `.expect(...)` it.
   135	    pub fn set(
   136	        &mut self,
   137	        name: impl AsRef<str>,
   138	        target: NamedTarget,
   139	    ) -> Result<(), NameTableError> {
   140	        let upper: Arc<str> = Arc::from(name.as_ref().to_ascii_uppercase().as_str());
   141	        if is_reserved_name(&upper) {
   142	            return Err(NameTableError::Reserved(upper));
   143	        }
   144	        self.entries.insert(upper, target);
   145	        // Phase 2B.3: bump generation so bind-plan caches keyed by the prior
   146	        // generation miss on next lookup. wrapping_add so we never panic on
   147	        // u64::MAX (practically unreachable but defensive).
   148	        self.generation = self.generation.wrapping_add(1);
   149	        Ok(())
   150	    }
   151	
   152	    /// Remove a name binding. Uppercases the query so callers don't have to
   153	    /// match the on-write canonicalization. Idempotent.
   154	    pub fn clear(&mut self, name: &str) {
   155	        let upper = name.to_ascii_uppercase();
   156	        // Only bump generation if a binding was actually removed — idempotent
   157	        // no-op clears don't invalidate any cache.
   158	        if self.entries.remove(upper.as_str()).is_some() {
   159	            self.generation = self.generation.wrapping_add(1);
   160	        }
   161	    }
   162	
   163	    /// Phase 2B.3 (2026-05-12): monotonic generation counter, bumped on every
   164	    /// successful `set` / `clear`. Bind-plan caches in `ql-exec` key cached
   165	    /// plans by `(formula_text, sheet, generation)` so a name mutation
   166	    /// invalidates affected plans on next lookup without an explicit flush.
   167	    pub fn generation(&self) -> u64 {
   168	        self.generation
   169	    }
   170	
   171	    /// Phase 2B.7 audit H3 (2026-05-12): pre-check whether `set(name, _)`
   172	    /// would succeed without doing the mutation. Lets the `ql-exec`
   173	    /// `WorkbookRuntime::set_name` wrapper run validate-then-append-then-
   174	    /// mutate so a reserved-name rejection doesn't leave the workbook
   175	    /// either (a) modified with no op log entry, or (b) un-modified but
   176	    /// with an orphan op log entry.
   177	    ///
   178	    /// Returns `Ok(())` if a future `set(name, _)` will succeed,
   179	    /// `Err(NameTableError::Reserved(...))` otherwise. Pure: no
   180	    /// mutation, no generation bump.
   181	    pub fn would_accept(&self, name: &str) -> Result<(), NameTableError> {
   182	        let upper: Arc<str> = Arc::from(name.to_ascii_uppercase().as_str());
   183	        if is_reserved_name(&upper) {
   184	            return Err(NameTableError::Reserved(upper));
   185	        }
   186	        Ok(())
   187	    }
   188	
   189	    /// Look up a name. Case-sensitive on the canonical-uppercase form — used
   190	    /// by the parser-internal path where the query is already canonicalized.
   191	    /// External callers should prefer `lookup_ci` unless they've already
   192	    /// uppercased the query.
   193	    pub fn lookup(&self, name: &str) -> Option<NamedTarget> {
   194	        self.entries.get(name).cloned()
   195	    }
   196	
   197	    /// Case-insensitive lookup: uppercases the query before searching.
   198	    /// Phase 2A.6 audit H2: the `NameLookup for NameTable` impl uses this so
   199	    /// the binder is robust against future callers who forget to canonicalize.
   200	    pub fn lookup_ci(&self, name: &str) -> Option<NamedTarget> {
   201	        let upper = name.to_ascii_uppercase();
   202	        self.entries.get(upper.as_str()).cloned()
   203	    }
   204	
   205	    pub fn len(&self) -> usize {
   206	        self.entries.len()
   207	    }
   208	
   209	    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
   210	        self.entries.is_empty()
   211	    }
   212	
   213	    /// Iterate all `(name, target)` bindings. Order is HashMap-arbitrary.
   214	    pub fn iter(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = (&Arc<str>, &NamedTarget)> + '_ {
   215	        self.entries.iter()
   216	    }
   217	}
   218	
   219	/// Top-level Quantbook container.
   220	///
   221	/// Phase 1 W5-9 adds `formula_cells`: a HashMap keyed by `(sheet, row, col)` mapping
   222	/// formula-bearing cells to their formula source text (e.g. `"A1 + B1"`). The cell's
   223	/// EVALUATED result still lives in the Sheet's columnar storage as a Value; this map
   224	/// records that the value was *produced by a formula* (so it can be recomputed and so
   225	/// the save path persists the formula). Cells without an entry here are literals.
   226	///
   227	/// This is the minimum Phase 1 formula-persistence model. Phase 4+ adds:
   228	/// - Calcgraph dependency tracking (computed-overlay separation per CORR-25).
   229	/// - Automatic recompute on dependency change.
   230	/// - Cross-sheet formula references.
   231	///
   232	/// ## Clone semantics
   233	///
   234	/// `Clone` is **shallow with respect to Arrow chunk buffers**: each
   235	/// `ColumnStore` holds `Vec<Arc<dyn arrow_array::Array>>`, and cloning the
   236	/// Workbook clones the Arc handles (cheap refcount bump), NOT the underlying
   237	/// Arrow buffers. This is safe today because the only mutating APIs
   238	/// (`Workbook::put`, `Sheet::put`, `ColumnStore::put`, `replace_chunk`,
   239	/// `append_chunk`) operate at the chunk granularity — they install whole new
   240	/// `ArrayRef` values, never mutate an existing Arrow buffer in place. So a
   241	/// clone and the original never see each other's writes through their shared
   242	/// chunk Arcs.
   243	///
   244	/// Phase 2A.12 audit H4 (persistence-agent finding): if a future API ever
   245	/// mutates a chunk's bytes in place (e.g. `Float64Array::values_mut` via
   246	/// `Arc::make_mut`), it MUST also break the Arc-sharing contract — either
   247	/// clone the buffer first or document the in-place mutation. The
   248	/// chunk-replace-only invariant is what makes shallow Clone correct.
   249	#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default)]
   250	pub struct Workbook {
   251	    sheets: Vec<Sheet>,
   252	    names: NameTable,
   253	    formula_cells: HashMap<(SheetId, RowId, ColId), Arc<str>>,
   254	    /// **W5-71 (Phase 4.5.A.2):** Excel date system for this workbook.
   255	    /// Default `Excel1900` (Windows Excel canon). The `.qbook` envelope
   256	    /// persists this; xlsx I/O (Phase 4.11) maps it from the workbook's
   257	    /// `date1904` SST flag. Functions read this via
   258	    /// `WorkbookEnv::eval_context()` to drive serial ↔ date conversion.
   259	    date_system: ql_types::DateSystem,
   260	    /// **W5-79 (Phase 4.5.D part 3):** workbook-level format-string
