     1	//! Abstract Syntax Tree node definitions.
     2	//!
     3	//! Per spec Part V §4 Week 2 Days 5-6. The AST shapes lock the parser's output
     4	//! vocabulary and the binder's input vocabulary; the Phase 1 W5-1 Pratt parser
     5	//! builds these trees, the Phase 1 W5-2 printer round-trips them, and the
     6	//! Phase 0 W4-1 binder in `ql-exec::plan` lowers them to `ExprPlan`.
     7	//!
     8	//! Phase 2A.1 (2026-05-12) added the `NameRef(Arc<str>)` variant for
     9	//! defined-name references; the binder resolves it via the workbook's
    10	//! `NameTable` at bind time.
    11	//!
    12	//! AST list aligned with `_QUANTBOOK-MASTER-PLAN.md` §6.3: Number, String,
    13	//! Bool, CellRef, RangeRef, Binary, Unary, Function, Array, Spill, NameRef.
    14	//! `Array` and `Spill` are reserved for Phase 3+ dynamic-array work; the
    15	//! binder rejects them with `BindError::UnsupportedVariant`.
    16	
    17	use std::sync::Arc;
    18	
    19	use ql_types::{ColId, RowId, SheetId};
    20	
    21	use crate::token::Operator;
    22	
    23	/// A formula expression. Tree-shaped — children are `Box<Expr>` to keep node size bounded.
    24	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
    25	pub enum Expr {
    26	    /// Numeric literal (already coerced to f64; lexer rejects NaN/Inf).
    27	    Number(f64),
    28	
    29	    /// String literal (interior content; quotes stripped).
    30	    String(Arc<str>),
    31	
    32	    /// Boolean literal `TRUE` / `FALSE` (parser recognizes these as keywords when they appear
    33	    /// as bare identifiers).
    34	    Bool(bool),
    35	
    36	    /// Cell reference. Sheet-qualification is deferred; Phase 0 only supports same-sheet refs.
    37	    CellRef(CellAddr),
    38	
    39	    /// Range reference (rectangular, inclusive). Includes whole-column / whole-row forms
    40	    /// via the `start`/`end` shape — when `start.row.is_none()` or `end.row.is_none()`, the
    41	    /// range spans the full column axis. Same idea for cols.
    42	    RangeRef(RangeRef),
    43	
    44	    /// Binary operator application.
    45	    Binary {
    46	        op: Operator,
    47	        lhs: Box<Expr>,
    48	        rhs: Box<Expr>,
    49	    },
    50	
    51	    /// Unary operator application (`-x`, `+x`, `x%`).
    52	    Unary { op: Operator, operand: Box<Expr> },
    53	
    54	    /// Function call. `name` is the function identifier (e.g. `"SUM"`); the parser uppercases
    55	    /// it for canonical comparison. Per CORR-06 / T4-D05: the parser builds `Function { name:
    56	    /// "AI", args: ... }` for any `AI(...)` source; the binder/evaluator then maps the call to
    57	    /// `Error(ErrorValue::AINotAvailable)`. AI is NOT a lexer-level keyword — see token.rs
    58	    /// module doc for the source-text propagation contract.
    59	    Function { name: Arc<str>, args: Vec<Expr> },
    60	
    61	    /// Array literal `{1,2;3,4}`. Phase 0 lexer doesn't recognize `{` — this variant is
    62	    /// shape-locked for Phase 3+ dynamic-array work.
    63	    Array(Vec<Vec<Expr>>),
    64	
    65	    /// Spill anchor — Phase 3+ dynamic-array result anchor. Shape-locked, not constructed
    66	    /// in Phase 0.
    67	    Spill(Box<Expr>),
    68	
    69	    /// Defined-name reference (Phase 2A.1). Parser emits this for any bare identifier
    70	    /// (4+ letter) that isn't `TRUE`/`FALSE` or followed by `LParen`. The binder
    71	    /// resolves the name against the `NameTable` at bind time:
    72	    /// - `NamedTarget::Cell(addr)` → `ExprPlan::CellRef`
    73	    /// - `NamedTarget::Constant(value)` → matching literal `ExprPlan` variant
    74	    /// - `NamedTarget::Range(range)` → unsupported in Phase 2 (Phase 4+ region binder)
    75	    /// - `NamedTarget::Formula(text)` → unsupported in Phase 2 (Phase 3+ semantic layer)
    76	    /// - Not found → `BindError::UnresolvedName`
    77	    ///
    78	    /// `name` is the canonical (uppercased) form as the parser stored it. NameTable
    79	    /// lookup is case-sensitive on this canonical form; the parser owns case-folding.
    80	    NameRef(Arc<str>),
    81	}
    82	
    83	/// **W5-87 (Phase 4.6.A part 1):** sheet-qualification for AST references.
    84	///
    85	/// - `Current` — formula's owning sheet (no `Sheet!` prefix in source).
    86	///   The historical `Option::None` value maps here.
    87	/// - `Name(Arc<str>)` — parser-emitted name for `Sheet!X` and `'Sheet'!X`
    88	///   syntax. Binder resolves to `Id` via `SheetResolver` (Phase 4.6.B).
    89	/// - `Id(SheetId)` — already-resolved sheet id (the historical
    90	///   `Option::Some(id)` value, plus what the binder produces after
    91	///   resolving `Name`).
    92	///
    93	/// Per design doc § 5.2 / Codex HIGH-3 fix: parser stays workbook-free
    94	/// by emitting `Name`; resolution happens at bind time. Tests + post-
    95	/// bind code construct `Id` directly.
    96	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
    97	pub enum SheetRef {
    98	    /// No `Sheet!` prefix in source — defaults to the formula's owning sheet.
    99	    Current,
   100	    /// Unresolved sheet name from the lexer. Resolved at bind time.
   101	    Name(Arc<str>),
   102	    /// Resolved sheet id. Produced post-bind or by tests bypassing the binder.
   103	    Id(SheetId),
   104	}
   105	
   106	impl SheetRef {
   107	    /// Backwards-compat helper for the Phase 4.6.A migration: the
   108	    /// historical `Option<SheetId>::None` value maps to `Current`,
   109	    /// `Some(id)` maps to `Id(id)`. Lets call sites that were
   110	    /// constructing `Option<SheetId>` switch with minimal churn.
   111	    pub fn from_option(opt: Option<SheetId>) -> Self {
   112	        match opt {
   113	            None => Self::Current,
   114	            Some(id) => Self::Id(id),
   115	        }
   116	    }
   117	
   118	    /// **W5-87 (Phase 4.6.A part 1):** resolve against a default sheet
   119	    /// (typically the formula's owning sheet). Mirrors the historical
   120	    /// `Option<SheetId>::unwrap_or(owning_sheet)` pattern. PANICS on
   121	    /// `SheetRef::Name`: that variant is parser-only, and any post-
   122	    /// parse code path should have resolved it through the binder
   123	    /// before reaching layers that call this method (calcgraph,
   124	    /// stripes, etc.).
   125	    pub fn resolve_or(&self, owning_sheet: SheetId) -> SheetId {
   126	        match self {
   127	            SheetRef::Current => owning_sheet,
   128	            SheetRef::Id(s) => *s,
   129	            SheetRef::Name(name) => panic!(
   130	                "unresolved SheetRef::Name({name:?}) at a layer that requires \
   131	                 a resolved sheet id — must go through the binder first"
   132	            ),
   133	        }
   134	    }
   135	
   136	    /// **W5-87 (Phase 4.6.A part 1):** if already resolved to an id,
   137	    /// return it; if `Current`, return None (caller decides what
   138	    /// default to use). Used by code paths that DON'T have an owning-
   139	    /// sheet context handy.
   140	    pub fn id(&self) -> Option<SheetId> {
   141	        match self {
   142	            SheetRef::Current => None,
   143	            SheetRef::Id(s) => Some(*s),
   144	            SheetRef::Name(_) => None,
   145	        }
   146	    }
   147	}
   148	
   149	/// **W5-91 (Phase 4.6.C):** recursively rewrite every `SheetRef::Name`
   150	/// in `expr` whose canonical form (ASCII uppercase) matches
   151	/// `old_canonical` to `SheetRef::Name(new_name)`. Used by
   152	/// `WorkbookRuntime::rename_sheet` to update stored formula text
   153	/// after a sheet rename, via a parse → rewrite → print round trip.
   154	///
   155	/// Returns a new Expr; the input is not mutated. `Current` and `Id`
   156	/// sheet refs pass through unchanged. Non-matching `Name` entries
   157	/// also pass through.
   158	pub fn rewrite_sheet_name_in_expr(expr: &Expr, old_canonical: &str, new_name: &Arc<str>) -> Expr {
   159	    match expr {
   160	        Expr::Number(n) => Expr::Number(*n),
   161	        Expr::String(s) => Expr::String(s.clone()),
   162	        Expr::Bool(b) => Expr::Bool(*b),
   163	        Expr::NameRef(n) => Expr::NameRef(n.clone()),
   164	        Expr::CellRef(addr) => Expr::CellRef(CellAddr {
   165	            sheet: rewrite_sheet_ref(&addr.sheet, old_canonical, new_name),
   166	            col: addr.col,
   167	            row: addr.row,
   168	            abs_col: addr.abs_col,
   169	            abs_row: addr.abs_row,
   170	        }),
   171	        Expr::RangeRef(r) => Expr::RangeRef(rewrite_range_ref(r, old_canonical, new_name)),
   172	        Expr::Binary { op, lhs, rhs } => Expr::Binary {
   173	            op: *op,
   174	            lhs: Box::new(rewrite_sheet_name_in_expr(lhs, old_canonical, new_name)),
   175	            rhs: Box::new(rewrite_sheet_name_in_expr(rhs, old_canonical, new_name)),
   176	        },
   177	        Expr::Unary { op, operand } => Expr::Unary {
   178	            op: *op,
   179	            operand: Box::new(rewrite_sheet_name_in_expr(operand, old_canonical, new_name)),
   180	        },
   181	        Expr::Function { name, args } => Expr::Function {
   182	            name: name.clone(),
   183	            args: args
   184	                .iter()
   185	                .map(|a| rewrite_sheet_name_in_expr(a, old_canonical, new_name))
   186	                .collect(),
   187	        },
   188	        Expr::Array(rows) => Expr::Array(
   189	            rows.iter()
   190	                .map(|row| {
   191	                    row.iter()
   192	                        .map(|cell| rewrite_sheet_name_in_expr(cell, old_canonical, new_name))
   193	                        .collect()
   194	                })
   195	                .collect(),
   196	        ),
   197	        Expr::Spill(inner) => Expr::Spill(Box::new(rewrite_sheet_name_in_expr(
   198	            inner,
   199	            old_canonical,
   200	            new_name,
   201	        ))),
   202	    }
   203	}
   204	
   205	fn rewrite_sheet_ref(sheet: &SheetRef, old_canonical: &str, new_name: &Arc<str>) -> SheetRef {
   206	    match sheet {
   207	        SheetRef::Name(n) if n.eq_ignore_ascii_case(old_canonical) => {
   208	            SheetRef::Name(new_name.clone())
   209	        }
   210	        other => other.clone(),
   211	    }
   212	}
   213	
   214	fn rewrite_range_ref(r: &RangeRef, old_canonical: &str, new_name: &Arc<str>) -> RangeRef {
   215	    match r {
   216	        RangeRef::Cells {
   217	            sheet,
   218	            start_col,
   219	            start_row,
   220	            end_col,
   221	            end_row,
   222	            abs_start_col,
   223	            abs_start_row,
   224	            abs_end_col,
   225	            abs_end_row,
   226	        } => RangeRef::Cells {
   227	            sheet: rewrite_sheet_ref(sheet, old_canonical, new_name),
   228	            start_col: *start_col,
   229	            start_row: *start_row,
   230	            end_col: *end_col,
   231	            end_row: *end_row,
   232	            abs_start_col: *abs_start_col,
   233	            abs_start_row: *abs_start_row,
   234	            abs_end_col: *abs_end_col,
   235	            abs_end_row: *abs_end_row,
   236	        },
   237	        RangeRef::WholeColumn {
   238	            sheet,
   239	            start_col,
   240	            end_col,
   241	            abs_start,
   242	            abs_end,
   243	        } => RangeRef::WholeColumn {
   244	            sheet: rewrite_sheet_ref(sheet, old_canonical, new_name),
   245	            start_col: *start_col,
   246	            end_col: *end_col,
   247	            abs_start: *abs_start,
   248	            abs_end: *abs_end,
   249	        },
   250	        RangeRef::WholeRow {
   251	            sheet,
   252	            start_row,
   253	            end_row,
   254	            abs_start,
   255	            abs_end,
   256	        } => RangeRef::WholeRow {
   257	            sheet: rewrite_sheet_ref(sheet, old_canonical, new_name),
   258	            start_row: *start_row,
   259	            end_row: *end_row,
   260	            abs_start: *abs_start,
   261	            abs_end: *abs_end,
   262	        },
   263	    }
   264	}
   265	
   266	/// Address inside an `Expr`. `sheet: SheetRef::Current` means "the
   267	/// formula's containing sheet" (resolved during binding). Sheet-
   268	/// qualified refs (`Sheet1!A1`, `'Q3 2025'!A1`) populate
   269	/// `SheetRef::Name(name)` until the binder resolves to `SheetRef::Id`.
   270	///
   271	/// **W5-87 (Phase 4.6.A part 1):** `Copy` dropped because `SheetRef`
   272	/// carries `Arc<str>` in the `Name` variant. Most existing callers
   273	/// switch to `.clone()` (Arc clone is cheap) or pass by reference.
   274	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
   275	pub struct CellAddr {
   276	    pub sheet: SheetRef,
   277	    pub col: ColId,
   278	    pub row: RowId,
   279	    pub abs_col: bool,
   280	    pub abs_row: bool,
   281	}
   282	
   283	/// Range reference inside an `Expr` — validated enum shape.
   284	///
   285	/// Per codex r13 N3: the prior `RangeRef` was an option-field bag where all-`None` and
   286	/// other ill-formed combinations were constructible. Amendment A4's structural graph-dump
   287	/// assertions explicitly need `SUM(A:A)` to compress to ONE whole-column variant. The
   288	/// option-bag couldn't pattern-match on "is this a whole-column range?" cleanly.
   289	///
   290	/// Three valid forms (matches Excel-canonical range types; sheet-qualification deferred):
   291	///   - `Cells { ... }` — bounded rectangular range `A1:B10`
   292	///   - `WholeColumn { ... }` — `A:A`, `A:C`, `$A:$D` (cols specified; rows are the full sheet)
   293	///   - `WholeRow { ... }` — `1:1`, `2:5`, `$3:$3` (rows specified; cols are the full sheet)
   294	///
   295	/// Same-sheet only in Phase 0.
   296	/// **W5-87 (Phase 4.6.A part 1):** `Copy` dropped — `SheetRef` carries
   297	/// `Arc<str>`. Same migration pattern as `CellAddr`.
   298	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
   299	pub enum RangeRef {
   300	    /// Bounded rectangular cell range. Start ≤ end on each axis (parser-normalized).
   301	    /// `sheet: SheetRef::Current` = the formula's containing sheet.
   302	    Cells {
   303	        sheet: SheetRef,
   304	        start_col: ColId,
   305	        start_row: RowId,
   306	        end_col: ColId,
   307	        end_row: RowId,
   308	        abs_start_col: bool,
   309	        abs_start_row: bool,
   310	        abs_end_col: bool,
   311	        abs_end_row: bool,
   312	    },
   313	    /// Whole-column range (e.g. `A:A`, `B:D`). Rows span the full sheet.
   314	    WholeColumn {
   315	        sheet: SheetRef,
   316	        start_col: ColId,
   317	        end_col: ColId,
   318	        abs_start: bool,
   319	        abs_end: bool,
   320	    },
   321	    /// Whole-row range (e.g. `1:1`, `2:5`). Cols span the full sheet.
   322	    WholeRow {
   323	        sheet: SheetRef,
   324	        start_row: RowId,
   325	        end_row: RowId,
   326	        abs_start: bool,
   327	        abs_end: bool,
   328	    },
   329	}

     1	//! Lexical tokens for the Quantbook formula language.
     2	//!
     3	//! Per spec Part V §4 Week 2 Days 5-6. **PHASE 0 MINIMUM** scope per audit recommendation:
     4	//! - Number literals (integer + decimal + exponent + trailing %)
     5	//! - String literals (`"..."` with `""` for embedded quote)
     6	//! - A1 cell refs (column letters + row digits, with `$` absolute markers)
     7	//! - Range refs (`A1:B10`, including infinite forms `A:A` / `1:1`)
     8	//! - Identifiers (function names + named refs — disambiguated at parser level)
     9	//! - Operators (`+ - * / % ^ & = <> < > <= >=`)
    10	//! - Punctuation (`( ) , : ;`)
    11	//!
    12	//! **Deferred to Phase 3+** (out of Phase 0 scope, would inflate the parser surface):
    13	//! - R1C1 references (`R1C1`, `R[1]C[1]`)
    14	//! - Sheet-qualified refs (`Sheet1!A1`, `'Sheet 1'!A1`)
    15	//! - Structured table refs (`Table1[Col]`, `Table1[@Col]`)
    16	//! - Cross-workbook refs (`[file.xlsx]Sheet1!A1`)
    17	//! - Array literal syntax `{1,2;3,4}`
    18	//!
    19	//! The `Token` enum is intentionally narrow — adding deferred surface later is non-breaking.
    20	//!
    21	//! **AI() reservation (CORR-06 / T4-D05) lives entirely at the parser level.** The lexer
    22	//! treats `AI` as a regular 2-letter column-letter identifier and emits `BareColumn { col,
    23	//! abs, text }` with `text = "AI"`. When the parser encounters `BareColumn`-or-`CellRef`
    24	//! immediately followed by `LParen`, it looks up the `text` field as a function name; if it
    25	//! matches `AI` (case-insensitive), the parser emits `Expr::Function { name: "AI", ... }`
    26	//! which the binder/evaluator then maps to `Error(ErrorValue::AINotAvailable)`. No special
    27	//! lexer-level reservation; the `text` field IS the disambiguation handle.
    28	
    29	use std::sync::Arc;
    30	
    31	/// A single lexed token. `Copy` for primitives; `Arc<str>` for the text-bearing variants.
    32	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
    33	pub enum Token {
    34	    /// Numeric literal (integer, decimal, exponent — already coerced to `f64`).
    35	    /// Audit L1 fix (2026-05-12): a trailing `%` is NOT folded into the literal at
    36	    /// lex time; the lexer emits a separate `Op(Percent)` token. The parser applies
    37	    /// percent semantics via `Unary { op: Percent, operand }` (postfix unary). To
    38	    /// match Excel canon more strictly, future Phase 2+ may fold `%` into the
    39	    /// literal at lex time; until then `50%` produces two tokens (Number(50.0),
    40	    /// Op(Percent)) and the parser produces Unary { Percent, Number(50.0) } which
    41	    /// evaluates to 0.5.
    42	    Number(f64),
    43	
    44	    /// String literal between `"..."` with `""` escape collapsed. The interior content only.
    45	    String(Arc<str>),
    46	
    47	    /// Identifier — function name (`SUM`, `AVERAGE`, `AI`) or named range. The parser
    48	    /// disambiguates based on the following token (`(` → function, otherwise → named ref).
    49	    Ident(Arc<str>),
    50	
    51	    /// A1-style cell reference. Stores zero-indexed `(col, row)` plus absolute-marker flags
    52	    /// AND the raw source text. The raw text is preserved so the parser can disambiguate
    53	    /// function calls: Excel-canonical, `LOG10(2)` lexes as `CellRef{col=8508, row=9,
    54	    /// text="LOG10"}` followed by `LParen` — the parser sees LParen and looks up `text` as
    55	    /// a function name. (Per codex r13 N6 + opus arch F6 audit; the lexer can't disambiguate
    56	    /// without lookahead beyond `(`, so the parser owns the dispatch.)
    57	    CellRef {
    58	        col: u32,
    59	        row: u32,
    60	        abs_col: bool,
    61	        abs_row: bool,
    62	        text: Arc<str>,
    63	    },
    64	
    65	    /// Open-axis cell reference for whole-column forms within a `Range` (e.g.  `A:A` lexes
    66	    /// as two `BareColumn` joined by `Colon`). Stores zero-indexed col, abs marker, and the
    67	    /// raw source text (for parser-level function-name disambiguation when followed by `(`).
    68	    BareColumn {
    69	        col: u32,
    70	        abs: bool,
    71	        text: Arc<str>,
    72	    },
    73	
    74	    /// Open-axis bare row for `1:1` forms — only meaningful inside a range. 0-indexed row
    75	    /// + abs marker (the `$` BEFORE the digits, e.g. `$5`).
    76	    BareRow {
    77	        row: u32,
    78	        abs: bool,
    79	    },
    80	
    81	    /// Operators — single tokens; the parser builds the precedence tree.
    82	    Op(Operator),
    83	
    84	    LParen,
    85	    RParen,
    86	    Comma,
    87	    Colon,
    88	    Semicolon,
    89	
    90	    /// **W5-88 (Phase 4.6.A part 2):** `!` separator between a sheet
    91	    /// name and a cell/range reference. Only meaningful immediately
    92	    /// after a `SheetName` / `QuotedSheetName` token; appearing
    93	    /// elsewhere is a parser error.
    94	    Bang,
    95	
    96	    /// **W5-88 (Phase 4.6.A part 2):** unquoted sheet name preceding `!`.
    97	    /// `[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_.]*` per design doc § 4.1. The lexer uses
    98	    /// one-token lookahead to disambiguate from `Ident`: only when the
    99	    /// next non-whitespace char is `!` does the run emit as
   100	    /// `SheetName + Bang` instead of an `Ident`. Stored as `Arc<str>`
   101	    /// for cheap clone-through-bind.
   102	    SheetName(Arc<str>),
   103	
   104	    /// **W5-88 (Phase 4.6.A part 2):** quoted sheet name `'...'` with
   105	    /// `''` → `'` escape decoded. Like `SheetName`, only emitted when
   106	    /// the closing quote is followed by `!` (skipping intervening
   107	    /// whitespace). The body is the decoded form (no surrounding quotes).
   108	    QuotedSheetName(Arc<str>),
   109	}
   110	
   111	/// Operator subtype carried by `Token::Op`.
   112	#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
   113	pub enum Operator {
   114	    Plus,    // +
   115	    Minus,   // -  (also unary)
   116	    Mul,     // *
   117	    Div,     // /
   118	    Percent, // %  (postfix unary; lex emits as Op for clean disambiguation in parser)
   119	    Pow,     // ^
   120	    Concat,  // &
   121	    Eq,      // =
   122	    Neq,     // <>
   123	    Lt,      // <
   124	    Le,      // <=
   125	    Gt,      // >
   126	    Ge,      // >=
   127	}
   128	
   129	impl Operator {
   130	    /// Stable text representation for AST printer / diagnostics.
   131	    pub fn as_str(self) -> &'static str {
   132	        match self {
   133	            Operator::Plus => "+",
   134	            Operator::Minus => "-",
   135	            Operator::Mul => "*",
   136	            Operator::Div => "/",
   137	            Operator::Percent => "%",
   138	            Operator::Pow => "^",
   139	            Operator::Concat => "&",
   140	            Operator::Eq => "=",
   141	            Operator::Neq => "<>",
   142	            Operator::Lt => "<",
   143	            Operator::Le => "<=",
   144	            Operator::Gt => ">",
   145	            Operator::Ge => ">=",
   146	        }
   147	    }
   148	}
   149

   151	mod tests {
   152	    use super::*;

   157	        assert_eq!(Operator::Minus.as_str(), "-");
   158	        assert_eq!(Operator::Mul.as_str(), "*");
   159	        assert_eq!(Operator::Div.as_str(), "/");
   160	        assert_eq!(Operator::Percent.as_str(), "%");
   161	        assert_eq!(Operator::Pow.as_str(), "^");
   162	        assert_eq!(Operator::Concat.as_str(), "&");
   163	        assert_eq!(Operator::Eq.as_str(), "=");
   164	        assert_eq!(Operator::Neq.as_str(), "<>");
   165	        assert_eq!(Operator::Lt.as_str(), "<");

   167	        assert_eq!(Operator::Gt.as_str(), ">");
   168	        assert_eq!(Operator::Ge.as_str(), ">=");

   175	        assert_eq!(a, b);
   176	        assert_ne!(Token::Number(1.5), Token::Number(2.5));

   311	    pub fn sheet_count(&self) -> usize {

   313	    }
   314	
   315	    // ===== Phase 4.6.AA (W5-86): sheet registry + canonicalizer =====
   316	
   317	    /// **Phase 4.6.AA (W5-86):** canonicalize a sheet name for
   318	    /// case-insensitive comparison. ASCII uppercase per the parent
   319	    /// design doc § 7.3 (consistency with `NameTable::set`'s rule;
   320	    /// Unicode casefolding deferred to a follow-up gap).
   321	    ///
   322	    /// Excel canonical: sheet names compare case-insensitively (so
   323	    /// `"Sheet1"` and `"SHEET1"` are the same sheet) but the DISPLAY
   324	    /// form preserves user-supplied case. This function returns the
   325	    /// canonical form used for lookup / duplicate-detection ONLY;
   326	    /// display goes through `Sheet::name()`.
   327	    ///
   328	    /// V1 simplification: ASCII-only uppercase. `"Café"` and `"café"`
   329	    /// canonicalize to different forms (the non-ASCII `é` is not
   330	    /// folded). Excel itself uses Unicode casefolding here; if a real
   331	    /// workbook with non-ASCII sheet names surfaces a collision, this
   332	    /// gets revisited.
   333	    pub fn canonical_sheet_name(name: &str) -> String {
   334	        name.to_ascii_uppercase()
   335	    }
   336	
   337	    /// **Phase 4.6.AA (W5-86):** find a sheet by name (case-insensitive
   338	    /// per `canonical_sheet_name`). Returns the first match in id order.
   339	    ///
   340	    /// Lookup cost: O(sheet_count). Acceptable for typical workbooks
   341	    /// (low double-digits of sheets). If profiling shows pain, a
   342	    /// canonical-name → id index can be added without changing this
   343	    /// API surface.
   344	    pub fn sheet_id_by_name(&self, name: &str) -> Option<SheetId> {
   345	        let canonical = Self::canonical_sheet_name(name);
   346	        for (idx, sheet) in self.sheets.iter().enumerate() {
   347	            if Self::canonical_sheet_name(sheet.name()) == canonical {
   348	                return Some(idx as SheetId);
   349	            }
   350	        }
   351	        None
   352	    }
   353	
   354	    /// **Phase 4.6.C (W5-91):** rename a sheet by id. Validates the new
   355	    /// name via [`validate_sheet_name`] (rejects empty, Excel-reserved
   356	    /// chars, canonical duplicates) and swaps the sheet's display name.
   357	    ///
   358	    /// **DOES NOT touch formula text.** Stored formulas in
   359	    /// `formula_cells` and `NamedTarget::Formula` entries continue to
   360	    /// reference the OLD sheet name. The runtime wrapper
   361	    /// `WorkbookRuntime::rename_sheet` (`ql-exec`) owns the
   362	    /// tokenization-aware text rewrite + op-log recording per the
   363	    /// parent design doc § 3.1. Direct callers of this method (loader,
   364	    /// tests, op-log replay) get bare name swap only.
   365	    ///
   366	    /// **Phase 4.6 design doc § 3 (Codex HIGH-1 closure):** the swap is
   367	    /// fast (O(1) on the sheet's `name` field); the expensive text
   368	    /// rewrite is the caller's responsibility and is bounded by the
   369	    /// workbook's total formula text size.
   370	    pub fn rename_sheet(
   371	        &mut self,
   372	        id: SheetId,
   373	        new_name: impl Into<String>,
   374	    ) -> Result<(), SheetNameError> {
   375	        let new_name: String = new_name.into();
   376	        // Validate first. If the new name canonically matches the CURRENT
   377	        // sheet name, that's a no-op rename (case-only change is allowed
   378	        // as a display update; duplicate check rejects all other matches).
   379	        let current_canonical = self
   380	            .sheet(id)
   381	            .map(|s| Self::canonical_sheet_name(s.name()))
   382	            .ok_or_else(|| SheetNameError::Duplicate {
   383	                name: format!("<sheet id {id} not found>"),
   384	            })?;
   385	        let new_canonical = Self::canonical_sheet_name(&new_name);
   386	        if new_canonical != current_canonical {
   387	            // Full validation only when canonical name actually changes.
   388	            self.validate_sheet_name(&new_name)?;
   389	        } else if new_name.is_empty() {
   390	            return Err(SheetNameError::Empty);
   391	        }
   392	        // Swap the name. `Sheet::set_name` is private; mutate through
   393	        // sheet_mut + a new accessor.
   394	        if let Some(sheet) = self.sheets.get_mut(id as usize) {
   395	            sheet.set_name(new_name);
   396	        }
   397	        Ok(())
   398	    }
   399	
   400	    /// **Phase 4.6.AA (W5-86):** validate a sheet name for use with
   401	    /// `add_sheet` / `rename_sheet`. Rejects:
   402	    /// - empty string (Excel canon)
   403	    /// - canonical duplicate of an existing sheet name
   404	    /// - Excel-reserved characters `:`, `\`, `/`, `?`, `*`, `[`, `]`
   405	    ///   (also xlsx round-trip prerequisite for Phase 4.11)
   406	    ///
   407	    /// Per the parent design's named-divergence catalog: V1 matches
   408	    /// Excel's rejection set exactly. Length limit (Excel's 31-char
   409	    /// max) is NOT enforced in V1 — that's a UX hint, not a correctness
   410	    /// boundary; tracked for follow-up if xlsx import surfaces it.
   411	    pub fn validate_sheet_name(&self, name: &str) -> Result<(), SheetNameError> {
   412	        if name.is_empty() {
   413	            return Err(SheetNameError::Empty);
   414	        }
   415	        for c in name.chars() {
   416	            if matches!(c, ':' | '\\' | '/' | '?' | '*' | '[' | ']') {
   417	                return Err(SheetNameError::ReservedCharacter(c));
   418	            }
   419	        }
   420	        if self.sheet_id_by_name(name).is_some() {
   421	            return Err(SheetNameError::Duplicate {
   422	                name: name.to_owned(),
   423	            });
   424	        }
   425	        Ok(())
   426	    }

   455	    /// Append a new sheet; returns its `SheetId`. Panics if the next ID would exceed
   456	    /// `SheetId::MAX` (65,535). Excel allows ≤255 sheets in practice; we cap at the type
   457	    /// limit so the ID always fits the field.
   458	    ///
   459	    /// **Phase 2B.5 (2026-05-12) — LOW-LEVEL.** Bypasses the op log
   460	    /// silently — use `ql_exec::WorkbookRuntime::add_sheet` to record the
   461	    /// sheet creation in an attached `OpLog`. Tracked as GAP-O-02 in
   462	    /// `docs/known-gaps.md`.
   463	    pub fn add_sheet(&mut self, name: impl Into<String>) -> SheetId {
   464	        let id = self.sheets.len();
   465	        assert!(
   466	            id < SheetId::MAX as usize,
   467	            "too many sheets (max {}, current {})",
   468	            SheetId::MAX as usize,
   469	            id
   470	        );
   471	        self.sheets.push(Sheet::new(name));
   472	        id as SheetId
   473	    }

   480	    /// **Phase 2B.5 (2026-05-12) — LOW-LEVEL.** Bypasses the op log. Use

   485	    pub fn add_sheet_with_chunk_rows(

   507	
   508	    /// Read by `Address`.
   509	    ///
   510	    /// - **Missing sheet** (sheet id ≥ `sheet_count()`) → `Value::Error(Ref)`
   511	    ///   (Excel canon: `#REF!`).
   512	    /// - **Within-sheet missing row/col** → `Value::Blank` (Excel canon: empty
   513	    ///   cell coerces to 0 / "" / FALSE in arithmetic / text / logical
   514	    ///   contexts).
   515	    ///
   516	    /// Phase 2A.13 audit cycle-3 M2: the prior implementation returned `Blank`
   517	    /// for missing-sheet too, conflating "sheet deleted" with "empty cell."
   518	    /// The Phase 2A.7 H6 fix corrected this at the evaluator layer
   519	    /// (`ql-exec::WorkbookEnv::read_cell`); this commit aligns the storage
   520	    /// layer's public read API. Any host code that bypasses `WorkbookEnv`
   521	    /// (CLI tools, IDE inspectors, tests) now sees the same `#REF!` semantic.
   522	    pub fn read(&self, addr: Address) -> Value {
   523	        match self.sheet(addr.sheet) {
   524	            Some(s) => s.read(addr.row, addr.col),
   525	            None => Value::Error(ErrorValue::Ref),
   526	        }
   527	    }
   528	
   529	    /// Write by `Address`. Panics if `addr.sheet` is out of bounds (callers must `add_sheet`
   530	    /// first; this avoids implicit-sheet-creation silent bugs).
   531	    pub fn put(&mut self, addr: Address, value: Value) {
   532	        // Capture sheet_count before the mut-borrow so the panic path can use it.
   533	        let total = self.sheet_count();
   534	        match self.sheet_mut(addr.sheet) {
   535	            Some(s) => s.put(addr.row, addr.col, value),
   536	            None => panic!(
   537	                "Workbook::put: sheet {} does not exist (have {})",
   538	                addr.sheet, total
   539	            ),
   540	        }
   541	    }
   542	
   543	    /// Convenience: write a literal row,col,value tuple without constructing an Address.
   544	    /// Phase 3.5: this is the USER-edit path — routes to `Sheet::put` →
   545	    /// `ColumnStore::put` which writes the user overlay AND clears any stale computed
   546	    /// entry at the same cell.
   547	    ///
   548	    /// **Phase 2B.5 (2026-05-12) — LOW-LEVEL.** Bypasses the op log AND the
   549	    /// runtime's bind-plan cache; does NOT clear any pre-existing formula
   550	    /// at the cell (use `clear_formula` separately if needed). Product code
   551	    /// should route through `ql_exec::WorkbookRuntime::set_value`, which
   552	    /// records `Op::PutValue` + an optional `Op::ClearFormula`. Direct
   553	    /// `put_at` is for the qbook loader, the op-log replay path, runtime-
   554	    /// internal pass 2 of formula recompute, and tests. GAP-O-03.
   555	    pub fn put_at(&mut self, sheet: SheetId, row: RowId, col: ColId, value: Value) {
   556	        self.put(Address::new(sheet, row, col), value);
   557	    }

   560	    /// `(sheet, row, col)`. Routes to the computed-overlay lane (NOT the user lane).

     1	//! Excel-formula lexer. Phase 0 minimum surface (see `token.rs` docs for scope).
     2	//!
     3	//! Produces a flat `Vec<Token>` from a formula source string. The caller (parser) handles
     4	//! precedence + AST building.
     5	//!
     6	//! Source convention: the leading `=` of an Excel formula is NOT consumed by the lexer — the
     7	//! caller strips it (`s.strip_prefix('=').unwrap_or(s)`). This keeps the lexer reusable for
     8	//! sub-expressions and criteria strings.
     9	
    10	use std::iter::Peekable;
    11	use std::str::Chars;
    12	use std::sync::Arc;
    13	
    14	use crate::token::{Operator, Token};
    15	
    16	/// Lex error — narrow, structured.
    17	///
    18	/// Phase 2A.11 audit M16 (2026-05-12): switched from hand-rolled Display +
    19	/// std::error::Error to `thiserror::Error` for consistency with QbookError,
    20	/// RuntimeError, NameTableError (all already use thiserror). Display strings
    21	/// are user-facing and identical to the prior hand-rolled formatters.
    22	/// (Phase 2B.2 removed `LoadAndRecomputeError`; recompute failures are now
    23	/// aggregated in `RecomputeResult` rather than surfaced as a wrapping enum.)
    24	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, thiserror::Error)]
    25	pub enum LexError {
    26	    #[error("unterminated string literal")]
    27	    UnterminatedString,
    28	
    29	    #[error("invalid number: {0:?}")]
    30	    InvalidNumber(String),
    31	
    32	    #[error("unexpected character: {0:?}")]
    33	    UnexpectedChar(char),
    34	
    35	    /// Cell-ref or column letters exceed the Excel column-letter max (XFD = 16383). The
    36	    /// constructor enforces this even though our `ColId = u32` could technically hold more.
    37	    #[error("column letters out of range: {0:?}")]
    38	    ColumnTooLarge(String),
    39	
    40	    /// Row digits exceed the Excel row max (1048576). Same enforcement reason.
    41	    #[error("row digits out of range: {0:?}")]
    42	    RowTooLarge(String),
    43	
    44	    /// **W5-88 (Phase 4.6.A part 2):** a `'...'`-style quoted sheet name
    45	    /// was opened but never closed before end-of-input.
    46	    #[error("unterminated quoted sheet name")]
    47	    UnterminatedQuotedSheetName,
    48	
    49	    /// **W5-88 (Phase 4.6.A part 2):** a `'...'` block was closed but not
    50	    /// followed by `!`. Bare quoted strings have no other lexical role
    51	    /// in Excel formulas — this is a clean syntax error.
    52	    #[error("quoted string not followed by '!' — only valid as a sheet-name prefix")]
    53	    DanglingQuotedString,
    54	}
    55	
    56	/// Excel's column-letter upper bound (XFD = 16383, zero-indexed).
    57	pub const MAX_COLUMN: u32 = 16_383;
    58	/// Excel's row upper bound (1,048,576 — 1-indexed in source, so max 0-indexed = 1,048,575).
    59	pub const MAX_ROW: u32 = 1_048_575;
    60	
    61	/// Tokenize a formula expression body (without the leading `=`).
    62	pub fn lex(input: &str) -> Result<Vec<Token>, LexError> {
    63	    let mut out = Vec::new();
    64	    let mut chars = input.chars().peekable();
    65	
    66	    while let Some(&c) = chars.peek() {
    67	        // Skip whitespace — Excel only treats ASCII space/tab/LF/CR as whitespace between
    68	        // tokens (NBSP, vertical-tab, BOM, etc. are NOT whitespace in Excel formulas).
    69	        // Per opus arch F14: `char::is_whitespace` is Unicode-defined and would silently
    70	        // accept clipboard-paste NBSP; we lex strictly per Excel canon.
    71	        if matches!(c, ' ' | '\t' | '\n' | '\r') {
    72	            chars.next();
    73	            continue;
    74	        }
    75	
    76	        match c {
    77	            '(' => {
    78	                chars.next();
    79	                out.push(Token::LParen);
    80	            }
    81	            ')' => {
    82	                chars.next();
    83	                out.push(Token::RParen);
    84	            }
    85	            ',' => {
    86	                chars.next();
    87	                out.push(Token::Comma);
    88	            }
    89	            ':' => {
    90	                chars.next();
    91	                out.push(Token::Colon);
    92	            }
    93	            ';' => {
    94	                chars.next();
    95	                out.push(Token::Semicolon);
    96	            }
    97	            '+' => {
    98	                chars.next();
    99	                out.push(Token::Op(Operator::Plus));
   100	            }
   101	            '-' => {
   102	                chars.next();
   103	                out.push(Token::Op(Operator::Minus));
   104	            }
   105	            '*' => {
   106	                chars.next();
   107	                out.push(Token::Op(Operator::Mul));
   108	            }
   109	            '/' => {
   110	                chars.next();
   111	                out.push(Token::Op(Operator::Div));
   112	            }
   113	            '%' => {
   114	                chars.next();
   115	                out.push(Token::Op(Operator::Percent));
   116	            }
   117	            '^' => {
   118	                chars.next();
   119	                out.push(Token::Op(Operator::Pow));
   120	            }
   121	            '&' => {
   122	                chars.next();
   123	                out.push(Token::Op(Operator::Concat));
   124	            }
   125	            '=' => {
   126	                chars.next();
   127	                out.push(Token::Op(Operator::Eq));
   128	            }
   129	            '<' => {
   130	                chars.next();
   131	                match chars.peek() {
   132	                    Some('>') => {
   133	                        chars.next();
   134	                        out.push(Token::Op(Operator::Neq));
   135	                    }
   136	                    Some('=') => {
   137	                        chars.next();
   138	                        out.push(Token::Op(Operator::Le));
   139	                    }
   140	                    _ => out.push(Token::Op(Operator::Lt)),
   141	                }
   142	            }
   143	            '>' => {
   144	                chars.next();
   145	                match chars.peek() {
   146	                    Some('=') => {
   147	                        chars.next();
   148	                        out.push(Token::Op(Operator::Ge));
   149	                    }
   150	                    _ => out.push(Token::Op(Operator::Gt)),
   151	                }
   152	            }
   153	            '"' => out.push(lex_string(&mut chars)?),
   154	            '0'..='9' | '.' => out.push(lex_number(&mut chars)?),
   155	            // **W5-88 (Phase 4.6.A part 2):** quoted sheet name `'...'`.
   156	            // Single-quote has no other lexical role in Excel formulas;
   157	            // bare/unclosed/non-prefix `'...'` surfaces as a clean lex
   158	            // error.
   159	            '\'' => {
   160	                lex_quoted_sheet_name(&mut chars, &mut out)?;
   161	            }
   162	            // **W5-88 (Phase 4.6.A part 2):** unquoted sheet-name prefix
   163	            // must be detected BEFORE the A1/function/cell-ref classifier
   164	            // per Codex MEDIUM-1: `A!B1` lexes sheet `A`, not bare column
   165	            // `A`; `A1!B1` lexes sheet `A1`, not cell `A1`. The check
   166	            // uses a cheap multi-char peek (Chars::clone) to avoid
   167	            // committing unless a real `!` follows.
   168	            'A'..='Z' | 'a'..='z' | '_' if try_lex_sheet_name_prefix(&mut chars, &mut out)? => {
   169	                // Token(s) emitted by try_lex_sheet_name_prefix when it
   170	                // returns true. Fall through to next loop iteration.
   171	            }
   172	            '$' | 'A'..='Z' | 'a'..='z' | '_' => out.push(lex_ident_or_ref(&mut chars)?),
   173	            other => return Err(LexError::UnexpectedChar(other)),
   174	        }
   175	    }
   176	
   177	    Ok(out)
   178	}
   179	
   180	fn lex_string(chars: &mut Peekable<Chars>) -> Result<Token, LexError> {
   181	    chars.next(); // consume opening quote
   182	    let mut s = String::new();
   183	    loop {
   184	        match chars.next() {
   185	            None => return Err(LexError::UnterminatedString),
   186	            Some('"') => {
   187	                // Doubled "" → embedded literal quote; otherwise end of string.
   188	                if chars.peek() == Some(&'"') {
   189	                    chars.next();
   190	                    s.push('"');
   191	                } else {
   192	                    return Ok(Token::String(Arc::from(s)));
   193	                }
   194	            }
   195	            Some(c) => s.push(c),
   196	        }
   197	    }
   198	}
   199	
   200	fn lex_number(chars: &mut Peekable<Chars>) -> Result<Token, LexError> {
   201	    let mut raw = String::new();
   202	    while let Some(&c) = chars.peek() {
   203	        match c {
   204	            '0'..='9' | '.' => {
   205	                raw.push(c);
   206	                chars.next();
   207	            }
   208	            'e' | 'E' => {
   209	                raw.push(c);
   210	                chars.next();
   211	                // optional sign immediately after exponent
   212	                if let Some(&sign) = chars.peek() {
   213	                    if sign == '+' || sign == '-' {
   214	                        raw.push(sign);
   215	                        chars.next();
   216	                    }
   217	                }
   218	            }
   219	            _ => break,
   220	        }
   221	    }
   222	
   223	    let n: f64 = raw
   224	        .parse()
   225	        .map_err(|_| LexError::InvalidNumber(raw.clone()))?;
   226	    if n.is_nan() || n.is_infinite() {
   227	        return Err(LexError::InvalidNumber(raw));
   228	    }
   229	    Ok(Token::Number(n))
   230	}
   231	
   232	/// Lex either an identifier (function name / named ref) OR an A1 cell reference / bare-axis
   233	/// reference. Source-shape grammar:
   234	///   `[$]LETTERS[$]DIGITS` → CellRef
   235	///   `[$]LETTERS`           → BareColumn (column letters with no row)
   236	///   `[$]DIGITS`            → BareRow (row digits alone; only meaningful inside a range)
   237	///   `_letters_digits…`     → Identifier (Ident / Word, parser disambiguates against `(`)
   238	///
   239	/// Tracks `leading_dollar` (the `$` before letters/digits) and `mid_dollar` (the `$` between
   240	/// letters and digits) SEPARATELY so each propagates correctly to the abs markers.
   241	/// Prior bug (codex r13 N5 / opus arch F1 / opus consistency B-2): pushing the leading `$`
   242	/// into the letter buffer caused `$5` to lex as `BareRow { abs: false }` — silently wrong.
   243	fn lex_ident_or_ref(chars: &mut Peekable<Chars>) -> Result<Token, LexError> {
   244	    // Track the leading `$` separately — NEVER push into the letter buffer.
   245	    let mut leading_dollar = false;
   246	    if chars.peek() == Some(&'$') {
   247	        leading_dollar = true;
   248	        chars.next();
   249	    }
   250	
   251	    // Letter prefix: ASCII letters and underscore.
   252	    let mut letters = String::new();
   253	    while let Some(&c) = chars.peek() {
   254	        if c.is_ascii_alphabetic() || c == '_' {
   255	            letters.push(c);
   256	            chars.next();
   257	        } else {
   258	            break;
   259	        }
   260	    }
   261	
   262	    // Phase 2A.5 (2026-05-12): dotted identifiers like `VAR.S`, `STDEV.P`. If we
   263	    // see `.LETTERS`, treat as a continuation of the identifier — this commits
   264	    // the token to the Ident path (no CellRef / BareColumn / BareRow
   265	    // interpretation after a dot, since Excel doesn't have dotted cell refs).
   266	    // An orphan dot (not followed by an ASCII letter) is a lex error here, not
   267	    // a separate token: we've already entered identifier-lex mode, and Excel
   268	    // canon doesn't put bare dots in identifier position.
   269	    //
   270	    // The `while` loop allows multi-dot patterns (e.g. `A.B.C`) for forward
   271	    // compatibility — Excel only uses single-dot today, but the parser/binder
   272	    // will reject unknown function names regardless of dot count.
   273	    let mut has_dot = false;
   274	    while chars.peek() == Some(&'.') {
   275	        // Commit to consuming the `.` only when a letter follows. We peek twice
   276	        // by cloning the iterator (cheap — Chars holds a single &str slice).
   277	        let mut lookahead = chars.clone();
   278	        lookahead.next(); // skip the `.` in the cloned view
   279	        let next_is_letter = lookahead.peek().is_some_and(|c| c.is_ascii_alphabetic());
   280	        if !next_is_letter {
   281	            // Bare dot in identifier position (or end-of-input). Reject loudly.
   282	            return Err(LexError::UnexpectedChar('.'));
   283	        }
   284	        chars.next(); // consume the `.` for real now
   285	        letters.push('.');
   286	        has_dot = true;
   287	        while let Some(&c) = chars.peek() {
   288	            if c.is_ascii_alphabetic() || c == '_' {
   289	                letters.push(c);
   290	                chars.next();
   291	            } else {
   292	                break;
   293	            }
   294	        }
   295	    }
   296	
   297	    // Phase 2A.5: once we've absorbed a dot, the token is unambiguously an
   298	    // Ident. Cell-ref syntax (digits / extra `$`) isn't legal after a dot, and
   299	    // leading `$` on an identifier isn't either. Reject these up front so the
   300	    // error points at the offending character, not at downstream parse mismatch.
   301	    if has_dot {
   302	        if leading_dollar {
   303	            return Err(LexError::UnexpectedChar('$'));
   304	        }
   305	        if chars.peek() == Some(&'$') {
   306	            return Err(LexError::UnexpectedChar('$'));
   307	        }
   308	        if chars.peek().is_some_and(|c| c.is_ascii_digit()) {
   309	            let bad = chars.peek().copied().unwrap();
   310	            return Err(LexError::UnexpectedChar(bad));
   311	        }
   312	        return Ok(Token::Ident(Arc::from(letters)));
   313	    }
   314	
   315	    // Optional `$` between letters and digits (row-absolute marker).
   316	    let mut mid_dollar = false;
   317	    if chars.peek() == Some(&'$') {
   318	        mid_dollar = true;
   319	        chars.next();
   320	    }
   321	
   322	    // If digits follow, we're committed to a CellRef / BareRow form.
   323	    if chars.peek().is_some_and(|c| c.is_ascii_digit()) {
   324	        let mut row_digits = String::new();
   325	        while let Some(&d) = chars.peek() {
   326	            if d.is_ascii_digit() {
   327	                row_digits.push(d);
   328	                chars.next();
   329	            } else {
   330	                break;
   331	            }
   332	        }
   333	
   334	        let row_1based: u32 = row_digits
   335	            .parse()
   336	            .map_err(|_| LexError::RowTooLarge(row_digits.clone()))?;
   337	        if row_1based == 0 {
   338	            return Err(LexError::InvalidNumber(format!(
   339	                "row 0 not allowed: {row_digits}"
   340	            )));
   341	        }
   342	        let row = row_1based - 1;
   343	        if row > MAX_ROW {
   344	            return Err(LexError::RowTooLarge(row_digits));
   345	        }
   346	
   347	        if letters.is_empty() {
   348	            // Pure `[$]<digits>` → BareRow. Excel allows this only inside a range. The
   349	            // absolute marker is the `leading_dollar` (the `$` that came BEFORE the digits).
   350	            // A `mid_dollar` here would mean the source was `$$5` which is invalid in Excel.
   351	            if mid_dollar {
   352	                return Err(LexError::UnexpectedChar('$'));
   353	            }
   354	            return Ok(Token::BareRow {
   355	                row,
   356	                abs: leading_dollar,
   357	            });
   358	        }
   359	
   360	        // letters + digits → CellRef. The raw `text` field is preserved so the parser can
   361	        // disambiguate function calls (`LOG10(2)` → CellRef + LParen; parser looks up text
   362	        // as a function name on encountering LParen). Excel-canonical: lexer emits CellRef
   363	        // for `[col-letters][row-digits]`; parser overrides when `(` follows.
   364	        let raw_text = format!(
   365	            "{}{}{}{}",
   366	            if leading_dollar { "$" } else { "" },
   367	            letters,
   368	            if mid_dollar { "$" } else { "" },
   369	            row_digits
   370	        );
   371	        let parts = classify_letter_prefix_simple(&letters)?;
   372	        Ok(Token::CellRef {
   373	            col: parts.col,
   374	            row,
   375	            abs_col: leading_dollar,
   376	            abs_row: mid_dollar,
   377	            text: Arc::from(raw_text),
   378	        })
   379	    } else {
   380	        // No trailing digits — either an identifier or a bare column.
   381	        // `mid_dollar = true` here means we saw `<letters>$<non-digit>` — invalid in Excel.
   382	        if mid_dollar {
   383	            return Err(LexError::UnexpectedChar('$'));
   384	        }
   385	        if letters.is_empty() {
   386	            // `$` alone with nothing after.
   387	            return Err(LexError::UnexpectedChar('$'));
   388	        }
   389	        // Try column-letter interpretation (length 1-3, all ASCII alpha, value ≤ XFD).
   390	        let raw_text = if leading_dollar {
   391	            format!("${letters}")
   392	        } else {
   393	            letters.clone()
   394	        };
   395	        if letters.chars().all(|c| c.is_ascii_alphabetic()) && letters.len() <= 3 {
   396	            // Try as column letters. If it fits, emit BareColumn; otherwise treat as Ident.
   397	            if let Ok(col) = column_letters_to_index(&letters) {
   398	                return Ok(Token::BareColumn {
   399	                    col,
   400	                    abs: leading_dollar,
   401	                    text: Arc::from(raw_text),
   402	                });
   403	            }
   404	        }
   405	        // Identifier path. Identifiers cannot carry a `$` prefix in Excel.
   406	        if leading_dollar {
   407	            return Err(LexError::UnexpectedChar('$'));
   408	        }
   409	        Ok(Token::Ident(Arc::from(letters)))
   410	    }
   411	}
   412	
   413	/// Simple classifier — letters-only (no `$` prefix; that's tracked separately by the caller).
   414	/// Returns the column index for valid 1-3 letter column-letter sequences; errors for too-long
   415	/// or non-alpha. The caller has already filtered out the `$` prefix.
   416	struct LetterColumn {
   417	    col: u32,
   418	}
   419	
   420	fn classify_letter_prefix_simple(letters: &str) -> Result<LetterColumn, LexError> {

exec

     1	//! Pratt parser — Phase B per CORR-20.
     2	//!
     3	//! Turns a `Vec<Token>` from the lexer into an `Expr` AST. Excel-canonical operator
     4	//! precedence (low → high):
     5	//!
     6	//! ```text
     7	//! comparison (=, <>, <, >, <=, >=)      bp 10/11 — left-assoc
     8	//! concat (&)                            bp 20/21
     9	//! addition (+, -)                       bp 30/31
    10	//! multiplication (*, /)                 bp 40/41
    11	//! power (^)                             bp 51/50 — right-assoc
    12	//! percent (postfix %)                   bp 60   — postfix unary
    13	//! unary prefix (-x, +x)                 bp 70
    14	//! range (:)                             bp 80/81 — highest
    15	//! function call / atomic                terminal
    16	//! ```
    17	//!
    18	//! ## Function-call disambiguation
    19	//!
    20	//! Per CORR-13 (lexer audit fix R7), function names are NOT lexer-level keywords. The
    21	//! lexer emits `CellRef { ..., text: "LOG10" }` and `BareColumn { ..., text: "AI" }`
    22	//! preserving the source text. The parser uses the `text` field for function-name
    23	//! lookup when the very next token is `LParen`:
    24	//!
    25	//! - `Ident("SUM")` + `LParen` → function call with name "SUM"
    26	//! - `CellRef { text: "LOG10", ... }` + `LParen` → function call with name "LOG10"
    27	//!   (NOT a cell-ref; precedence: `LParen` follows wins)
    28	//! - `BareColumn { text: "AI", ... }` + `LParen` → function call with name "AI"
    29	//!
    30	//! All function names are uppercased to canonical form (Excel convention).
    31	//!
    32	//! ## AI() reservation (CORR-06 / T4-D05)
    33	//!
    34	//! After uppercase normalization, function name `"AI"` emits an ordinary
    35	//! `Expr::Function { name: "AI", args }` AST node. The binder / evaluator maps that
    36	//! function dispatch to `Error(ErrorValue::AINotAvailable)` (the `#AI_NOT_AVAILABLE_V1`
    37	//! sigil). No special-case handling at parser level beyond name preservation.
    38	
    39	use std::sync::Arc;
    40	
    41	use crate::ast::{CellAddr, Expr, RangeRef, SheetRef};
    42	use crate::token::{Operator, Token};
    43	
    44	/// Parse error variants.
    45	///
    46	/// Phase 2A.11 audit M16 (2026-05-12): switched to `thiserror::Error` from
    47	/// hand-rolled Display + std::error::Error. Strings identical to prior
    48	/// hand-rolled formatters; consistency with the other error types.
    49	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, thiserror::Error)]
    50	pub enum ParseError {
    51	    /// Reached end of token stream while expecting more input.
    52	    #[error("unexpected end of input in {context}")]
    53	    UnexpectedEnd { context: &'static str },
    54	    /// A specific token type was expected but a different one was found.
    55	    #[error("unexpected token in {context}: {got}")]
    56	    Unexpected { context: &'static str, got: String },
    57	    /// Range bounds malformed (e.g. `A1:B10:C100` or unmatched range types like `A1:B`).
    58	    #[error("invalid range: {detail}")]
    59	    InvalidRange { detail: &'static str },
    60	    /// Trailing tokens after a complete expression.
    61	    #[error("trailing {count} token(s) after expression")]
    62	    Trailing { count: usize },
    63	    /// Unclosed function call or grouping.
    64	    #[error("unclosed {what}")]
    65	    UnclosedDelimiter { what: &'static str },
    66	
    67	    /// **W5-89 (Phase 4.6.A part 3):** `Sheet!` with no following ref
    68	    /// (end-of-input or a non-reference token).
    69	    #[error("dangling sheet qualifier — '{name}!' must be followed by a cell or range reference")]
    70	    DanglingBang { name: String },
    71	
    72	    /// **W5-89 (Phase 4.6.A part 3):** `Sheet1!Sheet2!X` — chained
    73	    /// sheet qualifiers. Cross-sheet refs in Excel are flat; only ONE
    74	    /// `Sheet!` per reference.
    75	    #[error("double sheet qualifier — only one 'Sheet!' is allowed per reference")]
    76	    DoubleSheetQualifier,
    77	
    78	    /// **W5-89 (Phase 4.6.A part 3):** `Sheet1!A1:Sheet2!B2` —
    79	    /// mixed-sheet endpoint range. Excel canon: the prefix applies to
    80	    /// the whole range; specifying it on BOTH endpoints with different
    81	    /// sheets is invalid.
    82	    #[error("mixed-sheet range endpoints — both endpoints must reference the same sheet")]
    83	    MixedSheetRangeEndpoints,
    84	
    85	    /// **W5-89 (Phase 4.6.A part 3):** any non-reference term after a
    86	    /// sheet qualifier — e.g. `Sheet1!SUM` (Function), `Sheet1!"x"`
    87	    /// (literal), `Sheet1!(A1)` (grouping).
    88	    #[error("sheet qualifier must be followed by a cell or range reference, got {got}")]
    89	    SheetQualifierFollowedByNonReference { got: String },
    90	}
    91	
    92	/// Parse a complete formula from `tokens` (lexer output) to an AST.
    93	pub fn parse(tokens: Vec<Token>) -> Result<Expr, ParseError> {
    94	    let mut p = Parser::new(tokens);
    95	    let e = p.parse_expr(0)?;
    96	    if p.pos < p.tokens.len() {
    97	        return Err(ParseError::Trailing {
    98	            count: p.tokens.len() - p.pos,
    99	        });
   100	    }
   101	    Ok(e)
   102	}
   103	
   104	struct Parser {
   105	    tokens: Vec<Token>,
   106	    pos: usize,
   107	}
   108	
   109	impl Parser {
   110	    fn new(tokens: Vec<Token>) -> Self {
   111	        Self { tokens, pos: 0 }
   112	    }
   113	
   114	    fn peek(&self) -> Option<&Token> {
   115	        self.tokens.get(self.pos)
   116	    }
   117	
   118	    fn advance(&mut self) -> Option<Token> {
   119	        let t = self.tokens.get(self.pos).cloned();
   120	        if t.is_some() {
   121	            self.pos += 1;
   122	        }
   123	        t
   124	    }
   125	
   126	    /// Pratt main loop. `min_bp` is the right-binding-power required to continue
   127	    /// consuming infix operators.
   128	    #[allow(clippy::while_let_loop)] // Loop body has multi-branch control flow
   129	                                     // (postfix/range/binary/terminator); clearer as `loop`.
   130	    fn parse_expr(&mut self, min_bp: u8) -> Result<Expr, ParseError> {
   131	        let mut lhs = self.parse_prefix()?;
   132	        // Audit H3 fix (2026-05-12): track whether we already consumed a `:` so
   133	        // `A:B:C` produces a parse error rather than silently merging spans via
   134	        // build_range's WholeColumn:WholeColumn arm. Excel rejects nested ranges
   135	        // outright. Reset is implicit: each recursive parse_expr call starts fresh,
   136	        // so `(A:B) + (C:D)` (two parenthesized ranges) parses cleanly.
   137	        let mut already_consumed_colon = false;
   138	
   139	        loop {
   140	            // Peek the next token; decide whether it's an infix/postfix operator we should
   141	            // continue consuming.
   142	            let next = match self.peek().cloned() {
   143	                Some(t) => t,
   144	                None => break,
   145	            };
   146	
   147	            // Postfix `%` — bp 60, no rhs.
   148	            if matches!(next, Token::Op(Operator::Percent)) {
   149	                if min_bp > 60 {
   150	                    break;
   151	                }
   152	                self.advance();
   153	                lhs = Expr::Unary {
   154	                    op: Operator::Percent,
   155	                    operand: Box::new(lhs),
   156	                };
   157	                continue;
   158	            }
   159	
   160	            // Range `:` — bp 80/81.
   161	            if matches!(next, Token::Colon) {
   162	                if min_bp > 80 {
   163	                    break;
   164	                }
   165	                if already_consumed_colon {
   166	                    return Err(ParseError::InvalidRange {
   167	                        detail: "nested range (e.g. A:B:C) — only one ':' per range; \
   168	                                 parenthesize sub-ranges if needed",
   169	                    });
   170	                }
   171	                self.advance();
   172	                let rhs = self.parse_prefix()?;
   173	                lhs = self.build_range(lhs, rhs)?;
   174	                already_consumed_colon = true;
   175	                continue;
   176	            }
   177	
   178	            // Binary operators.
   179	            if let Token::Op(op) = next {
   180	                let (lbp, rbp) = match infix_bp(op) {
   181	                    Some(bp) => bp,
   182	                    None => break,
   183	                };
   184	                if lbp < min_bp {
   185	                    break;
   186	                }
   187	                self.advance();
   188	                let rhs = self.parse_expr(rbp)?;
   189	                lhs = Expr::Binary {
   190	                    op,
   191	                    lhs: Box::new(lhs),
   192	                    rhs: Box::new(rhs),
   193	                };
   194	                continue;
   195	            }
   196	
   197	            // Anything else — terminator.
   198	            break;
   199	        }
   200	
   201	        Ok(lhs)
   202	    }
   203	
   204	    fn parse_prefix(&mut self) -> Result<Expr, ParseError> {
   205	        let token = self
   206	            .advance()
   207	            .ok_or(ParseError::UnexpectedEnd { context: "prefix" })?;
   208	
   209	        match token {
   210	            Token::Number(n) => Ok(Expr::Number(n)),
   211	            Token::String(s) => Ok(Expr::String(s)),
   212	
   213	            // Identifier — either a function call (if next is `(`) or a name-ref.
   214	            // Phase 2A.1 (2026-05-12): a bare identifier outside a function context
   215	            // emits `Expr::NameRef(name)`. The binder resolves the name against the
   216	            // workbook's `NameTable` at bind time and surfaces `UnresolvedName` if
   217	            // the name isn't registered.
   218	            Token::Ident(name) => {
   219	                if matches!(self.peek(), Some(Token::LParen)) {
   220	                    self.advance(); // consume `(`
   221	                    let args = self.parse_call_args()?;
   222	                    Ok(Expr::Function {
   223	                        name: canonicalize_function_name(&name),
   224	                        args,
   225	                    })
   226	                } else {
   227	                    // Recognize TRUE / FALSE as boolean literals here per Excel canon
   228	                    // (lexer doesn't yet emit Bool tokens; identifiers are routed via
   229	                    // the parser).
   230	                    let upper = name.to_ascii_uppercase();
   231	                    if upper == "TRUE" {
   232	                        return Ok(Expr::Bool(true));
   233	                    }
   234	                    if upper == "FALSE" {
   235	                        return Ok(Expr::Bool(false));
   236	                    }
   237	                    // Phase 2A.1 (2026-05-12): bare identifier (no LParen) emits
   238	                    // `Expr::NameRef(name)` — a defined-name reference resolved at
   239	                    // bind time against the workbook's NameTable. If the name isn't
   240	                    // registered, the binder returns `BindError::UnresolvedName`.
   241	                    //
   242	                    // This supersedes audit H2's parse-time rejection: the parse
   243	                    // succeeds; the rejection moves to bind time with richer info
   244	                    // (the actual unresolved name in the error variant).
   245	                    //
   246	                    // Built-in function names without parens (e.g. `=AVERAGE`) are
   247	                    // unresolved names too, since there's no NameTable entry for
   248	                    // them. Users get a clear "unresolved name AVERAGE" error
   249	                    // pointing them to `=AVERAGE()`.
   250	                    Ok(Expr::NameRef(canonicalize_function_name(&name)))
   251	                }
   252	            }
   253	
   254	            // CellRef — A1-style. Function-call disambiguation: if next is `(`, treat
   255	            // as a function name (e.g. `LOG10(2)`).
   256	            Token::CellRef {
   257	                col,
   258	                row,
   259	                abs_col,
   260	                abs_row,
   261	                text,
   262	            } => {
   263	                if matches!(self.peek(), Some(Token::LParen)) {
   264	                    self.advance();
   265	                    let args = self.parse_call_args()?;
   266	                    Ok(Expr::Function {
   267	                        name: canonicalize_function_name(&text),
   268	                        args,
   269	                    })
   270	                } else {
   271	                    Ok(Expr::CellRef(CellAddr {
   272	                        sheet: SheetRef::Current,
   273	                        col,
   274	                        row,
   275	                        abs_col,
   276	                        abs_row,
   277	                    }))
   278	                }
   279	            }
   280	
   281	            // BareColumn — `A`, `$AI` etc. Function call if followed by `(`; range
   282	            // anchor if followed by `:` (handled by the infix loop). Otherwise it's a
   283	            // standalone bare-column reference (e.g. inside `=A` — Excel parses this
   284	            // as a 1×N range). Audit L2 fix (2026-05-12): the ql-exec binder REJECTS
   285	            // standalone RangeRef::WholeColumn outside a Function context with
   286	            // BindError::UnsupportedVariant; previous doc claimed scalar evaluator
   287	            // fallback, which is wrong. Phase 4+ FormulaRegion binder handles this
   288	            // by lowering to row-aligned CellRefs.
   289	            Token::BareColumn { col, abs, text } => {
   290	                if matches!(self.peek(), Some(Token::LParen)) {
   291	                    self.advance();
   292	                    let args = self.parse_call_args()?;
   293	                    Ok(Expr::Function {
   294	                        name: canonicalize_function_name(&text),
   295	                        args,
   296	                    })
   297	                } else {
   298	                    // A bare column standing alone isn't a useful Phase 0 construct
   299	                    // (it'd evaluate over the entire column as a vector). Keep it as a
   300	                    // RangeRef::WholeColumn for the binder to handle / reject.
   301	                    Ok(Expr::RangeRef(RangeRef::WholeColumn {
   302	                        sheet: SheetRef::Current,
   303	                        start_col: col,
   304	                        end_col: col,
   305	                        abs_start: abs,
   306	                        abs_end: abs,
   307	                    }))
   308	                }
   309	            }
   310	
   311	            // BareRow — `1`, `$5`. Same logic but for rows. Cannot be a function call
   312	            // (digits-only identifiers aren't valid function names).
   313	            Token::BareRow { row, abs } => Ok(Expr::RangeRef(RangeRef::WholeRow {
   314	                sheet: SheetRef::Current,
   315	                start_row: row,
   316	                end_row: row,
   317	                abs_start: abs,
   318	                abs_end: abs,
   319	            })),
   320	
   321	            // Unary prefix operators.
   322	            Token::Op(Operator::Minus) => {
   323	                let operand = self.parse_expr(70)?;
   324	                Ok(Expr::Unary {
   325	                    op: Operator::Minus,
   326	                    operand: Box::new(operand),
   327	                })
   328	            }
   329	            Token::Op(Operator::Plus) => {
   330	                let operand = self.parse_expr(70)?;
   331	                Ok(Expr::Unary {
   332	                    op: Operator::Plus,
   333	                    operand: Box::new(operand),
   334	                })
   335	            }
   336	
   337	            // **W5-89 (Phase 4.6.A part 3):** sheet-qualified reference.
   338	            // Cross-sheet syntax `Sheet!Ref` and `'Sheet name'!Ref`.
   339	            // The lexer emits SheetName/QuotedSheetName + Bang as a
   340	            // two-token sequence; the parser consumes them as the
   341	            // PREFIX of a cell-or-range reference. The inner term must
   342	            // be a CellRef / BareColumn / BareRow / RangeRef shape —
   343	            // anything else is `SheetQualifierFollowedByNonReference`.
   344	            // Recursion: `parse_prefix` returns the FIRST term; range
   345	            // assembly (`A1:B2`) happens in the outer `parse_expr` loop
   346	            // via the `:` infix path.
   347	            Token::SheetName(name) | Token::QuotedSheetName(name) => {
   348	                // Expect Bang next (lexer guarantees this pairing).
   349	                match self.advance() {
   350	                    Some(Token::Bang) => {}
   351	                    Some(other) => {
   352	                        return Err(ParseError::Unexpected {
   353	                            context: "after sheet name",
   354	                            got: format!("{other:?}"),
   355	                        });
   356	                    }
   357	                    None => {
   358	                        return Err(ParseError::DanglingBang {
   359	                            name: name.as_ref().to_owned(),
   360	                        });
   361	                    }
   362	                }
   363	                // Special-case: bare `Sheet!` with end-of-input or a
   364	                // non-reference token. Surface as DanglingBang for
   365	                // clarity (vs the generic "unexpected end" / "unexpected
   366	                // token" the recursive call would emit).
   367	                if self.peek().is_none() {
   368	                    return Err(ParseError::DanglingBang {
   369	                        name: name.as_ref().to_owned(),
   370	                    });
   371	                }
   372	                let inner = self.parse_prefix()?;
   373	                Ok(apply_sheet_to_term(inner, name)?)
   374	            }
   375	
   376	            // Bare `!` without a preceding sheet name. The lexer doesn't
   377	            // emit `Token::Bang` outside the sheet-prefix sequence (it's
   378	            // only emitted via `try_lex_sheet_name_prefix` /
   379	            // `lex_quoted_sheet_name`), so reaching this arm means the
   380	            // user wrote `Sheet1!Sheet2!Foo` and our outer recursion
   381	            // ended up here. Reject explicitly as DoubleSheetQualifier
   382	            // — the more specific error than the generic "unexpected
   383	            // token" the wildcard would surface.
   384	            Token::Bang => Err(ParseError::DoubleSheetQualifier),
   385	
   386	            // Grouping `(...)`.
   387	            Token::LParen => {
   388	                let inner = self.parse_expr(0)?;
   389	                match self.advance() {
   390	                    Some(Token::RParen) => Ok(inner),
   391	                    Some(other) => Err(ParseError::Unexpected {
   392	                        context: "grouping",
   393	                        got: format!("{other:?}"),
   394	                    }),
   395	                    None => Err(ParseError::UnclosedDelimiter { what: "(" }),
   396	                }
   397	            }
   398	
   399	            other => Err(ParseError::Unexpected {
   400	                context: "prefix",
   401	                got: format!("{other:?}"),
   402	            }),
   403	        }
   404	    }
   405	
   406	    /// Parse comma-separated function-call arguments. Caller has already consumed `(`.
   407	    /// Consumes the closing `)`.
   408	    fn parse_call_args(&mut self) -> Result<Vec<Expr>, ParseError> {
   409	        let mut args = Vec::new();
   410	        // Empty arg list — immediate `)`.
   411	        if matches!(self.peek(), Some(Token::RParen)) {
   412	            self.advance();
   413	            return Ok(args);
   414	        }
   415	        loop {
   416	            let arg = self.parse_expr(0)?;
   417	            args.push(arg);
   418	            match self.advance() {
   419	                Some(Token::Comma) => continue,
   420	                Some(Token::RParen) => return Ok(args),
   421	                Some(other) => {
   422	                    return Err(ParseError::Unexpected {
   423	                        context: "function-args",
   424	                        got: format!("{other:?}"),
   425	                    })
   426	                }
   427	                None => return Err(ParseError::UnclosedDelimiter { what: "(" }),
   428	            }
   429	        }
   430	    }
   431	
   432	    /// Build a `RangeRef::Cells`-style range from a `:`-separated pair of operands.
   433	    /// Recognized patterns:
   434	    /// - `CellRef:CellRef` → `RangeRef::Cells`
   435	    /// - `WholeColumn:WholeColumn` → `RangeRef::WholeColumn` (merged spans)
   436	    /// - `WholeRow:WholeRow` → `RangeRef::WholeRow`
   437	    ///
   438	    /// Mixed cases (e.g. `A1:B`) are an `InvalidRange` parse error.
   439	    fn build_range(&self, lhs: Expr, rhs: Expr) -> Result<Expr, ParseError> {
   440	        // Normalize `Number:Number` (Excel whole-row notation `1:5`). The lexer emits
   441	        // Number tokens for bare digits without `$`; the parser converts them to
   442	        // 0-indexed row references here.
   443	        let lhs = number_to_bare_row(lhs)?;
   444	        let rhs = number_to_bare_row(rhs)?;
   445	        // **W5-89 (Phase 4.6.A part 3):** merge sheet refs across the
   446	        // range endpoints. Same-sheet, redundant-explicit, and prefix-
   447	        // on-LHS-only cases collapse to a single SheetRef; mixed-sheet
   448	        // endpoints reject. See `merge_sheet_refs` for the policy.
   449	        let merged_sheet = merge_sheet_refs(&sheet_of_term(&lhs), &sheet_of_term(&rhs))?;
   450	        match (lhs, rhs) {
   451	            (Expr::CellRef(a), Expr::CellRef(b)) => {
   452	                // Sort start/end on each axis (Excel normalizes A2:A1 → A1:A2).
   453	                let (start_col, end_col, abs_start_col, abs_end_col) = if a.col <= b.col {
   454	                    (a.col, b.col, a.abs_col, b.abs_col)
   455	                } else {
   456	                    (b.col, a.col, b.abs_col, a.abs_col)
   457	                };
   458	                let (start_row, end_row, abs_start_row, abs_end_row) = if a.row <= b.row {
   459	                    (a.row, b.row, a.abs_row, b.abs_row)
   460	                } else {
   461	                    (b.row, a.row, b.abs_row, a.abs_row)
   462	                };
   463	                Ok(Expr::RangeRef(RangeRef::Cells {
   464	                    sheet: merged_sheet.clone(),
   465	                    start_col,
   466	                    start_row,
   467	                    end_col,
   468	                    end_row,
   469	                    abs_start_col,
   470	                    abs_start_row,
   471	                    abs_end_col,
   472	                    abs_end_row,
   473	                }))
   474	            }
   475	            // `A:A` — both sides parsed as WholeColumn singletons; merge their spans.
   476	            (
   477	                Expr::RangeRef(RangeRef::WholeColumn {
   478	                    start_col: a_s,
   479	                    end_col: a_e,
   480	                    abs_start: a_abs_s,
   481	                    abs_end: a_abs_e,
   482	                    ..
   483	                }),
   484	                Expr::RangeRef(RangeRef::WholeColumn {
   485	                    start_col: b_s,
   486	                    end_col: b_e,
   487	                    abs_start: b_abs_s,
   488	                    abs_end: b_abs_e,
   489	                    ..
   490	                }),
   491	            ) => {
   492	                let lo = a_s.min(b_s).min(a_e).min(b_e);
   493	                let hi = a_e.max(b_e).max(a_s).max(b_s);
   494	                // Take the abs flag from whichever side contributed the extreme.
   495	                let abs_start = if a_s == lo { a_abs_s } else { b_abs_s };
   496	                let abs_end = if a_e == hi || b_e == hi {
   497	                    if a_e == hi {
   498	                        a_abs_e
   499	                    } else {
   500	                        b_abs_e
   501	                    }
   502	                } else {
   503	                    false
   504	                };
   505	                Ok(Expr::RangeRef(RangeRef::WholeColumn {
   506	                    sheet: merged_sheet.clone(),
   507	                    start_col: lo,
   508	                    end_col: hi,
   509	                    abs_start,
   510	                    abs_end,
   511	                }))
   512	            }
   513	            // `1:1` — both sides parsed as WholeRow singletons.
   514	            (
   515	                Expr::RangeRef(RangeRef::WholeRow {
   516	                    start_row: a_s,
   517	                    end_row: a_e,
   518	                    abs_start: a_abs_s,
   519	                    abs_end: a_abs_e,
   520	                    ..
