     1	//! AST printer — produces an A1-canonical string from an `Expr`.
     2	//!
     3	//! Phase B per CORR-20 deliverable: round-trip property `parse(print(parse(src))) ==
     4	//! parse(src)`. The printed form is NOT byte-identical to the source — operator
     5	//! spacing, parenthesization, and function-name casing normalize. The semantic
     6	//! equivalence is what matters (and what the round-trip property captures).
     7	//!
     8	//! ## Parenthesization
     9	//!
    10	//! The printer parenthesizes `Binary` and `Unary` subexpressions whenever the parent's
    11	//! operator binding power would cause re-parsing to misorder the result. The rule:
    12	//!
    13	//! - For a child binary expression with op `c_op`, parenthesize if `bp(c_op).lbp <
    14	//!   parent_min_bp`.
    15	//! - For a power right-operand: parenthesize if `bp(c_op).rbp <= parent_rbp` (since `^`
    16	//!   is right-associative).
    17	//!
    18	//! This produces canonical output: `=1 + 2 * 3` (no parens), `=(1 + 2) * 3` (parens
    19	//! around the addition).
    20	//!
    21	//! ## Round-trip semantics
    22	//!
    23	//! `Number::Number(0.5)` doesn't round-trip to the source `50%` — the lexer normalized
    24	//! percent at lex time. Other normalizations:
    25	//! - Function names → uppercase (`sum(...)` → `SUM(...)`).
    26	//! - Range corners → sorted (`A2:A1` → `A1:A2`).
    27	//! - Whitespace → eliminated except inside string literals.
    28	//!
    29	//! These are intentional canonical-form rewrites, not bugs. The round-trip property
    30	//! checks that `parse(printed) == parse(original)`, NOT that the strings match.
    31	
    32	use crate::ast::{CellAddr, Expr, RangeRef, SheetRef};
    33	use crate::token::Operator;
    34	
    35	/// Print an `Expr` to its A1-canonical string form.
    36	pub fn print(expr: &Expr) -> String {
    37	    let mut out = String::new();
    38	    print_expr(expr, &mut out, 0);
    39	    out
    40	}
    41	
    42	/// Convert a 0-indexed column number to Excel letters (A, B, ..., Z, AA, AB, ...).
    43	fn column_index_to_letters(mut col: u32) -> String {
    44	    let mut letters = String::new();
    45	    // Excel column letters are base-26 with A=1 (not base-26 with A=0). The conversion:
    46	    // 0 → A, 25 → Z, 26 → AA, 27 → AB, ..., 701 → ZZ, 702 → AAA.
    47	    loop {
    48	        let digit = (col % 26) as u8;
    49	        letters.insert(0, (b'A' + digit) as char);
    50	        if col < 26 {
    51	            break;
    52	        }
    53	        col = col / 26 - 1;
    54	    }
    55	    letters
    56	}
    57	
    58	fn print_cell_addr(addr: &CellAddr, out: &mut String) {
    59	    print_sheet_prefix(&addr.sheet, out);
    60	    if addr.abs_col {
    61	        out.push('$');
    62	    }
    63	    out.push_str(&column_index_to_letters(addr.col));
    64	    if addr.abs_row {
    65	        out.push('$');
    66	    }
    67	    // Row source-form is 1-based.
    68	    out.push_str(&(addr.row + 1).to_string());
    69	}
    70	
    71	/// **W5-89 (Phase 4.6.A part 3):** emit `Sheet!` or `'Sheet name'!`
    72	/// prefix for non-`Current` sheet refs. `Id` is post-bind-only; the
    73	/// printer panics if asked to render it (a future
    74	/// `print_with_resolver(expr, id_to_name)` API will handle that path).
    75	fn print_sheet_prefix(sheet: &SheetRef, out: &mut String) {
    76	    match sheet {
    77	        SheetRef::Current => {}
    78	        SheetRef::Name(name) => {
    79	            print_sheet_name(name, out);
    80	            out.push('!');
    81	        }
    82	        SheetRef::Id(id) => panic!(
    83	            "print: SheetRef::Id({id}) requires a sheet-name resolver — use a future print_with_resolver API"
    84	        ),
    85	    }
    86	}
    87	
    88	/// **W5-89 (Phase 4.6.A part 3):** emit a sheet name in its canonical
    89	/// source form. Quotes the name if it contains any character outside
    90	/// `[A-Za-z0-9_.]`, starts with a digit (would lex as a number/row),
    91	/// or is empty (defensive — empty names are rejected at registry time
    92	/// anyway). Embedded `'` is escaped as `''`.
    93	fn print_sheet_name(name: &str, out: &mut String) {
    94	    let needs_quoting = name.is_empty()
    95	        || name.starts_with(|c: char| c.is_ascii_digit())
    96	        || name
    97	            .chars()
    98	            .any(|c| !(c.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || c == '_' || c == '.'));
    99	    if needs_quoting {
   100	        out.push('\'');
   101	        for c in name.chars() {
   102	            if c == '\'' {
   103	                out.push('\'');
   104	                out.push('\'');
   105	            } else {
   106	                out.push(c);
   107	            }
   108	        }
   109	        out.push('\'');
   110	    } else {
   111	        out.push_str(name);
   112	    }
   113	}
   114	
   115	fn print_range(r: &RangeRef, out: &mut String) {
   116	    match r {
   117	        RangeRef::Cells {
   118	            sheet,
   119	            start_col,
   120	            start_row,
   121	            end_col,
   122	            end_row,
   123	            abs_start_col,
   124	            abs_start_row,
   125	            abs_end_col,
   126	            abs_end_row,
   127	        } => {
   128	            // **W5-89 (Phase 4.6.A part 3):** sheet prefix applies to the
   129	            // WHOLE range, emitted once at the start (Excel canon). The
   130	            // trailing endpoint omits the prefix; both endpoints get
   131	            // `SheetRef::Current` in the synthesized `CellAddr` so
   132	            // `print_cell_addr` emits no prefix internally.
   133	            print_sheet_prefix(sheet, out);
   134	            print_cell_addr(
   135	                &CellAddr {
   136	                    sheet: SheetRef::Current,
   137	                    col: *start_col,
   138	                    row: *start_row,
   139	                    abs_col: *abs_start_col,
   140	                    abs_row: *abs_start_row,
   141	                },
   142	                out,
   143	            );
   144	            out.push(':');
   145	            print_cell_addr(
   146	                &CellAddr {
   147	                    sheet: SheetRef::Current,
   148	                    col: *end_col,
   149	                    row: *end_row,
   150	                    abs_col: *abs_end_col,
   151	                    abs_row: *abs_end_row,
   152	                },
   153	                out,
   154	            );
   155	        }
   156	        RangeRef::WholeColumn {
   157	            sheet,
   158	            start_col,
   159	            end_col,
   160	            abs_start,
   161	            abs_end,
   162	        } => {
   163	            print_sheet_prefix(sheet, out);
   164	            if *abs_start {
   165	                out.push('$');
   166	            }
   167	            out.push_str(&column_index_to_letters(*start_col));
   168	            out.push(':');
   169	            if *abs_end {
   170	                out.push('$');
   171	            }
   172	            out.push_str(&column_index_to_letters(*end_col));
   173	        }
   174	        RangeRef::WholeRow {
   175	            sheet,
   176	            start_row,
   177	            end_row,
   178	            abs_start,
   179	            abs_end,
   180	        } => {
   181	            print_sheet_prefix(sheet, out);
   182	            if *abs_start {
   183	                out.push('$');
   184	            }
   185	            out.push_str(&(*start_row + 1).to_string());
   186	            out.push(':');
   187	            if *abs_end {
   188	                out.push('$');
   189	            }
   190	            out.push_str(&(*end_row + 1).to_string());
   191	        }
   192	    }
   193	}
   194	
   195	fn print_string_literal(s: &str, out: &mut String) {
   196	    out.push('"');
   197	    for ch in s.chars() {
   198	        if ch == '"' {
   199	            out.push('"');
   200	            out.push('"');
   201	        } else {
   202	            out.push(ch);
   203	        }
   204	    }
   205	    out.push('"');
   206	}
   207	
   208	fn print_number(n: f64, out: &mut String) {
   209	    // Use Rust's default f64 Display which matches Excel's general number formatting
   210	    // closely (e.g., 2.0 prints as "2", 2.5 as "2.5", 1e10 as "10000000000").
   211	    if n.fract() == 0.0 && n.abs() < 1.0e16 {
   212	        // Integer-valued f64 → no decimal point.
   213	        out.push_str(&format!("{}", n as i64));
   214	    } else {
   215	        out.push_str(&format!("{n}"));
   216	    }
   217	}
   218	
   219	/// Print an Expr with parenthesization decisions driven by `parent_min_bp` — the
   220	/// effective right-binding-power of the parent context. Higher = more aggressive
   221	/// parenthesization.
   222	fn print_expr(expr: &Expr, out: &mut String, parent_min_bp: u8) {
   223	    match expr {
   224	        Expr::Number(n) => print_number(*n, out),
   225	        Expr::Bool(b) => out.push_str(if *b { "TRUE" } else { "FALSE" }),
   226	        Expr::String(s) => print_string_literal(s, out),
   227	        Expr::CellRef(addr) => print_cell_addr(addr, out),
   228	        Expr::RangeRef(r) => print_range(r, out),
   229	        Expr::Binary { op, lhs, rhs } => {
   230	            let (lbp, rbp) = binary_bp(*op);
   231	            let needs_parens = lbp < parent_min_bp;
   232	            if needs_parens {
   233	                out.push('(');
   234	            }
   235	            // Audit H1 fix (2026-05-12): for right-associative ops (Pow has lbp > rbp
   236	            // in Pratt convention), the LHS of the same op MUST be parenthesized so
   237	            // `(a^b)^c` round-trips correctly. Previously the LHS got `lbp` which
   238	            // matched the child's lbp and produced no parens, silently re-parsing
   239	            // as `a^(b^c)`. For left-associative ops, lbp = rbp - 1, so passing lbp
   240	            // continues to allow `(a+b)+c` chains to print as `a + b + c` (correct
   241	            // left-assoc).
   242	            let is_right_assoc = lbp > rbp;
   243	            let lhs_min_bp = if is_right_assoc { lbp + 1 } else { lbp };
   244	            print_expr(lhs, out, lhs_min_bp);
   245	            out.push(' ');
   246	            out.push_str(op.as_str());
   247	            out.push(' ');
   248	            print_expr(rhs, out, rbp + 1); // +1 so right operand of same op gets parens
   249	            if needs_parens {
   250	                out.push(')');
   251	            }
   252	        }
   253	        Expr::Unary { op, operand } => {
   254	            match op {
   255	                Operator::Percent => {
   256	                    // Postfix: operand% — parenthesize if operand is a Binary/Unary
   257	                    // that has lower precedence than percent (bp 60).
   258	                    print_expr(operand, out, 61);
   259	                    out.push('%');
   260	                }
   261	                Operator::Minus | Operator::Plus => {
   262	                    // Prefix: -operand / +operand
   263	                    out.push_str(op.as_str());
   264	                    print_expr(operand, out, 70);
   265	                }
   266	                other => {
   267	                    // Other operators aren't valid as unary; print as prefix anyway.
   268	                    out.push_str(other.as_str());
   269	                    print_expr(operand, out, 70);
   270	                }
   271	            }
   272	        }
   273	        Expr::Function { name, args } => {
   274	            out.push_str(name);
   275	            out.push('(');
   276	            for (i, a) in args.iter().enumerate() {
   277	                if i > 0 {
   278	                    out.push_str(", ");
   279	                }
   280	                print_expr(a, out, 0);
   281	            }
   282	            out.push(')');
   283	        }
   284	        Expr::NameRef(name) => {
   285	            // Phase 2A.1: defined-name reference round-trips as the bare name.
   286	            out.push_str(name);
   287	        }
   288	        Expr::Array(_) | Expr::Spill(_) => {
   289	            // Audit M1 fix (2026-05-12): Phase 0/1 doesn't construct these; if one
   290	            // somehow appears in the AST it's a programmer bug, not a print-time
   291	            // fallback. Loud panic per the no-fallbacks rule.
   292	            unreachable!(
   293	                "print_expr: Array/Spill variants are Phase 3+ and not constructed in \
   294	                 Phase 0/1 ASTs — reaching here means the AST was malformed"
   295	            );
   296	        }
   297	    }
   298	}
   299	
   300	/// Binding-power table for binary operators — must match `parser::infix_bp`.
   301	fn binary_bp(op: Operator) -> (u8, u8) {
   302	    match op {
   303	        Operator::Eq
   304	        | Operator::Neq
   305	        | Operator::Lt
   306	        | Operator::Le
   307	        | Operator::Gt
   308	        | Operator::Ge => (10, 11),
   309	        Operator::Concat => (20, 21),
   310	        Operator::Plus | Operator::Minus => (30, 31),
   311	        Operator::Mul | Operator::Div => (40, 41),
   312	        Operator::Pow => (51, 50),
   313	        // Audit L4 fix (2026-05-12): Percent is postfix-only; `binary_bp(Percent)`
   314	        // never occurs in correct code. Loud panic per the no-fallbacks rule.
   315	        Operator::Percent => {
   316	            unreachable!("binary_bp called with Operator::Percent (postfix-only); programmer bug")
   317	        }
   318	    }
   319	}

   329	        let expr = parse(lex(src).expect("lex")).expect("parse");
   330	        print(&expr)

   333	    fn rt_roundtrip(src: &str) {
   334	        // Round-trip property: parse(print(parse(src))) == parse(src).
   335	        let first = parse(lex(src).expect("lex")).expect("parse");
   336	        let printed = print(&first);
   337	        let second = parse(lex(&printed).expect("lex of printed")).expect("re-parse");
   338	        assert_eq!(
   339	            first, second,
   340	            "round-trip failed for: {src}\nprinted: {printed}"
   341	        );
   342	    }

   420	fn classify_letter_prefix_simple(letters: &str) -> Result<LetterColumn, LexError> {
   421	    if letters.is_empty() || letters.len() > 3 {
   422	        return Err(LexError::ColumnTooLarge(letters.to_string()));
   423	    }
   424	    if !letters.chars().all(|c| c.is_ascii_alphabetic()) {
   425	        return Err(LexError::ColumnTooLarge(letters.to_string()));
   426	    }
   427	    let col = column_letters_to_index(letters)?;
   428	    Ok(LetterColumn { col })
   429	}
   430	
   431	/// Convert Excel column letters (case-insensitive, 1-3 letters) into 0-indexed column.
   432	/// A = 0, Z = 25, AA = 26, ZZ = 701, AAA = 702, XFD = 16383.
   433	pub fn column_letters_to_index(letters: &str) -> Result<u32, LexError> {
   434	    if letters.is_empty() || letters.len() > 3 {
   435	        return Err(LexError::ColumnTooLarge(letters.to_string()));
   436	    }
   437	    let mut value: u32 = 0;
   438	    for c in letters.chars() {
   439	        let digit = match c {
   440	            'A'..='Z' => (c as u32) - ('A' as u32) + 1,
   441	            'a'..='z' => (c as u32) - ('a' as u32) + 1,
   442	            _ => return Err(LexError::ColumnTooLarge(letters.to_string())),
   443	        };
   444	        // Excel uses bijective base-26: each position contributes (letter * 26^k).
   445	        value = value
   446	            .checked_mul(26)
   447	            .and_then(|v| v.checked_add(digit))
   448	            .ok_or_else(|| LexError::ColumnTooLarge(letters.to_string()))?;
   449	    }
   450	    let zero_based = value - 1;
   451	    if zero_based > MAX_COLUMN {
   452	        return Err(LexError::ColumnTooLarge(letters.to_string()));
   453	    }
   454	    Ok(zero_based)
   455	}
   456	
   457	// ===== W5-88 (Phase 4.6.A part 2) — sheet-prefix lexing =====
   458	
   459	/// Scan an unquoted sheet-name candidate at `chars` and peek ahead for
   460	/// `!`. If a `Sheet!` prefix is recognized, emit `SheetName + Bang` and
   461	/// return `Ok(true)`. If not (the leading run is actually an Ident /
   462	/// CellRef / etc.), leave `chars` untouched and return `Ok(false)` —
   463	/// the caller falls through to the existing dispatch.
   464	///
   465	/// Multi-char lookahead via `Chars::clone()`: cheap (just a `&str` +
   466	/// cursor) and doesn't disturb the real iterator.
   467	///
   468	/// Per design doc § 4.1: name shape `[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_.]*`. Per
   469	/// § 4.3 / Codex MEDIUM-1: this routine MUST run before the A1/
   470	/// function/name classifier so `A1!B1` lexes sheet `A1`, not cell
   471	/// `A1`. Edge cases (whitespace around `!`, dotted names, etc.) are
   472	/// pinned in the test corpus.
   473	fn try_lex_sheet_name_prefix(
   474	    chars: &mut Peekable<Chars>,
   475	    out: &mut Vec<Token>,
   476	) -> Result<bool, LexError> {
   477	    // Peek into a clone to scan the candidate name + lookahead.
   478	    let mut peek = chars.clone();
   479	    let mut name = String::new();
   480	
   481	    // First char: `[A-Za-z_]` (caller's match arm already verified).
   482	    let first = peek.next().expect("caller guaranteed at least one char");
   483	    debug_assert!(first.is_ascii_alphabetic() || first == '_');
   484	    name.push(first);
   485	
   486	    // Trailing chars: `[A-Za-z0-9_.]`.
   487	    while let Some(&c) = peek.peek() {
   488	        if c.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || c == '_' || c == '.' {
   489	            name.push(c);
   490	            peek.next();
   491	        } else {
   492	            break;
   493	        }
   494	    }
   495	
   496	    // Skip whitespace between name and `!` per design § 4.3.
   497	    while let Some(&c) = peek.peek() {
   498	        if matches!(c, ' ' | '\t' | '\n' | '\r') {
   499	            peek.next();
   500	        } else {
   501	            break;
   502	        }
   503	    }
   504	
   505	    // Decision: is the next char `!`?
   506	    let is_sheet_prefix = peek.peek() == Some(&'!');
   507	    if !is_sheet_prefix {
   508	        // Not a sheet prefix — leave `chars` untouched; caller falls
   509	        // through to the existing identifier dispatch.
   510	        return Ok(false);
   511	    }
   512	
   513	    // Commit: consume from the real `chars` iterator to match what we
   514	    // scanned in the clone, then emit SheetName + Bang.
   515	    for _ in 0..name.chars().count() {
   516	        chars.next();
   517	    }
   518	    // Skip whitespace between name and `!`.
   519	    while let Some(&c) = chars.peek() {
   520	        if matches!(c, ' ' | '\t' | '\n' | '\r') {
   521	            chars.next();
   522	        } else {
   523	            break;
   524	        }
   525	    }
   526	    // Consume the `!`.
   527	    chars.next();
   528	
   529	    out.push(Token::SheetName(Arc::from(name)));
   530	    out.push(Token::Bang);
   531	    Ok(true)
   532	}
   533	
   534	/// Lex a quoted-sheet-name prefix `'...'!`. The caller has just peeked
   535	/// the opening `'`. The body allows any char except `'`; `''` decodes
   536	/// to a single `'`. After the closing `'`, whitespace is skipped and
   537	/// `!` is required — bare `'...'` without `!` produces
   538	/// `LexError::DanglingQuotedString`.
   539	fn lex_quoted_sheet_name(
   540	    chars: &mut Peekable<Chars>,
   541	    out: &mut Vec<Token>,
   542	) -> Result<(), LexError> {
   543	    // Consume opening quote.
   544	    chars.next();
   545	    let mut name = String::new();
   546	    loop {
   547	        match chars.next() {
   548	            None => return Err(LexError::UnterminatedQuotedSheetName),
   549	            Some('\'') => {
   550	                // `''` is the embedded-quote escape; otherwise this is
   551	                // the closing quote.
   552	                if chars.peek() == Some(&'\'') {
   553	                    chars.next();
   554	                    name.push('\'');
   555	                } else {
   556	                    break;
   557	                }
   558	            }
   559	            Some(c) => name.push(c),
   560	        }
   561	    }
   562	    // Skip whitespace before required `!`.
   563	    while let Some(&c) = chars.peek() {
   564	        if matches!(c, ' ' | '\t' | '\n' | '\r') {
   565	            chars.next();
   566	        } else {
   567	            break;
   568	        }
   569	    }
   570	    if chars.peek() != Some(&'!') {
   571	        return Err(LexError::DanglingQuotedString);
   572	    }
   573	    chars.next();
   574	
   575	    out.push(Token::QuotedSheetName(Arc::from(name)));
   576	    out.push(Token::Bang);
   577	    Ok(())
   578	}

   611	        // are NOT whitespace in Excel formulas — should error out instead of being silently
   612	        // dropped. \r\n line endings ARE accepted (the four ASCII forms).
   613	        assert_eq!(

   521	                }),
   522	                Expr::RangeRef(RangeRef::WholeRow {
   523	                    start_row: b_s,
   524	                    end_row: b_e,
   525	                    abs_start: b_abs_s,
   526	                    abs_end: b_abs_e,

   528	                }),

   531	                let hi = a_e.max(b_e).max(a_s).max(b_s);
   532	                let abs_start = if a_s == lo { a_abs_s } else { b_abs_s };

   534	                    if a_e == hi {
   535	                        a_abs_e
   536	                    } else {
   537	                        b_abs_e
   538	                    }
   539	                } else {
   540	                    false
   541	                };

   544	                    start_row: lo,

   547	                    abs_end,
   548	                }))
   549	            }
   550	            _ => Err(ParseError::InvalidRange {
   551	                detail: "mixed CellRef/Column/Row range operands (e.g. A1:B); Phase 0 only allows matching types",
   552	            }),
   553	        }
   554	    }

   557	/// **W5-89 (Phase 4.6.A part 3):** attach a sheet name from `Sheet!Ref`
   558	/// syntax to the inner reference term. The inner term must be a
   559	/// reference shape (CellRef / RangeRef::*). Anything else is a parse
   560	/// error. If the inner reference already has a non-`Current` sheet,
   561	/// that's `DoubleSheetQualifier` (`Sheet1!Sheet2!X`).
   562	fn apply_sheet_to_term(expr: Expr, name: Arc<str>) -> Result<Expr, ParseError> {
   563	    // **W5-89:** `Sheet1!5:10` — the lexer emits `Number(5)` for bare
   564	    // digits. The whole-row range conversion happens in `build_range`
   565	    // when the `:` infix triggers. Up-front, we convert here so the
   566	    // sheet prefix attaches to the resulting `WholeRow` singleton (and
   567	    // the subsequent range merge keeps the prefix). Non-Number Exprs
   568	    // pass through unchanged.
   569	    let expr = if matches!(expr, Expr::Number(_)) {
   570	        number_to_bare_row(expr)?
   571	    } else {
   572	        expr
   573	    };
   574	    match expr {
   575	        Expr::CellRef(addr) => {
   576	            if !matches!(addr.sheet, SheetRef::Current) {
   577	                return Err(ParseError::DoubleSheetQualifier);
   578	            }
   579	            Ok(Expr::CellRef(CellAddr {
   580	                sheet: SheetRef::Name(name),
   581	                ..addr
   582	            }))
   583	        }
   584	        Expr::RangeRef(r) => {
   585	            let updated = match r {
   586	                RangeRef::Cells {
   587	                    sheet,
   588	                    start_col,
   589	                    start_row,
   590	                    end_col,
   591	                    end_row,
   592	                    abs_start_col,
   593	                    abs_start_row,
   594	                    abs_end_col,
   595	                    abs_end_row,
   596	                } => {
   597	                    if !matches!(sheet, SheetRef::Current) {
   598	                        return Err(ParseError::DoubleSheetQualifier);
   599	                    }
   600	                    RangeRef::Cells {
   601	                        sheet: SheetRef::Name(name),
   602	                        start_col,
   603	                        start_row,
   604	                        end_col,
   605	                        end_row,
   606	                        abs_start_col,
   607	                        abs_start_row,
   608	                        abs_end_col,
   609	                        abs_end_row,
   610	                    }
   611	                }
   612	                RangeRef::WholeColumn {
   613	                    sheet,
   614	                    start_col,
   615	                    end_col,
   616	                    abs_start,
   617	                    abs_end,
   618	                } => {
   619	                    if !matches!(sheet, SheetRef::Current) {
   620	                        return Err(ParseError::DoubleSheetQualifier);
   621	                    }
   622	                    RangeRef::WholeColumn {
   623	                        sheet: SheetRef::Name(name),
   624	                        start_col,
   625	                        end_col,
   626	                        abs_start,
   627	                        abs_end,
   628	                    }
   629	                }
   630	                RangeRef::WholeRow {
   631	                    sheet,
   632	                    start_row,
   633	                    end_row,
   634	                    abs_start,
   635	                    abs_end,
   636	                } => {
   637	                    if !matches!(sheet, SheetRef::Current) {
   638	                        return Err(ParseError::DoubleSheetQualifier);
   639	                    }
   640	                    RangeRef::WholeRow {
   641	                        sheet: SheetRef::Name(name),
   642	                        start_row,
   643	                        end_row,
   644	                        abs_start,
   645	                        abs_end,
   646	                    }
   647	                }
   648	            };
   649	            Ok(Expr::RangeRef(updated))
   650	        }
   651	        other => Err(ParseError::SheetQualifierFollowedByNonReference {
   652	            got: format!("{other:?}"),
   653	        }),
   654	    }
   655	}
   656	
   657	/// **W5-89 (Phase 4.6.A part 3):** merge two endpoint `SheetRef`s for
   658	/// range construction per design § 10.5:
   659	///
   660	/// - `(Current, Current)` → `Current` (same-sheet range).
   661	/// - `(Name(N), Current)` → `Name(N)` (prefix applies to whole range).
   662	/// - `(Current, Name(_))` → `MixedSheetRangeEndpoints` (Excel requires
   663	///   the prefix at the start, not on the trailing endpoint).
   664	/// - `(Name(A), Name(B))` with `A != B` (case-insensitive) →
   665	///   `MixedSheetRangeEndpoints`.
   666	/// - `(Name(A), Name(A))` → `Name(A)` (redundant explicit form,
   667	///   normalized to single-prefix).
   668	/// - `Id` variants are produced post-bind / by tests; treated symmetrically.
   669	fn merge_sheet_refs(lhs: &SheetRef, rhs: &SheetRef) -> Result<SheetRef, ParseError> {
   670	    match (lhs, rhs) {
   671	        (SheetRef::Current, SheetRef::Current) => Ok(SheetRef::Current),
   672	        (SheetRef::Name(n), SheetRef::Current) => Ok(SheetRef::Name(n.clone())),
   673	        (SheetRef::Id(s), SheetRef::Current) => Ok(SheetRef::Id(*s)),
   674	        (SheetRef::Current, SheetRef::Name(_) | SheetRef::Id(_)) => {
   675	            Err(ParseError::MixedSheetRangeEndpoints)
   676	        }
   677	        (SheetRef::Name(a), SheetRef::Name(b)) => {
   678	            if a.eq_ignore_ascii_case(b.as_ref()) {
   679	                Ok(SheetRef::Name(a.clone()))
   680	            } else {
   681	                Err(ParseError::MixedSheetRangeEndpoints)
   682	            }
   683	        }
   684	        (SheetRef::Id(a), SheetRef::Id(b)) if a == b => Ok(SheetRef::Id(*a)),
   685	        // Cross-variant mismatch (Name vs Id) or different Id values.
   686	        _ => Err(ParseError::MixedSheetRangeEndpoints),
   687	    }
   688	}
   689	
   690	/// **W5-89 (Phase 4.6.A part 3):** extract the `SheetRef` field from
   691	/// a reference-shaped Expr. Returns `Current` for non-reference shapes
   692	/// so the caller's pattern-match logic stays clean (those branches
   693	/// fail earlier with type-mismatch errors anyway).
   694	fn sheet_of_term(expr: &Expr) -> SheetRef {
   695	    match expr {
   696	        Expr::CellRef(addr) => addr.sheet.clone(),
   697	        Expr::RangeRef(RangeRef::Cells { sheet, .. })
   698	        | Expr::RangeRef(RangeRef::WholeColumn { sheet, .. })
   699	        | Expr::RangeRef(RangeRef::WholeRow { sheet, .. }) => sheet.clone(),
   700	        _ => SheetRef::Current,
   701	    }
   702	}
   703	
   704	/// Convert a literal `Number(N)` to a `RangeRef::WholeRow` singleton (1-indexed in source
   705	/// → 0-indexed internal). Used in range parsing for the `1:5` whole-row pattern. Other
   706	/// Expr variants pass through unchanged.
   707	fn number_to_bare_row(e: Expr) -> Result<Expr, ParseError> {
   708	    if let Expr::Number(n) = e {
   709	        // Excel row indices are 1-based positive integers in source. Validate:
   710	        if n.fract() != 0.0 || n < 1.0 || n > (u32::MAX as f64) {
   711	            return Err(ParseError::InvalidRange {
   712	                detail: "row index must be a positive integer",
   713	            });
   714	        }
   715	        let row = (n as u32) - 1;
   716	        Ok(Expr::RangeRef(RangeRef::WholeRow {
   717	            sheet: SheetRef::Current,
   718	            start_row: row,
   719	            end_row: row,
   720	            abs_start: false,
   721	            abs_end: false,
   722	        }))
   723	    } else {
   724	        Ok(e)
   725	    }
   726	}
   727	
   728	/// Infix-operator binding powers. Returns `None` for tokens that aren't infix operators.
   729	fn infix_bp(op: Operator) -> Option<(u8, u8)> {
   730	    match op {
   731	        Operator::Eq
   732	        | Operator::Neq
   733	        | Operator::Lt
   734	        | Operator::Le
   735	        | Operator::Gt
   736	        | Operator::Ge => Some((10, 11)),
   737	        Operator::Concat => Some((20, 21)),
   738	        Operator::Plus | Operator::Minus => Some((30, 31)),
   739	        Operator::Mul | Operator::Div => Some((40, 41)),
   740	        Operator::Pow => Some((51, 50)), // right-assoc: lbp > rbp by 1
   741	        Operator::Percent => None,       // postfix-only
   742	    }
   743	}

   745	/// Canonicalize a function name to uppercase (Excel convention) per CORR-06.
   746	/// Returns an Arc<str> for cheap clone into Expr::Function.name.
   747	fn canonicalize_function_name(name: &Arc<str>) -> Arc<str> {
   748	    let upper = name.as_ref().to_ascii_uppercase();
   749	    Arc::from(upper.as_str())
   750	}

     1	//! `ExprPlan` — execution-ready intermediate representation.
     2	//!
     3	//! Sits between `ql_formula_syntax::Expr` (parser output, may carry `Option<SheetId>` for
     4	//! unresolved same-sheet refs) and the kernel dispatch in `scalar.rs` / `simd.rs` (W4-2).
     5	//! `bind()` walks the Expr tree and resolves every `CellAddr.sheet: Option<SheetId>` to a
     6	//! concrete `SheetId` based on the formula's owning sheet.
     7	//!
     8	//! The plan is "execution-ready" but still tree-shaped — it does NOT yet flatten the
     9	//! expression into a SIMD-friendly opcode stream. That's the role of `kernel_simd` in W4-2,
    10	//! which lowers `ExprPlan::Binary { op: Mul, lhs: CellRef, rhs: Number }` into a
    11	//! `multiversion`-dispatched `Float64Array * f64` call.
    12	//!
    13	//! ## Phase 0 scope
    14	//!
    15	//! Subset of `Expr` variants covered:
    16	//! - `Number(f64)` — numeric literal
    17	//! - `Bool(bool)` — boolean literal
    18	//! - `String(Arc<str>)` — text literal (binds 1:1)
    19	//! - `CellRef { sheet, row, col, abs_col, abs_row }` — sheet RESOLVED to concrete SheetId
    20	//! - `Binary { op, lhs, rhs }` — arithmetic + concat + comparison
    21	//! - `Unary { op, operand }` — unary minus / plus / percent
    22	//!
    23	//! Deferred (will surface in W4-4 ql-functions integration):
    24	//! - `RangeRef` — single range used inside a Function call (e.g. `SUM(A:A)`)
    25	//! - `Function { name, args }` — registry dispatch
    26	//! - `Array`, `Spill` — Phase 3+ dynamic arrays
    27	
    28	use std::sync::Arc;
    29	
    30	use ql_formula_syntax::{Expr, Operator, SheetRef};
    31	use ql_types::{ColId, ErrorValue, Range, RowId, SheetId};
    32	
    33	/// Bound, execution-ready expression. Sheet refs are concrete.
    34	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
    35	pub enum ExprPlan {
    36	    Number(f64),
    37	    Bool(bool),
    38	    String(Arc<str>),
    39	    CellRef {
    40	        sheet: SheetId,
    41	        row: RowId,
    42	        col: ColId,
    43	        abs_col: bool,
    44	        abs_row: bool,
    45	    },
    46	    Binary {
    47	        op: Operator,
    48	        lhs: Box<ExprPlan>,
    49	        rhs: Box<ExprPlan>,
    50	    },
    51	    Unary {
    52	        op: Operator,
    53	        operand: Box<ExprPlan>,
    54	    },
    55	    /// Function call. Args are bound recursively. Phase 0 W4-5: name is preserved
    56	    /// verbatim from the AST (Excel-canonical uppercase normalization happens at the
    57	    /// parser level — W4-5 expects already-uppercased names from the binder). The
    58	    /// scalar evaluator looks up the name in a `FunctionRegistry` at evaluation time.
    59	    Function {
    60	        name: Arc<str>,
    61	        args: Vec<ExprPlan>,
    62	    },
    63	    /// Phase 2B.4 (2026-05-12): a `NameRef` resolving to a `NamedTarget::Range`
    64	    /// in an aggregate-argument position (e.g. the arg of `SUM`, `AVERAGE`,
    65	    /// `COUNT`). Carries the canonical name + the resolved Range. The scalar
    66	    /// evaluator currently rejects this with `Value::Error(ErrorValue::Calc)`
    67	    /// because aggregate-range evaluation is Engine Phase 3.6 work; the bind
    68	    /// shape lands now so the IDE / op-log surfaces are stable when Phase 3.6
    69	    /// flips the eval on.
    70	    ///
    71	    /// In scalar (non-aggregate) positions, the same `NameRef` produces
    72	    /// [`BindError::NamedRangeInScalarContext`] instead of binding to this
    73	    /// variant.
    74	    AggregateNameRef {
    75	        /// Canonical (uppercase) name as it appears in the NameTable.
    76	        name: Arc<str>,
    77	        /// Pre-resolved range payload. Phase 3.6 evaluator reads from this
    78	        /// directly rather than re-looking-up the name.
    79	        range: Range,
    80	    },
    81	}
    82	
    83	/// Error during binding. Phase 2A.1 added `UnresolvedName`; Phase 2A.6 added
    84	/// `NamedTargetIs{Blank,Error}`; Phase 2A.11 audit M16 switched to
    85	/// `thiserror::Error` for consistency with the rest of the error surface.
    86	/// Display strings are now user-facing (suitable for IDE diagnostics).
    87	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, thiserror::Error)]
    88	pub enum BindError {
    89	    /// The Expr contains a variant not supported in this build (e.g. RangeRef
    90	    /// outside a Function context, Array literal, Spill anchor, NamedTarget::Range
    91	    /// — all Engine Phase 4 work per `docs/MASTER-PLAN.md`).
    92	    #[error("unsupported expression variant: {0}")]
    93	    UnsupportedVariant(&'static str),
    94	    /// A `NameRef` was used but the name isn't registered in the active `NameTable`.
    95	    /// Phase 2A.1: equivalent to Excel's `#NAME?` but surfaced at bind time rather
    96	    /// than as a runtime Error value, so the IDE can highlight the offending token
    97	    /// before evaluation.
    98	    #[error("unresolved name {0:?}")]
    99	    UnresolvedName(Arc<str>),
   100	    /// A `NameRef` resolved to a `NamedTarget::Constant(Value::Blank)`. Phase 2A.6
   101	    /// audit M2: previously this was silently coerced to `Text("")`, producing
   102	    /// nonsense in arithmetic contexts. Now surfaced loudly so callers fix the
   103	    /// data binding.
   104	    #[error("named constant {0:?} is blank; cannot be used in this context")]
   105	    NamedTargetIsBlank(Arc<str>),
   106	    /// A `NameRef` resolved to a `NamedTarget::Constant(Value::Error(_))`. Phase 2A.6
   107	    /// audit M3: previously silently mapped to `UnresolvedName` (wrong error class —
   108	    /// the name IS resolved, just to an error value). Carries the underlying
   109	    /// `ErrorValue` so the IDE can echo `#DIV/0!` / `#REF!` etc. accurately.
   110	    // Phase 2A.13 audit cycle-3 M1: `{1}` (Display) instead of `{1:?}` (Debug).
   111	    // `ErrorValue` has a `Display` impl that renders the canonical Excel sigil
   112	    // (`#DIV/0!` etc.); the prior debug formatter leaked Rust enum identifiers
   113	    // ("DivZero") into IDE diagnostics — the same mistake WS-4 was supposed to
   114	    // close everywhere. Note `{0:?}` is kept deliberately on the name field
   115	    // so the Arc<str> is rendered with surrounding quotes for visual clarity
   116	    // ("named constant \"X\" ..." vs the bare-string ambiguous "named constant X").
   117	    #[error("named constant {0:?} holds an error value: {1}")]
   118	    NamedTargetIsError(Arc<str>, ErrorValue),
   119	    /// Phase 2B.4 (2026-05-12): a `NameRef` resolving to a `NamedTarget::Range`
   120	    /// appeared in a scalar-context position (e.g. `=Sales + 1` instead of
   121	    /// `=SUM(Sales)`). Excel returns `#VALUE!` at evaluation; we surface it at
   122	    /// bind so the IDE highlights the offending token before any state writes.
   123	    /// Distinct from the generic `UnsupportedVariant` so callers can render a
   124	    /// specific "this name is a range; use it inside an aggregate function"
   125	    /// hint.
   126	    #[error(
   127	        "named range {0:?} cannot be used in this scalar position; \
   128	         wrap it in an aggregate function such as SUM, AVERAGE, COUNT, MIN, MAX"
   129	    )]
   130	    NamedRangeInScalarContext(Arc<str>),
   131	    /// Phase 2B.4 (2026-05-12): a `NameRef` resolving to a
   132	    /// `NamedTarget::Formula` in a scalar-context position. Named formulas
   133	    /// (`Profit = Revenue - Costs`) are Engine Phase 4 work; this distinct
   134	    /// variant tells the IDE / caller that the name is recognized but the
   135	    /// named-formula feature itself is not yet shipped.
   136	    #[error(
   137	        "named formula {0:?} cannot be used in this position; \
   138	         named-formula resolution is Engine Phase 4 work"
   139	    )]
   140	    NamedFormulaUnsupported(Arc<str>),
   141	
   142	    /// **W5-90 (Phase 4.6.B):** `SheetRef::Name(N)` from cross-sheet
   143	    /// syntax (`Sheet1!A1`) didn't resolve to any sheet in the
   144	    /// workbook at bind time. Surfaces as a clean bind-time diagnostic
   145	    /// before any runtime read; closes XS-4-01.
   146	    #[error("unknown sheet {0:?}")]
   147	    UnknownSheet(Arc<str>),
   148	}
   149	
   150	/// Phase 2B.4 (2026-05-12): bind-time context for a sub-expression. Drives
   151	/// per-name-ref decisions about whether a `NamedTarget::Range` is acceptable
   152	/// (aggregate-arg) or surfaces as `BindError::NamedRangeInScalarContext`
   153	/// (scalar). Internal to the binder; not exposed in the public API yet.
   154	#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
   155	enum BindContext {
   156	    /// Default. NameRefs resolving to ranges or formulas are rejected with
   157	    /// precise errors.
   158	    Scalar,
   159	    /// Inside the argument list of an aggregate function (SUM, AVERAGE,
   160	    /// MIN, MAX, COUNT, etc. — see `is_aggregate_function`). NameRefs
   161	    /// resolving to ranges bind to `ExprPlan::AggregateNameRef`; named
   162	    /// formulas still error (Engine Phase 4 work).
   163	    AggregateArg,
   164	}
   165	
   166	/// Phase 2B.4 (2026-05-12): hardcoded list of functions whose arguments
   167	/// accept aggregate-range / named-range inputs. The function name is
   168	/// historical — this list is now better understood as "names for which
   169	/// the binder allows `AggregateNameRef` in arg positions"; both scalar
   170	/// aggregates (SUM, AVERAGE) AND range-aware functions (SUMIF, VLOOKUP,
   171	/// LARGE, ...) live here. The `is_aggregate_function_lists_only_registered_aggregates`
   172	/// test invariant pins the cross-table sync.
   173	///
   174	/// **Re-target:** the planned replacement with per-function metadata
   175	/// (`FunctionRegistry` attributes) is no longer Phase 4.3 work — Phase
   176	/// 4.3 wave 1 closed W5-58 with this matcher still in place. The right
   177	/// time to replace this is **Phase 4.7 (array formulas) or Phase 4.10
   178	/// (function library wave 2)**, whichever introduces richer per-
   179	/// function metadata first.
   180	///
   181	/// Aggregate detection is binary in V0 (all args are aggregate or no args
   182	/// are). Per-arg-position decisions (e.g. IF's then/else accept arrays in
   183	/// some contexts) land alongside Engine Phase 4.7 array formulas.
   184	pub(crate) fn is_aggregate_function(name: &str) -> bool {
   185	    // Phase 2B.7 audit (correctness M1): MEDIAN removed — it was listed
   186	    // here but not registered in `ql_functions::default_registry`. Keeping
   187	    // the list synced with the registry is a hard rule; the
   188	    // `is_aggregate_function_lists_only_registered_aggregates` test pins
   189	    // it. When Engine Phase 4.3 expands the function library, the metadata
   190	    // moves to per-function FunctionRegistry attributes and this hardcoded
   191	    // matcher goes away entirely.
   192	    //
   193	    // W5-53 (GAP-F-05 closure): SUMIF + COUNTIF are NOT scalar aggregates
   194	    // (they return a scalar but take range + criteria) — they live in the
   195	    // parallel `range_aware_fns` table. But the binder uses this list to
   196	    // decide whether `NamedRangeInScalarContext` should fire on
   197	    // `=SUMIF(NamedRange, 5)`. Since SUMIF NEEDS a range arg, we add it
   198	    // here so the binder allows the AggregateNameRef in arg position. The
   199	    // eval-side dispatch routes correctly via `lookup_range_aware` first.
   200	    matches!(
   201	        name,
   202	        "SUM"
   203	            | "AVERAGE"
   204	            | "AVG"
   205	            | "COUNT"
   206	            | "COUNTA"
   207	            | "MIN"
   208	            | "MAX"
   209	            | "PRODUCT"
   210	            | "VAR"
   211	            | "VAR.S"
   212	            | "VAR.P"
   213	            | "STDEV"
   214	            | "STDEV.S"
   215	            | "STDEV.P"
   216	            // Range-aware (W5-53): NOT scalar aggregates, but the binder
   217	            // treats them as aggregate-context for arg binding so named-
   218	            // range args resolve to `AggregateNameRef`.
   219	            | "SUMIF"
   220	            | "COUNTIF"
   221	            // Range-aware lookup family (W5-54): same reason — table
   222	            // args are range references that must bind as
   223	            // AggregateNameRef so the eval-side dispatch can construct
   224	            // `FnArg::Range { values, rows, cols }`.
   225	            | "MATCH"
   226	            | "INDEX"
   227	            | "VLOOKUP"
   228	            | "HLOOKUP"
   229	            | "CHOOSE"
   230	            // Range-aware conditional-aggregate completion (W5-55).
   231	            | "AVERAGEIF"
   232	            | "SUMIFS"
   233	            | "COUNTIFS"
   234	            | "AVERAGEIFS"
   235	            | "SUMPRODUCT"
   236	            // Range-aware stats family (W5-58, FN4-01 closure).
   237	            | "LARGE"
   238	            | "SMALL"
   239	            | "RANK"
   240	            | "RANK.EQ"
   241	            | "RANK.AVG"
   242	            | "MEDIAN"
   243	            | "MODE"
   244	            | "MODE.SNGL"
   245	            // Range-aware text completion (W5-61 polish).
   246	            | "CONCAT"
   247	    )
   248	}
   249	
   250	/// Resolve an Expr against an owning sheet, producing an ExprPlan. **No name
   251	/// resolution + no sheet resolution** — any `Expr::NameRef` produces
   252	/// `BindError::UnresolvedName`; any cross-sheet ref (`SheetRef::Name`)
   253	/// produces `BindError::UnknownSheet`.
   254	///
   255	/// `owning_sheet` is the sheet the formula lives on.
   256	pub fn bind(expr: &Expr, owning_sheet: SheetId) -> Result<ExprPlan, BindError> {
   257	    bind_with_names_and_sheets(expr, owning_sheet, &EmptyNameLookup, &EmptySheetResolver)
   258	}
   259	
   260	/// Phase 2A.1 — bind with a name resolver. **W5-90 update**: this entry
   261	/// point uses an `EmptySheetResolver`, so cross-sheet refs surface as
   262	/// `BindError::UnknownSheet`. Callers that need cross-sheet support
   263	/// should use [`bind_with_names_and_sheets`] and pass a real
   264	/// [`SheetResolver`] (typically `&Workbook` via the blanket impl).
   265	pub fn bind_with_names<L: NameLookup>(
   266	    expr: &Expr,
   267	    owning_sheet: SheetId,
   268	    names: &L,
   269	) -> Result<ExprPlan, BindError> {
   270	    bind_with_names_and_sheets(expr, owning_sheet, names, &EmptySheetResolver)
   271	}
   272	
   273	/// **W5-90 (Phase 4.6.B):** bind with BOTH name and sheet resolvers.
   274	/// Production callers (`WorkbookRuntime`, `WorkbookTransaction`,
   275	/// `CalcgraphSession`) pass `self.workbook.names()` for `names` and
   276	/// `self.workbook` for `sheets` (the blanket `SheetResolver for
   277	/// Workbook` impl in `env.rs` does the lookup via
   278	/// `Workbook::sheet_id_by_name`).
   279	pub fn bind_with_names_and_sheets<L: NameLookup>(
   280	    expr: &Expr,
   281	    owning_sheet: SheetId,
   282	    names: &L,
   283	    sheets: &dyn SheetResolver,
   284	) -> Result<ExprPlan, BindError> {
   285	    bind_with_context(expr, owning_sheet, names, sheets, BindContext::Scalar)
   286	}
   287	
   288	fn bind_with_context<L: NameLookup>(
   289	    expr: &Expr,
   290	    owning_sheet: SheetId,
   291	    names: &L,
   292	    sheets: &dyn SheetResolver,
   293	    ctx: BindContext,
   294	) -> Result<ExprPlan, BindError> {
   295	    match expr {
   296	        Expr::Number(n) => Ok(ExprPlan::Number(*n)),
   297	        Expr::Bool(b) => Ok(ExprPlan::Bool(*b)),
   298	        Expr::String(s) => Ok(ExprPlan::String(s.clone())),
   299	        Expr::CellRef(addr) => Ok(ExprPlan::CellRef {
   300	            sheet: resolve_sheet_ref(&addr.sheet, owning_sheet, sheets)?,
   301	            row: addr.row,
   302	            col: addr.col,
   303	            abs_col: addr.abs_col,
   304	            abs_row: addr.abs_row,
   305	        }),
   306	        Expr::Binary { op, lhs, rhs } => Ok(ExprPlan::Binary {
   307	            op: *op,
   308	            // Binary operands are always scalar context regardless of the
   309	            // outer context (Excel doesn't accept `=SUM(A1 + B1:B10)` —
   310	            // each operand to + is scalar).
   311	            lhs: Box::new(bind_with_context(
   312	                lhs,
   313	                owning_sheet,
   314	                names,
   315	                sheets,
   316	                BindContext::Scalar,
   317	            )?),
   318	            rhs: Box::new(bind_with_context(
   319	                rhs,
   320	                owning_sheet,
   321	                names,
   322	                sheets,
   323	                BindContext::Scalar,
   324	            )?),
   325	        }),
   326	        Expr::Unary { op, operand } => Ok(ExprPlan::Unary {
   327	            op: *op,
   328	            operand: Box::new(bind_with_context(
   329	                operand,
   330	                owning_sheet,
   331	                names,
   332	                sheets,
   333	                BindContext::Scalar,
   334	            )?),
   335	        }),
   336	        Expr::RangeRef(_) => Err(BindError::UnsupportedVariant(
   337	            "RangeRef requires a Function context; Phase 0 W4-1 has no function dispatch yet",
   338	        )),
   339	        Expr::Function { name, args } => {
   340	            // Phase 2B.4: switch context for aggregate-function arg lists.
   341	            let arg_ctx = if is_aggregate_function(name) {
   342	                BindContext::AggregateArg
   343	            } else {
   344	                BindContext::Scalar
   345	            };
   346	            let mut bound_args = Vec::with_capacity(args.len());
   347	            for a in args {
   348	                bound_args.push(bind_with_context(a, owning_sheet, names, sheets, arg_ctx)?);
   349	            }
   350	            Ok(ExprPlan::Function {
   351	                name: name.clone(),
   352	                args: bound_args,
   353	            })
   354	        }
   355	        Expr::Array(_) => Err(BindError::UnsupportedVariant("Array literals are Phase 3+")),
   356	        Expr::Spill(_) => Err(BindError::UnsupportedVariant("Spill anchors are Phase 3+")),
   357	        Expr::NameRef(name) => {
   358	            // Phase 2A.6 audit M1: parser-emitted `NameRef` must always carry a
   359	            // non-empty canonical name. An empty name here means the parser is
   360	            // broken; panic to surface the upstream bug per the no-fallbacks rule.

exec

 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	//! Cell-read environment for the scalar evaluator.
     2	//!
     3	//! The evaluator needs to look up cell values when it encounters `ExprPlan::CellRef`.
     4	//! Rather than coupling to `ql-storage::Workbook` directly, we abstract via a trait so:
     5	//!
     6	//! - Test harnesses can supply a `HashMap`-backed fake environment.
     7	//! - The Week 4+ runtime can wrap a real `Workbook` + `ColumnStore` chain.
     8	//! - Future cross-workbook references (Phase 6+) can implement the trait against an
     9	//!   external dataset adapter.
    10	//!
    11	//! The trait is intentionally narrow: just `read_cell`. Sheet-/column-bulk reads belong on
    12	//! `ql-storage` directly and the SIMD path (W4-2) doesn't go through this trait at all —
    13	//! it reads Arrow chunks via `ColumnStore::iter_chunks` for batch processing.
    14	
    15	use ql_storage::{NameTable, NamedTarget};
    16	use ql_types::{
    17	    ColId, ErrorValue, EvalContext, Range, RowId, SheetId, Value, DEFAULT_EVAL_CONTEXT,
    18	};
    19	
    20	use crate::plan::{NameLookup, ResolvedName};
    21	
    22	/// Read a single cell value. Out-of-bounds reads return `Value::Blank` per Excel semantics.
    23	pub trait CellEnv {
    24	    fn read_cell(&self, sheet: SheetId, row: RowId, col: ColId) -> Value;
    25	
    26	    /// Phase 3.6 (2026-05-12) — AGG-3-04 entry point. Materialize a range
    27	    /// into a flat `Vec<Value>` for aggregate functions (SUM, AVERAGE,
    28	    /// MIN, MAX, COUNT, PRODUCT). Default impl iterates the range row by
    29	    /// row using `read_cell`; impls that have cheaper bounds info (e.g.
    30	    /// `WorkbookEnv` clamps to `Sheet::bounds`) override.
    31	    ///
    32	    /// Returns an empty Vec for a degenerate range (e.g. range entirely
    33	    /// outside a sheet's populated area). The aggregate function then
    34	    /// applies its empty-input rule (MIN/MAX → 0; AVERAGE → `#DIV/0!`;
    35	    /// SUM/COUNT/PRODUCT → 0 or 1; see `ql-functions::scalar_fns`).
    36	    fn read_range(&self, range: Range) -> Vec<Value> {
    37	        let mut out = Vec::new();
    38	        for row in range.start_row..=range.end_row {
    39	            for col in range.start_col..=range.end_col {
    40	                out.push(self.read_cell(range.sheet, row, col));
    41	            }
    42	            // Safety: end_row could be `RowId::MAX` (whole-column); the
    43	            // outer range syntax must be bounded by the caller. The
    44	            // default impl iterates to completion — the WorkbookEnv
    45	            // override clamps to `Sheet::bounds().row_extent`.
    46	        }
    47	        out
    48	    }
    49	
    50	    /// **W5-54 (range-aware functions, GAP-F-05 follow-up):** return
    51	    /// the flat `Vec<Value>` for the range PLUS its 2D shape (rows,
    52	    /// cols). For range-aware functions like VLOOKUP/INDEX which
    53	    /// need to address by `(row, col)`, the dispatcher uses this to
    54	    /// construct `FnArg::Range { values, rows, cols }`.
    55	    ///
    56	    /// `rows * cols == values.len()` is the invariant. If the env
    57	    /// clamps to sheet bounds (`WorkbookEnv`), the returned shape
    58	    /// reflects the clamped dimensions. The default impl below
    59	    /// returns the unclamped shape derived from the range bounds —
    60	    /// matching the unclamped `read_range` default.
    61	    fn read_range_with_shape(&self, range: Range) -> (Vec<Value>, usize, usize) {
    62	        let values = self.read_range(range);
    63	        let rows = (range.end_row - range.start_row + 1) as usize;
    64	        let cols = (range.end_col - range.start_col + 1) as usize;
    65	        debug_assert_eq!(rows.saturating_mul(cols), values.len());
    66	        (values, rows, cols)
    67	    }
    68	
    69	    /// **W5-69 (Phase 4.5.A.0):** the evaluator context for date /
    70	    /// locale / clock-aware function dispatch. Default impl returns
    71	    /// `&DEFAULT_EVAL_CONTEXT` (Excel1900 + EnUs + System), suitable
    72	    /// for tests, MapEnv, and benches that don't carry workbook state.
    73	    /// `WorkbookEnv` overrides this with the workbook's actual
    74	    /// `date_system` field once Sub-phase 4.5.A adds it.
    75	    fn eval_context(&self) -> &EvalContext {
    76	        &DEFAULT_EVAL_CONTEXT
    77	    }
    78	}
    79	
    80	/// `ql-storage::Workbook`-backed implementation. Wraps a Workbook reference; reads dispatch
    81	/// via the Workbook's sheet lookup + the sheet's `read(row, col)`.
    82	///
    83	/// **W5-71 (Phase 4.5.A.2):** caches an `EvalContext` built from the
    84	/// workbook's `date_system` at construction time. Locale + NowProvider
    85	/// stay at their defaults (`EnUs` + `System`) — Phase 4.9 + future
    86	/// Phase-6.3 WASM bindings will plumb those through too.
    87	pub struct WorkbookEnv<'w> {
    88	    workbook: &'w ql_storage::Workbook,
    89	    eval_ctx: EvalContext,
    90	}
    91	
    92	impl<'w> WorkbookEnv<'w> {
    93	    pub fn new(workbook: &'w ql_storage::Workbook) -> Self {
    94	        let eval_ctx = EvalContext {
    95	            date_system: workbook.date_system(),
    96	            ..EvalContext::default()
    97	        };
    98	        Self { workbook, eval_ctx }
    99	    }
   100	}
   101	
   102	impl<'w> CellEnv for WorkbookEnv<'w> {
   103	    fn read_cell(&self, sheet: SheetId, row: RowId, col: ColId) -> Value {
   104	        // Phase 2A.7 audit H6 (2026-05-12): out-of-bounds sheet now surfaces
   105	        // `Value::Error(ErrorValue::Ref)` — Excel's canonical `#REF!`. Prior
   106	        // behavior silently mapped missing-sheet to `Value::Blank`, which
   107	        // hid stale-sheet refs in saved formulas (e.g., a formula authored
   108	        // against Sheet3 in a workbook later truncated to 2 sheets). Two
   109	        // megaudit agents flagged the silent fallback. Within an existing
   110	        // sheet, missing cells still return `Blank` — that's correct Excel
   111	        // canon for "empty cell."
   112	        match self.workbook.sheet(sheet) {
   113	            Some(s) => s.read(row, col),
   114	            None => Value::Error(ErrorValue::Ref),
   115	        }
   116	    }
   117	
   118	    /// Phase 3.6 override: clamps the iteration to `Sheet::bounds` so a
   119	    /// `SUM(A:A)` named range with `end_row = RowId::MAX` reads only the
   120	    /// populated rows (typically a few hundred thousand at most on a
   121	    /// real sheet), not all 4 billion `RowId` slots. Without this clamp
   122	    /// AGG-3-04 would correctness-pass but AGG-3-03 (full-column-still-
   123	    /// usable) would hang at evaluation time.
   124	    fn read_range(&self, range: Range) -> Vec<Value> {
   125	        let Some(sheet) = self.workbook.sheet(range.sheet) else {
   126	            return vec![Value::Error(ErrorValue::Ref)];
   127	        };
   128	        let bounds = sheet.bounds();
   129	        // bounds extents are "one past max"; convert to inclusive bounds.
   130	        if bounds.row_extent == 0 || bounds.col_extent == 0 {
   131	            return Vec::new();
   132	        }
   133	        let max_row = bounds.row_extent - 1;
   134	        let max_col = bounds.col_extent - 1;
   135	        let end_row = range.end_row.min(max_row);
   136	        let end_col = range.end_col.min(max_col);
   137	        if range.start_row > end_row || range.start_col > end_col {
   138	            return Vec::new();
   139	        }
   140	        let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(
   141	            ((end_row - range.start_row + 1) as usize)
   142	                .saturating_mul((end_col - range.start_col + 1) as usize),
   143	        );
   144	        for row in range.start_row..=end_row {
   145	            for col in range.start_col..=end_col {
   146	                out.push(sheet.read(row, col));
   147	            }
   148	        }
   149	        out
   150	    }
   151	
   152	    /// W5-54 / W5-60: shape handling for explicit vs open-ended ranges.
   153	    ///
   154	    /// Two cases:
   155	    /// - **Open-ended** (whole-column / whole-row, end_row or end_col at
   156	    ///   `RowId::MAX` / `ColId::MAX`): clamp to sheet bounds. Shape and
   157	    ///   values reflect the populated subset. This is the W5-54 behavior
   158	    ///   for aggregate scans like `SUM(A:A)` over a sparse column.
   159	    /// - **Bounded explicit** (every coordinate < MAX): preserve the
   160	    ///   REQUESTED shape; pad out-of-bounds cells with `Value::Blank`.
   161	    ///   W5-60 fix per the W5-49→W5-58 mega-audit: shape-aware functions
   162	    ///   (INDEX / VLOOKUP / HLOOKUP / SUMIFS / SUMPRODUCT) rely on the
   163	    ///   shape matching the user's explicit range bounds. The W5-54
   164	    ///   clamp behavior produced `#REF!` for `INDEX(A1:B10, 10, 2)` when
   165	    ///   row 10 was blank because shape shrank to the populated subset.
   166	    fn read_range_with_shape(&self, range: Range) -> (Vec<Value>, usize, usize) {
   167	        let Some(sheet) = self.workbook.sheet(range.sheet) else {
   168	            return (vec![Value::Error(ErrorValue::Ref)], 1, 1);
   169	        };
   170	        let is_open_ended =
   171	            range.end_row == ql_types::RowId::MAX || range.end_col == ql_types::ColId::MAX;
   172	        if is_open_ended {
   173	            // Clamp to bounds (existing W5-54 behavior for SUM(A:A)
   174	            // and similar whole-column / whole-row aggregate scans).
   175	            let bounds = sheet.bounds();
   176	            if bounds.row_extent == 0 || bounds.col_extent == 0 {
   177	                return (Vec::new(), 0, 0);
   178	            }
   179	            let max_row = bounds.row_extent - 1;
   180	            let max_col = bounds.col_extent - 1;
   181	            let end_row = range.end_row.min(max_row);
   182	            let end_col = range.end_col.min(max_col);
   183	            if range.start_row > end_row || range.start_col > end_col {
   184	                return (Vec::new(), 0, 0);
   185	            }
   186	            let rows = (end_row - range.start_row + 1) as usize;
   187	            let cols = (end_col - range.start_col + 1) as usize;
   188	            let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(rows.saturating_mul(cols));
   189	            for row in range.start_row..=end_row {
   190	                for col in range.start_col..=end_col {
   191	                    out.push(sheet.read(row, col));
   192	                }
   193	            }
   194	            (out, rows, cols)
   195	        } else {
   196	            // Bounded explicit range: preserve requested shape; pad
   197	            // out-of-bounds cells with Blank. The user asked for
   198	            // exactly A1:B10; even if only A1:A5 is populated, the
   199	            // shape must remain 10×2 so INDEX / VLOOKUP /
   200	            // SUMIFS / SUMPRODUCT see the layout the user authored.
   201	            let rows = (range.end_row - range.start_row + 1) as usize;
   202	            let cols = (range.end_col - range.start_col + 1) as usize;
   203	            let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(rows.saturating_mul(cols));
   204	            for row in range.start_row..=range.end_row {
   205	                for col in range.start_col..=range.end_col {
   206	                    out.push(sheet.read(row, col));
   207	                }
   208	            }
   209	            (out, rows, cols)
   210	        }
   211	    }
   212	
   213	    /// **W5-71 (Phase 4.5.A.2):** return the cached `EvalContext` built
   214	    /// from the workbook's `date_system` at WorkbookEnv construction.
   215	    /// Overrides the trait default (which returns
   216	    /// `&DEFAULT_EVAL_CONTEXT`).
   217	    fn eval_context(&self) -> &EvalContext {
   218	        &self.eval_ctx
   219	    }
   220	}
   221	
   222	/// HashMap-backed env for tests + simple harnesses. Stores `((sheet, row, col), Value)`
   223	/// triples; missing keys return `Value::Blank` per Excel semantics.
   224	#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default)]
   225	pub struct MapEnv {
   226	    cells: std::collections::HashMap<(SheetId, RowId, ColId), Value>,
   227	}
   228	
   229	impl MapEnv {
   230	    pub fn new() -> Self {
   231	        Self::default()
   232	    }
   233	
   234	    pub fn put(&mut self, sheet: SheetId, row: RowId, col: ColId, value: Value) {
   235	        self.cells.insert((sheet, row, col), value);
   236	    }
   237	}
   238	
   239	impl CellEnv for MapEnv {
   240	    fn read_cell(&self, sheet: SheetId, row: RowId, col: ColId) -> Value {
   241	        self.cells
   242	            .get(&(sheet, row, col))
   243	            .cloned()
   244	            .unwrap_or(Value::Blank)
   245	    }
   246	}

   248	// Phase 2A.1 (2026-05-12): NameTable → NameLookup wiring so the binder can resolve
   249	// `Expr::NameRef` against the workbook's name table. Keeps ql-exec's `plan` module
   250	// agnostic to ql-storage (the trait lives there); this impl bridges them in env.rs
   251	// where ql-storage is already imported.
   252	//
   253	// Phase 2A.6 audit H2: use `lookup_ci` so the binder is robust against callers
   254	// who bypass parser-side canonicalization. The parser already uppercases names in
   255	// `Expr::NameRef`, so this is a safety net for hand-constructed ASTs / tests; it
   256	// removes a case-sensitivity footgun without breaking any happy path.
   257	impl NameLookup for NameTable {
   258	    /// Legacy single-table lookup. Ignores `owning_sheet` — used by tests
   259	    /// that don't need the sheet-scoped chain, and by the legacy
   260	    /// `bind_with_names` entry point. Production callers should pass
   261	    /// `&Workbook` (see the blanket impl below) so sheet-scoped names
   262	    /// resolve correctly per Phase 4.6.D § 7.3.
   263	    fn lookup_named_target(
   264	        &self,
   265	        name: &str,
   266	        _owning_sheet: ql_types::SheetId,
   267	    ) -> Option<ResolvedName> {
   268	        named_target_to_resolved(self.lookup_ci(name)?)
   269	    }
   270	}
   271	
   272	/// **W5-92 (Phase 4.6.D):** the production `NameLookup` impl —
   273	/// `&Workbook` does the two-tier sheet-then-workbook chain per
   274	/// design doc § 7.3:
   275	///
   276	/// 1. If `owning_sheet`'s `scoped_names` table has the name, return it.
   277	/// 2. Else, fall back to the workbook-scoped `NameTable`.
   278	///
   279	/// This matches Excel's resolution rule: sheet-scoped names shadow
   280	/// workbook-scoped names with the same identifier when accessed from a
   281	/// formula on that sheet. The production binder call sites pass
   282	/// `&self.workbook` for the `names` argument; legacy callers that
   283	/// passed `self.workbook.names()` get workbook-only behavior (no chain
   284	/// walk) and won't see sheet-scoped names.
   285	impl NameLookup for ql_storage::Workbook {
   286	    fn lookup_named_target(
   287	        &self,
   288	        name: &str,
   289	        owning_sheet: ql_types::SheetId,
   290	    ) -> Option<ResolvedName> {
   291	        // Tier 1: sheet-scoped lookup against the owning sheet.
   292	        if let Some(sheet) = self.sheet(owning_sheet) {
   293	            if let Some(target) = sheet.scoped_names().lookup_ci(name) {
   294	                return named_target_to_resolved(target);
   295	            }
   296	        }
   297	        // Tier 2: workbook-scoped fallback.
   298	        named_target_to_resolved(self.names().lookup_ci(name)?)
   299	    }
   300	}
   301	
   302	/// **W5-90 (Phase 4.6.B):** the production `SheetResolver` impl —
   303	/// `&Workbook` resolves sheet names via `Workbook::sheet_id_by_name`
   304	/// (the canonicalizing case-insensitive lookup added in W5-86).
   305	/// Production callers (`WorkbookRuntime`, `WorkbookTransaction`,
   306	/// `CalcgraphSession`) pass `&self.workbook` as the `&dyn SheetResolver`
   307	/// argument to `bind_with_names_and_sheets`.
   308	impl crate::plan::SheetResolver for ql_storage::Workbook {
   309	    fn resolve_sheet(&self, name: &str) -> Option<ql_types::SheetId> {
   310	        self.sheet_id_by_name(name)
   311	    }
   312	}
   313	
   314	/// Project a `NamedTarget` into the binder-side `ResolvedName` vocabulary. Phase
   315	/// 2A.6 audit M2/M3: `Constant(Blank)` and `Constant(Error)` now map to distinct
   316	/// `ResolvedName` variants (the binder converts them to specific `BindError`
   317	/// kinds) — previously they were silently coerced to `Text("")` and `None`
   318	/// respectively, both of which violate the no-fallbacks rule.
   319	fn named_target_to_resolved(target: NamedTarget) -> Option<ResolvedName> {
   320	    match target {

 succeeded in 0ms:
   360	            // broken; panic to surface the upstream bug per the no-fallbacks rule.
   361	            assert!(
   362	                !name.is_empty(),
   363	                "bind_with_context: Expr::NameRef carries an empty string — parser invariant violated"
   364	            );
   365	            // Phase 2A.1: resolve the name via the active NameTable; Phase 2A.6
   366	            // audit H2 uses case-insensitive lookup in the `NameTable` impl so
   367	            // every entry point canonicalizes uniformly.
   368	            match names.lookup_named_target(name, owning_sheet) {
   369	                Some(ResolvedName::Cell(sheet, row, col, abs_col, abs_row)) => {
   370	                    Ok(ExprPlan::CellRef {
   371	                        sheet,
   372	                        row,
   373	                        col,
   374	                        abs_col,
   375	                        abs_row,
   376	                    })
   377	                }
   378	                Some(ResolvedName::Number(n)) => Ok(ExprPlan::Number(n)),
   379	                Some(ResolvedName::Bool(b)) => Ok(ExprPlan::Bool(b)),
   380	                Some(ResolvedName::Text(s)) => Ok(ExprPlan::String(s)),
   381	                // Phase 2B.4: context-aware Range handling.
   382	                Some(ResolvedName::Range(range)) => match ctx {
   383	                    BindContext::AggregateArg => Ok(ExprPlan::AggregateNameRef {
   384	                        name: name.clone(),
   385	                        range,
   386	                    }),
   387	                    BindContext::Scalar => Err(BindError::NamedRangeInScalarContext(name.clone())),
   388	                },
   389	                // Phase 2B.4: named formulas still unsupported (Engine Phase 4).
   390	                // We surface a distinct error rather than the generic
   391	                // UnsupportedVariant so the IDE can render a specific hint.
   392	                Some(ResolvedName::Formula(_)) => {
   393	                    Err(BindError::NamedFormulaUnsupported(name.clone()))
   394	                }
   395	                // Phase 2A.6 audit M2/M3: surface Blank- and Error-targets loudly.
   396	                Some(ResolvedName::Blank) => Err(BindError::NamedTargetIsBlank(name.clone())),
   397	                Some(ResolvedName::ErrorValue(e)) => {
   398	                    Err(BindError::NamedTargetIsError(name.clone(), e))
   399	                }
   400	                None => Err(BindError::UnresolvedName(name.clone())),
   401	            }
   402	        }
   403	    }
   404	}
   405	
   406	/// Variant describing a resolved name's target, mapped from `ql_storage::NamedTarget`.
   407	/// Phase 2A.1: this is the binder's intermediate vocabulary — the binder doesn't
   408	/// import ql-storage directly, so the lookup trait projects NamedTarget into this
   409	/// enum first. Phase 2 supports Cell + Constant; Range and Formula return
   410	/// unsupported errors.
   411	///
   412	/// Phase 2A.6 audit M2/M3: added `Blank` and `ErrorValue` so the binder can
   413	/// surface them as distinct `BindError` variants instead of either coercing
   414	/// silently or returning the wrong error class.
   415	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
   416	pub enum ResolvedName {
   417	    Cell(SheetId, RowId, ColId, bool, bool),
   418	    Number(f64),
   419	    Bool(bool),
   420	    Text(Arc<str>),
   421	    /// `NamedTarget::Range(_)` resolved with payload. Phase 2B.4 (2026-05-12):
   422	    /// previously carried no data; now threads the underlying `Range` so the
   423	    /// binder can pre-resolve into `ExprPlan::AggregateNameRef` without
   424	    /// asking the name table again at eval time.
   425	    Range(Range),
   426	    /// `NamedTarget::Formula(_)` resolved with payload. Phase 2B.4 added the
   427	    /// formula text payload; the binder rejects this for now (named-formula
   428	    /// resolution is Engine Phase 4) but the shape is in place.
   429	    Formula(Arc<str>),
   430	    /// `NamedTarget::Constant(Value::Blank)`. The binder rejects this loudly so
   431	    /// the caller can fix the data binding.
   432	    Blank,
   433	    /// `NamedTarget::Constant(Value::Error(...))`. The binder rejects with a
   434	    /// distinct error carrying the underlying `ErrorValue`.
   435	    ErrorValue(ErrorValue),
   436	}
   437	
   438	/// Name-resolution interface. `ql-exec` stays decoupled from `ql-storage`;
   439	/// the production caller wires a `Workbook` through this trait.
   440	///
   441	/// **W5-92 (Phase 4.6.D):** the trait now takes `owning_sheet` so impls
   442	/// that hold scoped tables (the `Workbook` blanket impl in
   443	/// `env.rs`) can run the two-tier chain: sheet-scoped first, then
   444	/// workbook-scoped. Impls without a sheet axis (legacy
   445	/// `NameLookup for NameTable`, `EmptyNameLookup`) ignore the parameter.
   446	pub trait NameLookup {
   447	    fn lookup_named_target(&self, name: &str, owning_sheet: SheetId) -> Option<ResolvedName>;
   448	}
   449	
   450	/// Default empty-lookup resolver used by the legacy `bind()` entry point. Always
   451	/// returns `None`, so any NameRef becomes `BindError::UnresolvedName`.
   452	struct EmptyNameLookup;
   453	
   454	impl NameLookup for EmptyNameLookup {
   455	    fn lookup_named_target(&self, _name: &str, _owning_sheet: SheetId) -> Option<ResolvedName> {
   456	        None
   457	    }
   458	}
   459	
   460	/// **W5-90 (Phase 4.6.B):** sheet-name resolution interface. Mirrors
   461	/// `NameLookup`: `ql-exec` stays decoupled from `ql-storage`; the
   462	/// production caller wires a `Workbook` through this trait. Used by
   463	/// the binder to resolve `SheetRef::Name(N)` → `SheetRef::Id(id)`.
   464	///
   465	/// Lookup contract: case-insensitive (per `Workbook::canonical_sheet_name`).
   466	pub trait SheetResolver {
   467	    fn resolve_sheet(&self, name: &str) -> Option<SheetId>;
   468	}
   469	
   470	/// Default empty-resolver. Used by the legacy `bind()` / `bind_with_names()`
   471	/// entry points: any cross-sheet ref surfaces as `BindError::UnknownSheet`.
   472	pub(crate) struct EmptySheetResolver;
   473	
   474	impl SheetResolver for EmptySheetResolver {
   475	    fn resolve_sheet(&self, _name: &str) -> Option<SheetId> {
   476	        None
   477	    }
   478	}
   479	
   480	/// **W5-90 (Phase 4.6.B):** resolve a `SheetRef` for the binder.
   481	///
   482	/// - `Current` → `owning_sheet` (the formula's own sheet).
   483	/// - `Id(s)` → `s` (already resolved; produced post-bind or by tests).
   484	/// - `Name(n)` → lookup via `sheets.resolve_sheet(n)`; `None` →
   485	///   `BindError::UnknownSheet`.
   486	fn resolve_sheet_ref(
   487	    sheet: &SheetRef,
   488	    owning_sheet: SheetId,
   489	    sheets: &dyn SheetResolver,
   490	) -> Result<SheetId, BindError> {
   491	    match sheet {
   492	        SheetRef::Current => Ok(owning_sheet),
   493	        SheetRef::Id(s) => Ok(*s),
   494	        SheetRef::Name(n) => sheets
   495	            .resolve_sheet(n)
   496	            .ok_or_else(|| BindError::UnknownSheet(n.clone())),
   497	    }
   498	}

   683	        map: std::collections::HashMap<String, SheetId>,
   684	    }
   685	    impl MockSheetResolver {
   686	        fn new(pairs: &[(&str, SheetId)]) -> Self {
   687	            Self {
   688	                map: pairs
   689	                    .iter()
   690	                    .map(|(n, s)| (n.to_ascii_uppercase(), *s))
   691	                    .collect(),
   692	            }
   693	        }
   694	    }

   696	        fn resolve_sheet(&self, name: &str) -> Option<SheetId> {
   697	            self.map.get(&name.to_ascii_uppercase()).copied()
   698	        }
   699	    }

     1	//! `WorkbookRuntime` — the live-formula facade per Phase 1 W5-10.
     2	//!
     3	//! Ties the lex → parse → bind → eval → put pipeline together so callers (IDE,
     4	//! tests, future REPL) get one method that does the right thing for a user-typed
     5	//! formula. Plus a `recompute_all` method that re-evaluates every formula cell in
     6	//! the workbook — used after loading a `.qbook/` directory where formula values
     7	//! were left as `#NULL!` sentinels.
     8	//!
     9	//! Phase 1 W5-10 scope:
    10	//! - `set_formula(sheet, row, col, text)` — parse + eval + persist formula text +
    11	//!   evaluated value. Phase 3.5: routes the evaluated value to the COMPUTED
    12	//!   overlay; clears any stale user-overlay entry at the cell first.
    13	//! - `set_value(sheet, row, col, value)` — literal-only write; clears any existing
    14	//!   formula association. Phase 3.5: `clear_formula` cascades to drop the
    15	//!   computed-overlay entry too.
    16	//! - `recompute_all()` — legacy HashMap-order full pass; no graph awareness.
    17	//! - `recompute_dirty()` (Phase 3.4 W5-37) — incremental graph-driven recompute.
    18	//!   Runs Tarjan SCC over the attached `CalcgraphSession`'s dirty set; cycled
    19	//!   members get `Value::Error(ErrorValue::Circ)`. Phase 3.6 routes aggregates
    20	//!   through the session's `InMemAggregateCache`; Phase 3.8 applies value-
    21	//!   equality short-circuit; Phase 3.9 V1 tallies SIMD-eligible formulas in
    22	//!   `RecomputeResult.simd_classified`.
    23	//! - `validate_formula(sheet, row, col, text)` (Phase 2B.7) — full pipeline
    24	//!   without mutation; IDE on-keystroke validation entry.
    25	//!
    26	//! Phase 4+ deferred (per Phase 3.10 megaudit + W5-48 Codex deep-audit):
    27	//! - GAP-G-01 / GAP-G-03 — append-only graph rebind staleness + range-deps-
    28	//!   not-scheduler-edges. Architectural decision needed before Phase 4.3 V2.
    29	//! - GAP-G-02 — bulk SIMD region dispatch from graph scheduler (V1 is
    30	//!   observability only).
    31	//! - FN4-03 — lazy IF/IFERROR (needs scalar.rs Function-branch refactor).
    32	//! - Cross-sheet formula references via NameTable resolution (Phase 4.6).

    35	
    36	use ql_formula_syntax::{lex, parse, LexError, ParseError};
    37	use ql_functions::format::{self, FormatString};
    38	use ql_functions::FunctionRegistry;
