  1348	                return Err(QbookError::MalformedCell {
  1349	                    file: sheet_path.clone(),
  1350	                    line: line_no + 1,
  1351	                    detail: format!("row {} exceeds MAX_ROW {MAX_ROW}", rec.row),
  1352	                });
  1353	            }
  1354	            if rec.col > MAX_COLUMN {
  1355	                return Err(QbookError::MalformedCell {
  1356	                    file: sheet_path.clone(),
  1357	                    line: line_no + 1,
  1358	                    detail: format!("col {} exceeds MAX_COLUMN {MAX_COLUMN}", rec.col),
  1359	                });
  1360	            }
  1361	            // Phase 2A.8 audit M11: detect the v1 legacy encoding of a
  1362	            // formula-bearing Blank cell (`Error("#NULL!")` + formula) and
  1363	            // rewrite it to the v2 Pending semantic during the v1 load path.
  1364	            // v2 files emit `Pending` directly and skip this branch.
  1365	            //
  1366	            // Phase 2A.13 audit cycle-3 H4: this rule cannot distinguish
  1367	            // "engine wrote #NULL! as Pending stand-in" from "user's formula
  1368	            // legitimately evaluated to #NULL!" (Excel's intersection-of-
  1369	            // disjoint-ranges error, e.g. `=SUM(A1 B1)` with a space). Both
  1370	            // encode identically in v1. We count migrations and warn ONCE
  1371	            // per load at the end so the user can audit suspect formulas
  1372	            // rather than silently corrupting real #NULL! results.
  1373	            let is_legacy_pending = is_v1
  1374	                && rec.formula.is_some()
  1375	                && matches!(&rec.value, CellWireValue::Error(s) if s == "#NULL!");
  1376	            if is_legacy_pending {
  1377	                legacy_pending_migrations += 1;
  1378	            }
  1379	
  1380	            let value = if rec.value.is_pending() || is_legacy_pending {
  1381	                // Pending cells contribute no Value to the cell — leave it
  1382	                // Blank until recompute runs. The formula text below is what
  1383	                // matters for the recompute pass.
  1384	                Value::Blank
  1385	            } else {
  1386	                rec.value.to_value().map_err(|e| match e {
  1387	                    QbookError::MalformedCell { detail, .. } => QbookError::MalformedCell {
  1388	                        file: sheet_path.clone(),
  1389	                        line: line_no + 1,
  1390	                        detail,
  1391	                    },
  1392	                    other => other,
  1393	                })?
  1394	            };
  1395	            // Phase 3.5 (CORR-25, 2026-05-12) — OVR-3-03: load routes
  1396	            // cells with formulas to the COMPUTED overlay; cells without
  1397	            // formulas go to the USER overlay. This preserves the
  1398	            // engine's semantic invariant ("formula-owned cell has no
  1399	            // user-overlay entry"). Pending/Blank values are skipped on
  1400	            // the formula path because there's nothing to display until
  1401	            // recompute runs; the formula text alone is enough to drive
  1402	            // the recompute pass.
  1403	            let row = rec.row as RowId;
  1404	            let col = rec.col as ColId;
  1405	            if let Some(formula_text) = rec.formula {
  1406	                wb.put_formula(sheet_id, row, col, formula_text);
  1407	                if !matches!(value, Value::Blank) {
  1408	                    wb.put_computed_at(sheet_id, row, col, value);
  1409	                }
  1410	            } else {
  1411	                wb.put_at(sheet_id, row, col, value);
  1412	            }
  1413	        }
  1414	    }
  1415	
  1416	    // Phase 2A.8: hydrate the workbook's NameTable from the envelope's
  1417	    // `names` section. v1 envelopes lack the section (envelope.names is None);
  1418	    // those workbooks load with an empty NameTable, matching prior behavior.
  1419	    //
  1420	    // Phase 2A.9 audit M6: `Workbook::set_name` may now refuse reserved names
  1421	    // (currently `AI` per CORR-06). A v2 file that somehow contains a reserved
  1422	    // name (e.g., hand-edited TOML) surfaces as `QbookError::MalformedName`.
  1423	    // **W5-92 (Phase 4.6.D):** route by scope. `scope: None` (the v1-v4
  1424	    // historical case) lands in the workbook-scoped `NameTable`;
  1425	    // `scope: Some(id)` lands in that sheet's `scoped_names` table. An
  1426	    // unknown sheet id surfaces as `MalformedName` (the load-time
  1427	    // analog of the op-log replay's `InvalidSheet` — we can't carry the
  1428	    // structural error through `QbookError`'s vocabulary without a new
  1429	    // variant, and `MalformedName` is the right shape for "name's
  1430	    // target is bad").
  1431	    if let Some(names_section) = envelope.names {
  1432	        for entry in names_section.entries {
  1433	            let target = entry.target.to_target(&entry.name)?;
  1434	            match entry.scope {
  1435	                None => {
  1436	                    wb.set_name(&entry.name, target)
  1437	                        .map_err(|e| QbookError::MalformedName {
  1438	                            name: entry.name.clone(),
  1439	                            reason: format!("rejected by workbook NameTable: {e}"),
  1440	                        })?;
  1441	                }
  1442	                Some(sheet_id) => {
  1443	                    let sheet =
  1444	                        wb.sheet_mut(sheet_id)
  1445	                            .ok_or_else(|| QbookError::MalformedName {
  1446	                                name: entry.name.clone(),
  1447	                                reason: format!("scope refers to unknown sheet id {sheet_id}"),
  1448	                            })?;
  1449	                    sheet.set_scoped_name(&entry.name, target).map_err(|e| {
  1450	                        QbookError::MalformedName {
  1451	                            name: entry.name.clone(),
  1452	                            reason: format!("rejected by sheet {sheet_id} NameTable: {e}"),
  1453	                        }
  1454	                    })?;
  1455	                }
  1456	            }
  1457	        }
  1458	    }
  1459	
  1460	    // Phase 2A.13 audit cycle-3 H4: warn once per load if we rewrote any v1
  1461	    // `Error("#NULL!") + formula` cells to Pending. The rewrite is correct
  1462	    // for engine-produced files (where #NULL! was a stand-in for "not yet
  1463	    // evaluated") but COULD silently corrupt legitimate Excel #NULL! results
  1464	    // — the v1 wire format conflated both cases. The user should re-check
  1465	    // any formulas that legitimately produce #NULL! after this load.
  1466	    if legacy_pending_migrations > 0 {
  1467	        eprintln!(
  1468	            "warning: load_workbook {path:?} migrated {legacy_pending_migrations} v1 \
  1469	             `Error(#NULL!) + formula` cells to the v2 Pending semantic. \
  1470	             If any of those formulas were authored to produce #NULL! intentionally \
  1471	             (e.g. `=SUM(A1 B1)` with a space), their evaluated values will be lost \
  1472	             until recompute. Re-save the workbook in v2 format to remove this ambiguity."
  1473	        );
  1474	    }
  1475	
  1476	    Ok(wb)
  1477	}
  1478	
  1479	/// Read the actual `chunk_rows` from a sheet. Audit H5 fix (2026-05-12): uses the
  1480	/// `Sheet::chunk_rows()` accessor (added in W5-7) so the envelope reflects the
  1481	/// real layout instead of the env default.
  1482	fn sheet_chunk_rows(sheet: &Sheet) -> u32 {
  1483	    sheet.chunk_rows()
  1484	}

     1	//! Phase 2A.3.a integration test — producer-replay equivalence.
     2	//!
     3	//! The load-bearing invariant for the op log: any sequence of state
     4	//! mutations that we'd record as a sequence of `Op` values, when replayed
     5	//! against a fresh `Workbook`, produces the same observable workbook
     6	//! state as the producer side achieved by direct mutation.
     7	//!
     8	//! This test mocks the producer side (we don't have `WorkbookRuntime`
     9	//! integration yet — that's 2A.3.b). Instead, the test:
    10	//!
    11	//! 1. Builds a sequence of `Op` values matching a realistic IDE session
    12	//!    (paste block + named-range + multi-sheet).
    13	//! 2. Replays them against a fresh workbook (state A).
    14	//! 3. Manually applies the equivalent state mutations to a SECOND fresh
    15	//!    workbook (state B), using the same `Workbook` APIs that replay uses
    16	//!    internally.
    17	//! 4. Asserts the two workbooks are observationally equal on every cell +
    18	//!    formula + name that either touches.
    19	//!
    20	//! Future commits (2A.3.b) will replace step 3 with real producer-side
    21	//! op recording from `WorkbookRuntime` / `WorkbookTransaction`. The test
    22	//! shape stays the same; the producer just becomes "real" instead of
    23	//! "synthetic by hand."
    24	
    25	use ql_functions::default_registry;
    26	use ql_io::{CellWireValue, NamedTargetWire};
    27	use ql_oplog::{replay_into, Op, OpLog};
    28	use ql_storage::{NamedTarget, Workbook};
    29	use ql_types::{Address, Value};
    30	
    31	/// Build a realistic op sequence: paste 5x5 block of literals, add a
    32	/// formula referencing one of them, register a named constant, add a
    33	/// second sheet, write a value on it.
    34	fn realistic_op_sequence() -> Vec<Op> {
    35	    let mut ops = Vec::new();
    36	    // 5x5 paste block on sheet 0.
    37	    for r in 0..5u32 {
    38	        for c in 0..5u32 {
    39	            ops.push(Op::PutValue {
    40	                sheet: 0,
    41	                row: r,
    42	                col: c,
    43	                value: CellWireValue::Number((r * 10 + c) as f64),
    44	            });
    45	        }
    46	    }
    47	    // Formula at (5, 0).
    48	    ops.push(Op::PutFormula {
    49	        sheet: 0,
    50	        row: 5,
    51	        col: 0,
    52	        text: "A1 + B2 + C3".to_owned(),
    53	    });
    54	    // Named constant.
    55	    ops.push(Op::SetName {
    56	        scope: None,
    57	        name: "TaxRate".to_owned(),
    58	        target: NamedTargetWire::Constant {
    59	            value: CellWireValue::Number(0.21),
    60	        },
    61	    });
    62	    // Second sheet.
    63	    ops.push(Op::AddSheet {
    64	        name: "Inventory".to_owned(),
    65	        chunk_rows: 16384,
    66	    });
    67	    // Value on sheet 1.
    68	    ops.push(Op::PutValue {
    69	        sheet: 1,
    70	        row: 0,
    71	        col: 0,
    72	        value: CellWireValue::Text("widget".to_owned()),
    73	    });
    74	    // **W5-84 closure (Sonnet MEDIUM D.3):** include format ops in the
    75	    // realistic sequence so the equivalence comparator exercises the
    76	    // FormatTable + CellFormatOverlay paths.
    77	    //
    78	    // Register a custom format id (164 = first custom).
    79	    ops.push(Op::RegisterFormat {
    80	        id: 164,
    81	        string: "\"€\" #,##0.00".to_owned(),
    82	    });
    83	    // Bind a cell on sheet 0 to a built-in id 14 (m/d/yyyy) — no
    84	    // RegisterFormat needed; the built-in is pre-seeded by
    85	    // `FormatTable::default()`.
    86	    ops.push(Op::SetCellFormat {
    87	        sheet: 0,
    88	        row: 0,
    89	        col: 0,
    90	        id: Some(14),
    91	    });
    92	    // Bind another cell to the custom id.
    93	    ops.push(Op::SetCellFormat {
    94	        sheet: 0,
    95	        row: 1,
    96	        col: 0,
    97	        id: Some(164),
    98	    });
    99	    // And clear a binding (Op::SetCellFormat with None on a previously-
   100	    // bound cell). Tests the clear path.
   101	    ops.push(Op::SetCellFormat {
   102	        sheet: 0,
   103	        row: 0,
   104	        col: 0,
   105	        id: None,
   106	    });
   107	    ops
   108	}
   109	
   110	/// Mocks the producer side by applying ops directly to a workbook via the
   111	/// same APIs that replay uses internally. In 2A.3.b this becomes the
   112	/// real `WorkbookRuntime` + `WorkbookTransaction` calls; the public
   113	/// invariant is unchanged.
   114	fn apply_producer_side(ops: &[Op], wb: &mut Workbook) {
   115	    for op in ops {
   116	        match op {
   117	            Op::PutValue {
   118	                sheet,
   119	                row,
   120	                col,
   121	                value,
   122	            } => {
   123	                let v = value.to_value().unwrap();
   124	                wb.put_at(*sheet, *row, *col, v);
   125	            }
   126	            Op::PutFormula {
   127	                sheet,
   128	                row,
   129	                col,
   130	                text,
   131	            } => {
   132	                wb.put_formula(*sheet, *row, *col, text.as_str());
   133	            }
   134	            Op::ClearFormula { sheet, row, col } => {
   135	                wb.clear_formula(*sheet, *row, *col);
   136	            }
   137	            Op::SetName {
   138	                scope,
   139	                name,
   140	                target,
   141	            } => {
   142	                let t = target.to_target(name).unwrap();
   143	                match scope {
   144	                    None => {
   145	                        wb.set_name(name, t).unwrap();
   146	                    }
   147	                    Some(sheet) => {
   148	                        // W5-92 (Phase 4.6.D): sheet-scoped name; producer
   149	                        // mirrors replay by routing through Sheet::set_scoped_name.
   150	                        wb.sheet_mut(*sheet)
   151	                            .expect("producer-side sheet lookup must succeed")
   152	                            .set_scoped_name(name, t)
   153	                            .expect("producer-side set_scoped_name must succeed");
   154	                    }
   155	                }
   156	            }
   157	            Op::AddSheet { name, chunk_rows } => {
   158	                wb.add_sheet_with_chunk_rows(name.clone(), *chunk_rows);
   159	            }
   160	            Op::RenameSheet {
   161	                id,
   162	                old_name: _,
   163	                new_name,
   164	            } => {
   165	                // W5-91: producer side calls `Workbook::rename_sheet`
   166	                // directly here (the formula-text rewrite happens at the
   167	                // `WorkbookRuntime` layer, above the op-log boundary).
   168	                wb.rename_sheet(*id, new_name.clone())
   169	                    .expect("producer-side rename_sheet must succeed");
   170	            }
   171	            Op::RegisterFormat { id, string } => {
   172	                wb.formats_mut()
   173	                    .register_at(ql_storage::FormatId(*id), string.as_str())
   174	                    .expect("producer-side register_at must succeed");
   175	            }
   176	            Op::SetCellFormat {
   177	                sheet,
   178	                row,
   179	                col,
   180	                id,
   181	            } => {
   182	                let s = wb.sheet_mut(*sheet).expect("sheet exists");
   183	                match id {
   184	                    Some(raw_id) => {
   185	                        s.format_overlay_mut()
   186	                            .set(*row, *col, ql_storage::FormatId(*raw_id));
   187	                    }
   188	                    None => {
   189	                        s.format_overlay_mut().clear(*row, *col);
   190	                    }
   191	                }
   192	            }
   193	            Op::BatchCommit { ops } => {
   194	                apply_producer_side(ops, wb);
   195	            }
   196	        }
   197	    }
   198	}

   200	/// Observational equality: compare two workbooks by cell-by-cell read,
   201	/// formula text, sheet count, sheet names, and NameTable contents.
   202	fn assert_workbooks_observationally_equal(a: &Workbook, b: &Workbook) {
   203	    assert_eq!(a.sheet_count(), b.sheet_count(), "sheet counts differ");
   204	    for sheet_id in 0..(a.sheet_count() as u16) {

   207	        assert_eq!(sa.name(), sb.name(), "sheet {sheet_id} name differs");
   208	        // Compare bounds + every cell within them. Outside-bounds cells
   209	        // are uniformly Blank, so bound-equality + within-bounds-equality
   210	        // suffices.

   212	        let bounds_b = sb.bounds();

   214	        let col_extent = bounds_a.col_extent.max(bounds_b.col_extent);
   215	        for row in 0..row_extent {
   216	            for col in 0..col_extent {
   217	                let va = sa.read(row, col);
   218	                let vb = sb.read(row, col);
   219	                assert_eq!(
   220	                    va, vb,

   290	#[test]
   291	fn producer_and_replay_produce_observationally_equivalent_workbooks() {
   292	    let ops = realistic_op_sequence();
   293	
   294	    // Producer side: direct mutations.
   295	    let mut producer_wb = Workbook::new();
   296	    producer_wb.add_sheet("S"); // sheet 0 pre-exists (matches the test setup).
   297	    apply_producer_side(&ops, &mut producer_wb);
   298	
   299	    // Replay side: build an OpLog, replay against an empty workbook with
   300	    // the same pre-existing sheet 0.
   301	    let mut log = OpLog::new();
   302	    for op in &ops {
   303	        log.append(op.clone()).unwrap();
   304	    }
   305	    let mut replay_wb = Workbook::new();
   306	    replay_wb.add_sheet("S");
   307	    let reg = default_registry();
   308	    let count = replay_into(&log, &mut replay_wb, &reg).unwrap();
   309	    assert_eq!(count, ops.len());
   310	
   311	    // Observational equality.
   312	    assert_workbooks_observationally_equal(&producer_wb, &replay_wb);
   313	
   314	    // Sanity-spot-check some cells / names by hand.
   315	    assert_eq!(
   316	        replay_wb.read(Address::new(0, 2, 2)),
   317	        Value::Number(22.0),
   318	        "spot-check: (2,2) in the 5x5 paste block"
   319	    );
   320	    assert_eq!(
   321	        replay_wb.formula_at(0, 5, 0).map(|s| s.as_ref()),
   322	        Some("A1 + B2 + C3"),
   323	        "spot-check: formula at (5, 0) survived round-trip"
   324	    );
   325	    assert!(matches!(
   326	        replay_wb.names().lookup_ci("TaxRate"),
   327	        Some(NamedTarget::Constant(Value::Number(n))) if n == 0.21
   328	    ));
   329	    assert_eq!(replay_wb.sheet_count(), 2);
   330	    assert_eq!(
   331	        replay_wb.read(Address::new(1, 0, 0)),
   332	        Value::text("widget"),
   333	        "spot-check: text value on second sheet"
   334	    );
   335	}

   337	#[test]
   338	fn binary_export_then_replay_equivalent_to_in_memory_replay() {
   339	    // Verifies that going through the binary persistence layer doesn't
   340	    // change replay results.
   341	    let ops = realistic_op_sequence();
   342	
   343	    let mut log = OpLog::new();
   344	    for op in &ops {
   345	        log.append(op.clone()).unwrap();
   346	    }
   347	
   348	    // In-memory replay → workbook A.
   349	    let mut wb_in_memory = Workbook::new();
   350	    wb_in_memory.add_sheet("S");
   351	    let reg = default_registry();
   352	    replay_into(&log, &mut wb_in_memory, &reg).unwrap();
   353	
   354	    // Binary round-trip → restored log → replay → workbook B.
   355	    let bytes = log.export_bytes().unwrap();
   356	    let restored = OpLog::import_bytes(&bytes).unwrap();
   357	    let mut wb_via_disk = Workbook::new();
   358	    wb_via_disk.add_sheet("S");
   359	    replay_into(&restored, &mut wb_via_disk, &reg).unwrap();
   360	
   361	    assert_workbooks_observationally_equal(&wb_in_memory, &wb_via_disk);
   362	}

   364	#[test]
   365	fn batch_commit_replay_equivalent_to_flat_replay() {
   366	    // Two op logs: one with ops flat, the other wrapping the same ops in
   367	    // a single BatchCommit. Both should produce the same workbook state.
   368	    let flat_ops = vec![
   369	        Op::PutValue {
   370	            sheet: 0,
   371	            row: 0,
   372	            col: 0,
   373	            value: CellWireValue::Number(1.0),
   374	        },
   375	        Op::PutValue {
   376	            sheet: 0,
   377	            row: 0,
   378	            col: 1,
   379	            value: CellWireValue::Number(2.0),
   380	        },
   381	        Op::PutFormula {
   382	            sheet: 0,
   383	            row: 1,
   384	            col: 0,
   385	            text: "A1 + B1".to_owned(),
   386	        },
   387	    ];
   388	
   389	    let batched = vec![Op::BatchCommit {
   390	        ops: flat_ops.clone(),
   391	    }];
   392	
   393	    let reg = default_registry();
   394	
   395	    let mut wb_flat = Workbook::new();
   396	    wb_flat.add_sheet("S");
   397	    let mut log_flat = OpLog::new();
   398	    for op in &flat_ops {
   399	        log_flat.append(op.clone()).unwrap();
   400	    }
   401	    replay_into(&log_flat, &mut wb_flat, &reg).unwrap();
   402	
   403	    let mut wb_batched = Workbook::new();
   404	    wb_batched.add_sheet("S");
   405	    let mut log_batched = OpLog::new();
   406	    for op in &batched {
   407	        log_batched.append(op.clone()).unwrap();
   408	    }

   278	/// **W5-87 (Phase 4.6.A part 1):** replaces the old `hash_option_sheet`
   279	/// helper. Tags 0/1 preserve fingerprint stability for `Current`/`Id`
   280	/// (the historical `None`/`Some` cases); `Name` gets tag 2 and emits
   281	/// the string bytes.
   282	fn hash_sheet_ref(s: &ql_formula_syntax::SheetRef, h: &mut impl Hasher) {
   283	    use ql_formula_syntax::SheetRef;
   284	    match s {
   285	        SheetRef::Current => 0u8.hash(h),
   286	        SheetRef::Id(sheet) => {
   287	            1u8.hash(h);
   288	            sheet.hash(h);
   289	        }
   290	        SheetRef::Name(name) => {
   291	            2u8.hash(h);
   292	            name.as_bytes().hash(h);
   293	        }
   294	    }
   295	}

I read both required files completely and audited the scoped code paths. I could not write the requested output file because the workspace is mounted read-only here:

`zsh: operation not permitted: docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.6-closing-megaudit-codex.txt`

Key closure findings from the report I attempted to write:

- **A FAIL / HIGH:** `Workbook::add_sheet` and `add_sheet_with_chunk_rows` bypass `validate_sheet_name`, so `Sheet1` + `SHEET1` and reserved characters can enter storage. Citations: `crates/ql-storage/src/workbook.rs:463`, `crates/ql-storage/src/workbook.rs:485`; runtime/replay also bypass at `crates/ql-exec/src/workbook_runtime.rs:781` and `crates/ql-oplog/src/replay.rs:298`.
- **G FAIL / HIGH:** sheet-scoped name changes do not invalidate `PlanCache`; the key uses only workbook `names().generation()`. Citations: `crates/ql-exec/src/workbook_runtime.rs:495`, mutation at `crates/ql-exec/src/workbook_runtime.rs:734`.
- **E FAIL / MEDIUM:** printer panics on every `SheetRef::Id`, so the post-bind/same-sheet-id print contract is not implemented. Citation: `crates/ql-formula-syntax/src/printer.rs:82`.
- **J MIXED / MEDIUM:** rename rewrites formula cells but not `NamedTarget::Formula` in workbook or sheet-scoped names, despite design §3.1. Citation: `crates/ql-exec/src/workbook_runtime.rs:855`.
- **H/M MIXED / MEDIUM:** producer-replay equivalence still lacks `scope: Some(_)`, and its comparator ignores per-sheet scoped names. Citations: `crates/ql-oplog/tests/producer_replay_equivalence.rs:55`, `crates/ql-oplog/tests/producer_replay_equivalence.rs:236`.
- **L/N MIXED / MEDIUM:** docs are stale: Phase 4.6 gaps remain open in `docs/known-gaps.md:30` and `docs/known-gaps.md:31`; MASTER-PLAN still has pre-implementation Phase 4.6 text at `docs/MASTER-PLAN.md:465`.

Pass areas: B, D, F, I, K are materially aligned with the spec. C is mixed only because `A1!B2` is intentionally accepted by the lexer/tests, which conflicts with the audit prompt’s Excel-rejection question.

**What is needed for W5-93 closure:**

1. **HIGH:** validate sheet names on all add-sheet paths before logging/mutation, including replay.
2. **HIGH:** invalidate or key `PlanCache` on sheet-scoped name generation.
3. **MEDIUM:** implement or explicitly defer resolver-backed printing for `SheetRef::Id`.
4. **MEDIUM:** rewrite `NamedTarget::Formula` text during sheet rename, or file a named-formula gap and fix docs.
5. **MEDIUM:** extend producer-replay scoped-name coverage and comparator.
6. **MEDIUM:** update `known-gaps` and `MASTER-PLAN` for Phase 4.6 status.
7. **LOW:** decide and document whether unquoted A1-shaped sheet names are accepted.
